One rule holds for every row: a TLS 1.2 connection is always non-PQ, so tlsDetails.tlsVersion or the logged protocol is enough to flag it.

CloudTrail records keyExchange only for KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager, ACM and Payment Cryptography so far; AWS lists it as coming soon for S3, DynamoDB, SQS, SNS, EventBridge, ECR and Direct Connect. Systems Manager isn't on AWS's PQ readiness list at all. Details are on the AWS service endpoints page.

3.3 Zeek: network detection

Zeek is the only signal that sees east-west and egress traffic that no AWS log covers, whatever language or library made the connection. It reads the ServerHello, so it reports what was negotiated, not what was offered.

What we tested. In the e2e stack, Zeek 7.2.2 sniffed the observer's interface with our pq-tls.zeek loaded and JSON logs on, while an OpenSSL 3.5 client and an OpenSSL 3.0 client connected to the hybrid, TLS 1.2 and dual-certificate listeners. Every session was classified correctly, and Zeek raised one notice per classical server and group (e2e/zeek/check.sh, passed 2026-11-16). Zeek 8.0.10 gave the same result on a pcap.

Port Client version curve pq
8443 (hybrid) OpenSSL 3.5 TLSv13 X25519MLKEM768 true
8443 (hybrid) OpenSSL 3.0 TLSv13 x25519 false
8447 (TLS 1.2 only) both TLSv12 x25519 false
8448 (dual certificate) OpenSSL 3.5 TLSv13 X25519MLKEM768 true
8448 (dual certificate) OpenSSL 3.0 TLSv13 x25519 false

The main change is one script that adds a pq column and a notice (full file: pq-tls.zeek):

event ssl_established(c: connection) &priority=3 {
    local grp = c$ssl?$curve ? c$ssl$curve : "none";
    c$ssl$pq = grp in pq_groups;     # the three hybrid ML-KEM groups
    if ( ! c$ssl$pq )
        NOTICE([$note=PQTLS::Classical_Key_Exchange, $conn=c,
                $identifier=cat(c$id$resp_h, c$id$resp_p, grp), $suppress_for=1hr,
                $msg=fmt("non-PQ TLS key exchange: group=%s", grp)]);
}

Deploying it on AWS.

  • Where it can look. VPC Traffic Mirroring works only from Nitro EC2 network interfaces. It cannot mirror Fargate tasks, Lambda functions or AWS-managed endpoints. Mirror the ENIs of NAT instances, egress proxies and application hosts (including ECS and EKS nodes) to a sensor ENI, or to an NLB in front of a sensor fleet.
  • What to mirror. TCP 443 and other TLS ports only, to cut cost. Don't truncate packets: the hybrid ClientHello is about 1.2 KB larger and spans two segments.
  • Encapsulation. Mirrored packets arrive VXLAN-encapsulated on UDP 4789; Zeek decodes it by default.
  • Logs. Run with LogAscii::use_json=T and ship ssl.log and notice.log to S3. The Athena table zeek_ssl reads that format (athena-views.sql).
  • Offered groups. To find clients that never offer ML-KEM (as opposed to servers that refuse it), add the JA4 package (zkg install zeek/foxio/ja4).

What Zeek cannot see. In TLS 1.3 the certificate is encrypted, so Zeek cannot tell an ML-DSA certificate from an ECDSA one; certificate posture comes from the inventory and server logs. Inside a service mesh, Zeek sees the mesh's mTLS, not the application's own TLS.

3.4 Application and server handshake logs

Where no sensor can look (Lambda, Fargate), the application logs its own handshakes. Every e2e probe writes one JSON line per handshake, which a CloudWatch Logs metric filter counts:

{"event":"tls_handshake","lang":"python","runtime":"3.12.16","compute":"lambda","target":"api.partner.example",
 "ok":true,"tls_version":"TLSv1.3","group":"X25519","pq":false,"pq_status":"classical"}

{ ($.event = "tls_handshake") && ($.pq_status = "classical") }

The filter found exactly the 892 classical handshakes the clients reported in run r1117041043 (e2e check detect-cloudwatch-nonpq). The observer's own server-side log held all of them plus every TLS 1.2 negotiation (detect-server-handshake-log), which is the pattern for servers we run: log the negotiated group on the server. Not every runtime can read the negotiated group: Go 1.25+ exposes ConnectionState().CurveID and Python 3.15 SSLSocket.group(), while Java and .NET have no public API for it. So the e2e probes log the group the server reports (the observer echoes it), and for servers we run, the server log is the better source. Each language chapter's "How to test" has the check for that language.

3.5 Alerting

Alerts are configured in Enforcement and guardrails (runtime detection): a CloudTrail metric filter and alarm for classical AWS API calls, an hourly classical-share metric from ELB logs, EventBridge rules on non-PQ listener changes, and Zeek notices for destinations on a PQ-required list. Dashboards are in the next chapter.

3.6 What detection cannot see

Gap Why What covers it
Lambda and Fargate calls to non-AWS services No AWS log records the group; no packet mirroring Posture, application handshake logs, or an egress proxy that logs the group
AWS API calls to services without keyExchange in CloudTrail (S3, DynamoDB, SQS and others, "coming soon") Not logged yet Posture: the SDK and runtime version
Certificate algorithm in TLS 1.3 Certificate is encrypted on the wire Inventory of issued certificates (Private CA, ACM) and server configuration
AWS-managed legs: CloudFront to origin, API Gateway to integrations, ALB to targets AWS does not log the group on these legs Target-side server logs for ALB targets; the rest are recorded exceptions
IoT devices on AWS IoT Core IoT Core has no PQ policy; every leg is classical today Inventory of device firmware and TLS library

4 Inventory and dashboards

Detection produces signals; the inventory turns them into a list of everything we own that makes or ends a TLS connection, each with a status, an owner and a next action. Dashboards track that list to done. A component is done when its posture is PQ and the observed signals show no classical connections for seven days, or when it is a recorded exception.

4.1 What the inventory holds

Component Source What is read How it is classified In pq_inventory.py
Lambda functions lambda:ListFunctions Runtime, package type, OPENSSL_CONF, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, GODEBUG, PYTHONPATH, AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER, layers, role Language chapters, Lambda rows; AWS block-update date Yes (Tested)
ECS task definitions (Fargate and EC2) ecs:ListTaskDefinitions, DescribeTaskDefinition Image, environment, task role Base image (PQ by default or not), PQ variables Yes (Tested; image classification is by tag, confirm with a scan)
Container images (ECS, EKS, Lambda images) Amazon Inspector SBOM export or a scanner in CI OS OpenSSL, JDK, Node, Python's linked OpenSSL, Go build info (go version -m) Language chapters, container rows No: status scan points here
EKS workloads Kubernetes API (Deployment, Pod specs) Image, environment As for ECS No (add: same rules as ECS)
EC2 hosts SSM Inventory OS, OpenSSL version, crypto policy (update-crypto-policies --show) Hosts and OS No (add)
ALB and NLB listeners elbv2:DescribeListeners SslPolicy PQ policy names (-PQ-) Yes (Tested)
Classic Load Balancers elb:DescribeLoadBalancers Existence Always blocked: migrate Yes (Tested)
API Gateway apigateway:GetDomainNames, apigatewayv2:GetApis REST domain security policy; HTTP and WebSocket APIs REST: PQ policy or not; HTTP and WebSocket: blocked Yes (Tested)
CloudFront distributions cloudfront:ListDistributions Origins Viewer leg PQ; origin leg a recorded exception No (add)
Databases and data services rds:DescribeDBInstances, ElastiCache, MSK, OpenSearch Engine and version Databases and data services No (add)
Certificates ACM, AWS Private CA, the CLM product Key algorithm (RSA, ECDSA, ML-DSA), expiry, issuer Use cases No (add)
IoT devices IoT fleet indexing TLS library and version as a thing attribute IoT No (add)
External destinations Zeek ssl.log, egress proxy logs Server name, negotiated group Classical destinations become third-party exceptions or upgrade requests From observed signals

The script (pq_inventory.py) is read-only. It writes one JSON line per component, for one account and Region. Run it per account from a scheduled job, with a read-only role assumed from the organisation's security account.

4.2 Status values

Status Meaning Next action
pq PQ today with no change (Java 25, a Debian 13 image, a PQ listener policy) Confirm with observed signals
pq-fixed Our fix is applied (config file, agent, layer, GODEBUG) Confirm with observed signals; a pq-fixed component with classical traffic is a failed fix
bridge A partial fix: Python 3.9 to 3.11 shim (requests and boto3 only) or the pq-egress extension Plan the runtime upgrade
fixable One change makes it PQ Release the fix through the build template
upgrade Only a runtime or version upgrade helps (Node 18 and 20, Python 3.9 to 3.11 without the shim) Schedule before the block-update date
blocked AWS offers no PQ option (Classic LB, API Gateway HTTP and WebSocket) Migrate or front it, or record an exception
scan Metadata is not enough (container images, custom runtimes) Scan the image or binary

The main classification rule, for Python on Lambda:

if rt.startswith("python3."):
    if minor >= 12:
        # fixed = OPENSSL_CONF points at openssl-pq.cnf, or the pq-tls layer wraps the runtime
        return "pq-fixed" if pq_conf or wrapped else "fixable"
    if wrapped or "pyshim" in env.get("PYTHONPATH", ""):
        return "bridge"          # the shim covers requests and boto3 only
    return "upgrade"

Tested. e2e/inventory/test_inventory.py creates 25 components in LocalStack Pro: 17 Lambda functions (every runtime family, with and without each fix), 3 ECS task definitions, an ALB with a PQ and a classical listener, a Classic LB, an API Gateway REST domain and an HTTP API. It runs the script and checks every status: 25 of 25 passed on 2026-11-16. Not run against a real AWS account.

4.3 From signals to dashboards

 Posture                         Observed
 pq_inventory.py (daily)         CloudTrail       ALB / NLB logs     Zeek ssl.log      App tls_handshake logs
        |                             |                  |                 |                  |
        v                             v                  v                 v                  v
 s3://.../pq-inventory/          S3 (trail)       S3 (ELB logs)     S3 (Firehose)     CloudWatch -> S3 export
        \___________________________ Athena: pq_inventory, pq_observed, pq_workload ___________/
                                                    |
                         Dashboards (QuickSight or Managed Grafana) + CloudWatch alarms

The Athena views (athena-views.sql) put every observed source into one shape (source, ts, who, dest, tls_version, grp, pq) and join it to the latest inventory by IAM role, which CloudTrail records for every call. The views follow AWS's and Zeek's documented formats; the Zeek table matches the JSON our Zeek check produced. They have not been run on Athena (Unverified).

4.4 Dashboards

Dashboard Question it answers Source Done when
Programme How many components are PQ, and is the classical share of handshakes falling? pq_inventory_latest by status over time; pq_observed classical share per day and source Every component pq, pq-fixed or an exception; classical share flat at the exception level
By compute type Where is each Lambda, ECS, EKS and EC2 workload? pq_inventory_latest by compute and status No fixable or upgrade left
By language and runtime Which runtimes need an upgrade, and before which date? pq_inventory_latest by language, runtime and block_update No component on a runtime past or near its block-update date
Front doors and infrastructure Are all listeners and APIs on PQ policies? Which legs are blocked by AWS? Inventory listener and API rows; ALB and NLB tls_keyexchange Every listener PQ; blocked legs in the exception register
Network (Zeek) Which hosts still make classical TLS connections, and to where? zeek_ssl classical flows by client and server name; notice.log Only exception destinations remain
Fix verification Did each fix work? pq_workload: posture pq-fixed against observed classical AWS calls No pq-fixed workload with classical calls
Certificates Which certificates are RSA, ECDSA or ML-DSA, and when do long-lived roots expire? Certificate inventory ML-DSA roots in place for the use cases that need them
Exceptions What is classical on purpose, who owns it, and when is it reviewed? Exception register Every exception has an owner and a review date in the future

Alarms come from the same data: a CloudTrail metric filter for classical AWS API calls, the hourly classical share from ELB logs, and Zeek notices for destinations on the PQ-required list (Enforcement and guardrails).

4.5 Rollout of the inventory

  1. Week 1: run pq_inventory.py in every account and Region; load the output to S3; create the Athena tables.
  2. Week 2: turn on ALB connection logs and NLB access logs; add keyexchange to the CloudTrail table; deploy the CloudTrail alarm.
  3. Weeks 2 to 4: deploy Zeek sensors on the mirrored ENIs of NAT instances, egress proxies and the busiest EC2 hosts; ship JSON logs to S3.
  4. Week 4: build the Programme, By compute type and Fix verification dashboards first; the rest follow.
  5. Then: extend the script with the "No (add)" rows above, starting with container image scans (they replace the scan status).

5 Solutions by compute type

The language and its version decide whether a connection can be PQ. The compute type decides who owns the TLS library, where the fix lives, how it ships and how we see the result. This chapter has one section per compute type. Each gives the per-language fix in one line and links to the language chapter for the detail, cases and full files.

Compute type Who owns the OS and TLS library Where the fix lives How we see it e2e client cases (all pass)
AWS Lambda AWS (zip); you, from an AWS base image (container image) Files in the package under /var/task/pq-tls/ and function variables; or the pq-tls layer CloudTrail for AWS APIs; application logs 250
Amazon ECS on AWS Fargate You (the image) Base image and Dockerfile; task definition variables CloudTrail; application logs; an egress proxy 109, plus 27 load balancer target cases
Amazon ECS on EC2 and Amazon EKS You (the image); the node's OS does not affect the container's TLS As for Fargate; pod spec for EKS As for Fargate, plus Zeek on mirrored node ENIs 24, plus 5 load balancer target cases
Amazon EC2 hosts You (the AMI and OS packages) OS packages, crypto policy via SSM, the service's systemd unit CloudTrail; Zeek; SSM Inventory 54

e2e = the LocalStack end-to-end suite, run r1117041043 (2026-11-16): 437 client cases and 32 load balancer target cases, 471 of 471 passed including the two detection checks. "Pass" means the observed behaviour matched the documented expectation, which for a baseline case is "classical". Per-language counts are in Appendix A.

5.1 The shared kit

Every fix is one of a few files or variables, versioned once in the pq-tls kit and pulled in by the shared build template, so a workload picks it up on its next release.

File or variable Used by Purpose Full file
openssl-pq.cnf + OPENSSL_CONF Python, .NET (and distro Node) on Amazon Linux 2023 Keeps the AL2023 crypto policy, puts X25519MLKEM768 first configs/python/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf
openssl-pq-node.cnf + OPENSSL_CONF Node.js 22 and 24 on Lambda AWS's Node group list with X25519MLKEM768 added first configs/nodejs
pq-tls-agent.jar (kit v2) + Bouncy Castle 1.87 + JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS Java 8 to 21 Bouncy Castle JSSE as the TLS provider with ML-KEM; PKIX key manager for EC server certificates kit/java-v2
pyshim/sitecustomize.py + PYTHONPATH Python 3.9 to 3.11 on Lambda only Routes requests and boto3 through pyOpenSSL's OpenSSL 3.5 (bridge) configs/python/pq-tls/pyshim/sitecustomize.py
GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1 Go modules with a go line below 1.24 Turns ML-KEM on without a go.mod change Go
pq-tls layer, AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper Lambda python3.9+, nodejs22+, java8 to 21, dotnet8 and 10 Applies the right fix per runtime at start; PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 rolls back e2e/build/layers.sh
pq-egress Lambda extension Interim, any Lambda runtime with no in-process fix Makes the outbound TLS connection with PQ; the function calls it over loopback HTTP e2e/lambda/extension/pq-egress

Where a variable goes:

Component How to set NAME=value
Lambda (zip or image) Environment.Variables in CloudFormation or SAM; environment in CDK or Terraform aws_lambda_function
Lambda image, Fargate, EKS, ECS on EC2 ENV NAME=value in the Dockerfile, so every deployment of the image carries it
ECS task (override) containerDefinitions[].environment in the task definition
EKS pod (override) spec.containers[].env in the Deployment or pod spec
EC2 service Environment=NAME=value in the systemd unit, rolled out by SSM or the AMI pipeline

5.2 AWS Lambda (zip and container image)

What decides PQ. On a zip function AWS owns the OS and the language runtime, so the fix is a file, agent or shim shipped in the package plus a variable. Python, Node.js 22+ and .NET runtimes on Amazon Linux 2023 have OpenSSL 3.5 but negotiate classical X25519, because the AL2023 crypto policy (Python, .NET) or the Lambda bootstrap's own config file (Node.js) leaves ML-KEM out. A container image function built from an AWS base image (public.ecr.aws/lambda/...) behaves the same; function variables override image ENV.

Language and runtime PQ today Fix Detail
Python 3.12, 3.13, 3.14 No Ship openssl-pq.cnf, set OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf Python
Python 3.9, 3.10, 3.11 (Amazon Linux 2, OpenSSL 1.1.1) No Upgrade to 3.12+. Bridge: the shim (requests and boto3 only) or the pq-tls layer Python
Python libraries with bundled TLS (grpcio, awscrt, psycopg binary, pyOpenSSL) Yes, on every runtime Nothing beyond a current wheel Python
Node.js 22.x, 24.x No (the bootstrap config removes ML-KEM) Ship openssl-pq-node.cnf, set OPENSSL_CONF to it Node.js
Node.js 18.x, 20.x No, and no setting helps (OpenSSL 3.1 and 3.2) Upgrade; interim, the pq-egress extension Node.js
Java 8, 11, 17, 21 No Kit v2 agent: JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=-javaagent:/var/task/pq-tls/pq-tls-agent.jar (the layer adds the Java 8 flags) Java
Java 25 Yes (25.0.5+) Nothing Java
.NET 8, 10 No Ship openssl-pq.cnf, set OPENSSL_CONF .NET
Go on provided.al2023 or provided.al2 Yes with Go 1.24+ and a go line of 1.24+ Rebuild with Go 1.26 and raise the go line; or GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1 Go

Three ways to ship it, in order of preference:

  1. Bundle in the package through the shared build template: files under /var/task/pq-tls/, variables in the function's IaC. Avoids Lambda's five-layer limit and keeps the fix versioned with the function.
  2. The pq-tls layer with AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper: one layer for every runtime; it leaves a function's own settings alone and PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 rolls it back (Tested in e2e on every supported runtime).
  3. The pq-egress extension, interim, for runtimes with no in-process fix (Node.js 18 and 20) or functions that cannot be rebuilt soon. The function sends plain HTTP to http://127.0.0.1:2779/<route>/<path> and the extension makes the TLS connection with X25519MLKEM768. Tested in e2e on every Lambda runtime (28 cases); not tested for AWS SDK calls (SigV4 signs the host name) or on real AWS.

The main change, a SAM function with the bundled config (full templates per language: configs/python/template.yaml, configs/go/template.yaml):

Resources:
  Fn:
    Type: AWS::Serverless::Function
    Properties:
      Runtime: python3.13
      Environment:
        Variables:
          OPENSSL_CONF: /var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf

Detection. CloudTrail keyExchange for calls to KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager and ACM; the function's own handshake log for everything else. Lambda cannot be packet-mirrored.

Deadline. AWS blocks updates to nodejs18.x, nodejs20.x, python3.9 to 3.11, java8.al2, java11, java17, provided.al2 and dotnet8 from 2027-08-31 (function creation from 2027-07-29). After that no file, layer or variable can be added, so these functions move first.

5.3 Amazon ECS on AWS Fargate

What decides PQ. You own the image, so the fix is usually the base image: its OpenSSL, JDK or Node version. The host is AWS's and does not matter. Choose a base image that is PQ by default, and you need no variable at all.

Language PQ by default in Not PQ by default in Fix Detail
Python Debian 13 (-trixie), Alpine 3.24 Debian 12 (-bookworm), Ubuntu 24.04, Azure Linux 3.0, AL2023 Move to a trixie or Alpine image; on AL2023 update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ in the Dockerfile or openssl-pq.cnf Python
Node.js Official node images 22.20+ and 24.5+ (bundled OpenSSL 3.5) Node 18 and 20; AL2023 dnf install nodejs22 Official image 24.9+; distro Node: system policy or config Node.js
Java Corretto or Temurin 25.0.5+ JDK 8 to 21 JDK 25.0.5+ image, or the kit v2 agent, or a static java.security Bouncy Castle entry (17, 21) Java
.NET .NET 10 on Alpine or Ubuntu 26.04 (resolute) .NET 8 default images (Debian 12), AL2023 Alpine or resolute image; on AL2023 the config or DEFAULT:PQ .NET
Go Any image, with Go 1.24+ and a go line of 1.24+ Older toolchain or go line Rebuild; base image does not matter Go

The main change is usually one line of the Dockerfile:

FROM python:3.13-slim-trixie        # was python:3.13-slim-bookworm: OpenSSL 3.5, PQ by default

As a load balancer target. When an ALB or NLB with a PQ listener policy forwards to an HTTPS target, the load balancer's leg is PQ only if the target's TLS server offers ML-KEM. The same fixes apply to servers: 27 Fargate target cases (Tested) show a fixed server negotiates PQ with the ALB's PQ backend policy and classical TLS 1.2 with the classic one. Java servers with EC certificates under Bouncy Castle also need ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX (kit v2 sets it). See Load balancers.

Detection. CloudTrail for AWS API calls; application handshake logs; an egress proxy that logs the group for third-party traffic. VPC Traffic Mirroring does not support Fargate.

Ship and roll back. Base image bump or Dockerfile ENV through the normal pipeline; roll back by reverting the image tag. Task definition variables override image ENV for a fast rollback without a rebuild.

5.4 Amazon ECS on EC2 and Amazon EKS

What decides PQ. The same as Fargate: the container image decides, and the node's OS and crypto policy do not reach inside the container. Everything in the Fargate table applies unchanged; EKS sets variables in the pod spec instead of the task definition.

What is different on EC2-backed clusters:

  • Detection is better. Node ENIs are Nitro EC2 interfaces, so VPC Traffic Mirroring to a Zeek sensor sees every pod's and task's east-west and egress TLS, whatever the language (Detection).
  • Host agents follow the node OS. The ECS agent, kubelet, SSM agent and node-level tools use the AMI's OpenSSL and crypto policy; treat them as EC2 hosts.
  • A service mesh can carry PQ for the app. On EKS, Linkerd 2.19+ is PQ by default and Istio 1.27+ can be set to PQ, covering mesh-owned mTLS legs whatever the app's language. ECS Service Connect has no group setting. See Service mesh.
  • Admission control. Kyverno or Gatekeeper policies check images and pod variables at admission (Enforcement and guardrails).

The main change for an EKS Deployment that keeps an AL2023 image:

spec:
  containers:
    - name: app
      image: <account>.dkr.ecr.<region>.amazonaws.com/payments:1.4.2
      env:
        - name: OPENSSL_CONF
          value: /etc/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf     # baked into the image

Full files: configs/go/deployment.yaml, configs/go/taskdef.json and the matching files in each language folder under configs/.

5.5 Amazon EC2 hosts

What decides PQ. You own the OS. Programs that link the system OpenSSL (Python, .NET, distro Node.js, curl, nginx) follow the OS default and crypto policy; Java, Go and official Node.js binaries bring their own TLS and follow their language rules.

OS PQ by default for OpenSSL programs? Fix
Amazon Linux 2023 No (crypto policy omits ML-KEM) update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ via SSM State Manager, in waves by tag; or per service, OPENSSL_CONF in a systemd drop-in
Amazon Linux 2 No, and cannot be (OpenSSL 1.0.2 and 1.1.1) Migrate to AL2023
Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04 Yes, for OpenSSL programs. GnuTLS programs (wget, Debian git) stay classical Use OpenSSL-linked tools where the traffic matters
Windows Server Opt-in (Schannel x25519_mlkem768, TLS 1.3 only) Schannel group order via GPO or SSM
Language on the host Fix Detail
Python, .NET, distro Node.js The OS fix above Python, .NET, Node.js
Node.js official binaries Node 22.20+ or 24.5+; nothing else Node.js
Java JDK 25.0.5+, or the kit v2 agent in the unit's JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS Java
Go Rebuild with Go 1.24+ and a go line of 1.24+ Go

The main change, host-wide on AL2023 (full SSM document: configs/hosts-and-os):

sudo update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ    # every OpenSSL program on the host; reboot or restart services

Host-wide DEFAULT:PQ changes every OpenSSL program at once, so roll it out in waves by tag with SSM State Manager and keep the per-service OPENSSL_CONF drop-in for hosts that cannot take the whole policy. See Hosts and OS.

Detection. CloudTrail; Zeek on mirrored ENIs; SSM Inventory for OS, OpenSSL version and crypto policy.

6 Solutions by language

Each language chapter that follows is written for the application team that owns the code. It covers every version line, every compute type and every case a team meets (simple clients and servers, existing certificates and keystores, custom CA trust, mutual TLS, explicit TLS settings, FIPS mode, libraries with their own TLS, pinning and HSM keys). The compute-type view of the same fixes is in Solutions by compute type.

6.1 Languages at a glance

Language What decides PQ Status today Main action
Go Go's own crypto/tls: toolchain, go line in go.mod, GODEBUG, CurvePreferences, FIPS mode PQ by default with Go 1.24+ and a go line of 1.24+; FIPS needs 1.25+ (1.26+ for the SecP hybrids) Build with Go 1.26, raise the go line, drop explicit CurvePreferences; fix the AWS SDK's FIPS curve list
Python stdlib ssl follows the platform OpenSSL; some wheels bundle their own TLS Debian 13 and Alpine images PQ; Lambda and AL2023 need the config; grpcio, awscrt, psycopg binary, pyOpenSSL PQ everywhere Ship openssl-pq.cnf on Lambda/AL2023 or use an OpenSSL 3.5 image; shim or upgrade 3.9 to 3.11 on Lambda
Node.js Node's bundled OpenSSL plus the config file it reads Official 22.20+ and 24.5+ PQ by default (policy floor 24.9); Lambda bootstrap config removes it; 18 and 20 can't Override the Lambda config with openssl-pq-node.cnf; upgrade 18 and 20
Java The JSSE provider in the JVM JDK 25.0.5+ PQ by default (JEP 527 backport); 8 to 21 have no ML-KEM Move to JDK 25.0.5+, or add Bouncy Castle JSSE (kit v2 agent or java.security); servers with EC certificates also need ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX; tcnative or CRT clients are PQ on any JDK
.NET The OS stack: OpenSSL on Linux, Schannel on Windows; no .NET group API Alpine and Ubuntu 26.04 images PQ; Lambda and AL2023 need the config; Windows opt-in Pick a PQ base image or ship openssl-pq.cnf; set the Schannel group order on Windows

6.2 How each language chapter is organised

  1. Problem statement: what goes wrong today in this language, and who controls the handshake.
  2. Versions and native support: one row per version line, with PQ defaults per compute type, FIPS behaviour, the fix and an evidence label.
  3. Cases: what to change: A simple client, B simple server, C existing server certificates, D custom CA trust, E mutual TLS, F explicit TLS settings that block PQ, G FIPS mode, H libraries with their own TLS, I pinning and HSM or KMS keys, J other cases. Each says who it applies to (with a grep), what happens today, what to change (often nothing) and side effects.
  4. Solution and automation: inventory, the change per compute type, CI guardrails, canary rollout and rollback.
  5. How to test: posture, a PQ-only and a classical-only test server, a test per case, the in-cloud signal, and the rollback test.
  6. Pitfalls and rollback.
  7. Configuration files: links to every complete file in the repository (configs/<language>/). The paper keeps only the main change; the files are the source of truth for copying.

6.3 Existing certificates, keystores and trust stores

Most application teams already load their own server certificates, client certificates and private CAs. PQ key exchange does not change certificates, so in almost every case nothing in the keystore or CA bundle has to change. Each language chapter has a full case list (cases C, D and E), tested with the PQ fix in place. In summary:

Language Own server cert or keystore Own CA trust Mutual TLS What can still break
Go No change; tls.LoadX509KeyPair, GetCertificate and SNI work as before (Tested) No change; SSL_CERT_FILE replaces the default bundle rather than adding to it (Tested) No change (Tested) AWS_CA_BUNDLE with a plain *http.Client fails at config load: set RootCAs on the SDK's BuildableClient. x/crypto/pkcs12 can't read OpenSSL 3 PKCS#12 files
Python No change; load_cert_chain with RSA, EC or encrypted keys works (Tested) No change for cafile, certifi and REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE (Tested) No change (Tested) truststore.inject_into_ssl() disables the Lambda 3.9–3.11 shim for requests; gunicorn with an encrypted keyfile fails every worker (use an ssl_context hook); set_ecdh_curve or a TLS 1.2 maximum blocks PQ
Node.js No change for key/cert or modern PFX (Tested) No change for ca and NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS (Tested) No change (Tested) Legacy RC2/3DES PFX files fail on every current runtime: re-export them. The Lambda image has an empty system CA store: set SSL_CERT_FILE
Java No change to JKS or PKCS#12 stores (Tested). With the Bouncy Castle agent, EC server certificates need the PKIX key manager (below) No change for cacerts or javax.net.ssl.trustStore (Tested) Works with kit v2; kit v1 silently sent no EC client cert (Tested) Code that hard-codes KeyManagerFactory.getInstance("SunX509") or Tomcat keyManagerAlgorithm="SunX509". RSA+EC stores serve EC to default clients
.NET No change for PFX, PEM or X509Store (Tested on Linux) No change for custom validation callbacks and CustomTrustStore (Tested) No change (Tested) X509Certificate2.CreateFromPemFile(caPath) throws on a CA-only file: use CreateFromPem(File.ReadAllText(path))

Java with the Bouncy Castle agent and an EC server certificate. Every handshake used to fail. The cause is the key manager, not the certificate: Bouncy Castle asks for key type EC/secp256r1, and the JDK default SunX509 key manager returns no alias for it. The fix is one security property, ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX, in a file passed with -Djava.security.properties=<file> or added to java.security. Kit v2's agent sets it and ships pq-tls.security. With it, Tomcat 11.0.29, Jetty 12.1.15 and HttpsServer on JDK 17 and 21 negotiate X25519MLKEM768 with an EC certificate, and an X25519-only client still connects (Tested). Spring Boot 3.5.16 SSL bundles use the default algorithm and work. An app's own -Djava.security.properties overrides the kit file, so add the line there too. On JDK 25 with the default SunX509, the server ignored SNI on a two-certificate store; PKIX fixes that as well (Tested).

6.4 Other languages

  • Rust: rustls with the aws-lc-rs provider supports X25519MLKEM768; sources disagree on which 0.23.x release made it the default (0.23.22 or 0.23.27). The AWS SDK for Rust defaults to PQ for crates published after 2025-08-29 (Documented). Apps on the openssl crate follow the system OpenSSL. Not tested here.
  • Ruby, PHP: follow the system OpenSSL 3.5+, so they should behave like Python with the same config file (Documented in the AWS SDK guide; Lambda Unverified).

6.5 Go

Problem statement

Go's own crypto/tls, compiled into the binary, controls every handshake. OpenSSL, the OS crypto policy, the base image and the Lambda runtime play no part (Tested: an AL2023 host or image with update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ, and Amazon Linux 2 with OpenSSL 1.0.2, gave the same results as scratch). Five inputs decide the result:

Input Classical when Evidence
Toolchain Go 1.23 or older (no ML-KEM; 1.23 offers only the Kyber draft) Tested
go line in the main module's go.mod (sets DefaultGODEBUG) Below 1.24 (tlsmlkem=0), whatever the toolchain Tested
GODEBUG at run time tlsmlkem=0 Tested
tls.Config in your code or a library CurvePreferences without a hybrid, or MaxVersion: tls.VersionTLS12 Tested
FIPS 140-3 mode Go 1.24 FIPS, or boringcrypto with crypto/tls/fipsonly; Go 1.25 fips140=only fails every handshake Tested

The handshake still succeeds over X25519, so nothing breaks and nothing is logged: the gap is silent (Tested). The risk is harvest-now-decrypt-later; hybrid X25519MLKEM768 removes it for new sessions. Certificates are a separate track (see Certificates (ML-DSA)). Not tested: the Microsoft build of Go and Red Hat's Go toolset, which can route crypto to OpenSSL in FIPS mode.

Quick start by compute type

The fix is the same everywhere: build with Go 1.26+ and go 1.26 in go.mod. Where the go line can't move yet, set GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1,tlssecpmlkem=1 in the deployment (needs a Go 1.24+ binary; Go ignores unknown GODEBUG names at run time, Documented). The compute type only changes where you check, where the variable goes and how you roll back.

What to check (every compute type): go version -m <binary> shows go1.24 or later and no tlsmlkem=0; the deployment sets no GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0; go-pq-lint.sh finds no blocking tls.Config (configs/go/go-pq-posture.sh, configs/go/go-pq-lint.sh).

Compute Where to check The one change How to verify How to roll back Evidence
Lambda zip (provided.al2023, provided.al2) bootstrap from the deployed zip (aws lambda get-function gives the code URL); function Environment Rebuild bootstrap with Go 1.26+ and go 1.26. Interim: GODEBUG in Environment.Variables CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for the function role's KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager and ACM calls; the function's own CurveID log GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0,tlssecpmlkem=0 in the function environment, or shift the alias back to the previous version Tested (e2e, 75 zip cases)
Lambda container image Builder stage golang: tag; binary copied out of the image Builder golang:1.26, go 1.26; final stage public.ecr.aws/lambda/provided:al2023 As for zip Function environment overrides image ENV; or the previous image digest Tested (e2e, 2 image cases)
ECS on Fargate Binary from the image; task definition environment Rebuild the image. Any base works (scratch, distroless, golang, AL2023, AL2). Interim: GODEBUG in the task definition CloudTrail; application CurveID log; for a load balancer target, the ALB/NLB target leg New task definition revision with GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0,tlssecpmlkem=0, or the previous image tag Tested (e2e, 22 client + 4 target cases)
ECS on EC2, EKS As for Fargate; pod env on EKS As for Fargate; the node OS doesn't matter As for Fargate, plus Zeek ssl.log on mirrored node ENIs As for Fargate; on EKS a pod env change rolls the Deployment Tested on ECS EC2 (5 client + 1 target cases); EKS not run (Unverified, same image)
EC2 Binary on the host (go-pq-posture.sh through SSM Run Command); systemd unit Redeploy a binary built with Go 1.26+. Interim: Environment=GODEBUG=... drop-in CloudTrail; Zeek; application log Drop-in with GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0,tlssecpmlkem=0 and restart, or the previous binary Tested (e2e, 14 cases on AL2023 and AL2 AMIs)

Lambda: shared kit pieces. The pq-tls layer (AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER) does nothing for Go: it has no provided.* branch and Go ignores OPENSSL_CONF (Tested: Go 1.23 stayed classical, Go 1.27 stayed PQ, 4 cases). A Go 1.23 function that can't be rebuilt soon can send its calls through the pq-egress extension, which makes the TLS connection with X25519MLKEM768 (Tested, 3 cases; not tested for AWS SDK calls; see Solutions by compute type). The go1.x managed runtime is retired; use provided.al2023 (AWS). provided.al2 works, but AWS blocks updates to it from 2027-08-31.

Versions and native support

Version PQ client and server by default API to set groups FIPS-mode behaviour Proposed solution Evidence
1.27.x (supported; 1.27.4 tested) Yes when go ≥ 1.24: X25519MLKEM768; plus SecP256r1MLKEM768 and SecP384r1MLKEM1024 when go ≥ 1.26 CurvePreferences (an explicit list now beats tlsmlkem=0); GODEBUG tlsmlkem, tlssecpmlkem; opt-in MLKEM1024 fips140=on or only: the three hybrids + P-256, P-384, P-521 Keep; set go 1.26+ Tested
1.26.x (supported; 1.26.11 tested) Same rule as 1.27 CurvePreferences; GODEBUG (tlsmlkem=0 beats an explicit list) Same as 1.27 Target. Build with 1.26+, go 1.26 Tested
1.25.x (no security fixes since 1.27.0, 2026-08-19) Yes if go ≥ 1.24, X25519MLKEM768 only Same as 1.26 on: X25519MLKEM768 + P-256, P-384, P-521; only: every handshake fails Upgrade to 1.26 Tested
1.24.x (unsupported) Yes if go ≥ 1.24; no with go 1.23 or lower (tlsmlkem=0), as client and as server Same on or only: P-256, P-384, P-521, no PQ Upgrade; interim GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1 Tested
1.23 and older (unsupported) No ML-KEM. 1.23 offers the pre-standard X25519Kyber768Draft00 (0x6399), which PQ-only servers reject tlskyber only No PQ Rebuild with 1.26 Tested (1.23.12)

Support: "each major Go release is supported until there are two newer major releases", so 1.27 and 1.26 today (release history; Documented).

Decision guide

go version -m or the code shows Do this
go1.23 or older Rebuild with Go 1.26. Nothing else helps (or pq-egress on Lambda, interim)
tlsmlkem=0 in DefaultGODEBUG Raise the go line to 1.26; interim godebug tlsmlkem=1 in go.mod or GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1
CurvePreferences without a hybrid, or MaxVersion: tls.VersionTLS12 Fix the setting (case F)
GOFIPS140=, fips140=on or GOEXPERIMENT=boringcrypto Go 1.26+ native FIPS, not boringcrypto; AWS SDK fix (cases G, H)
None of these Already PQ. Verify (How to test) and move on

Detail: toolchain and go line

Client with default tls.Config against one server per group (Tested: e2e on Lambda, ECS and EC2, and golang images pinned by digest, linux/arm64).

Toolchain go line X25519MLKEM768 SecP256r1MLKEM768, SecP384r1MLKEM1024 Classical server still works
1.27.4 1.27 Yes Yes Yes
1.27.4 1.23 No (tlsmlkem=0) No Yes
1.26.11 1.26 Yes Yes Yes
1.26.11 1.25 Yes No (tlssecpmlkem=0) Yes
1.26.11 1.23 No No Yes
1.25.14 1.25 Yes No (added in 1.26) Yes
1.25.14 1.23 No No Yes
1.24.13 1.24 Yes No Yes
1.24.13 1.23 No No Yes
1.23.12 any No (no ML-KEM in 1.23) No Yes

All rows Tested. The rules behind the table:

  1. Toolchain sets what exists: 1.24 added X25519MLKEM768, 1.26 added the two SecP hybrids to the defaults, 1.27 added opt-in pure MLKEM1024, offered only when listed in CurvePreferences (Go 1.26, Go 1.27; Tested).
  2. The go line of the main module (or go.work) sets the defaults: below 1.24 gives tlsmlkem=0, below 1.26 gives tlssecpmlkem=0. A dependency's go.mod doesn't count (Documented; Tested).
  3. Override at build time without raising the go line: godebug tlsmlkem=1 in go.mod (Go 1.23+ toolchains) or //go:debug tlsmlkem=1 above package main. Both put tlsmlkem=1 into DefaultGODEBUG (Tested 1.24 to 1.27). Add tlssecpmlkem=1 on 1.26+ only: a 1.24 or 1.25 build fails with unknown godebug "tlssecpmlkem" (Tested, e2e build log; Documented).
  4. Run-time GODEBUG beats both: tlsmlkem=1 turns PQ on with no rebuild, tlsmlkem=0 turns it off (Tested). Unknown names are ignored at run time, so one value tlsmlkem=1,tlssecpmlkem=1 is safe on 1.24+ (Documented; tested on 1.26 only).
  5. Explicit CurvePreferences vs tlsmlkem=0. On 1.24 to 1.26, tlsmlkem=0 drops X25519MLKEM768 even from an explicit list. From 1.27 an explicit list wins (Documented; Tested both).

Raising the go line changes other defaults too. Going to 1.27 removes the tls10server, tlsrsakex, tls3des, tlsunsafeekm, x509keypairleaf and asynctimerchan settings (Go 1.27; Documented), so run the service tests. The GODEBUG route changes only the TLS groups.

Cases: what to change

Every case below was tested with Go 1.24.13 (go 1.24), 1.25.14 and 1.26.11 against OpenSSL 3.5.7 and Go test servers, unless a row says otherwise; Go 1.23.12 and go 1.22/1.23 builds were classical in every row, so the case was never the cause. Programs: tests/deep/go/v2/cases/main.go, tests/deep/go/v2/deps/main.go; outputs: tests/deep/go/v2/run-v2.out, run-v2b.out, run-v2c.out.

A. Simple client

Who this is. No TLS settings in code: grep -rnE --include='*.go' 'tls\.Config|TLSClientConfig|CurvePreferences|MaxVersion' . finds nothing. You use http.Get, the AWS SDK default client, or a driver with a DSN.

What happens today. PQ when the toolchain and the go line are both 1.24+; classical otherwise (Tested). What to change. Nothing in code: raise the go line and rebuild. Side effects. The ClientHello grows by about 1.2 KB (Pitfalls).

Rule for every library: pass a tls.Config without CurvePreferences, or none. Tested with Go 1.26.11 (go 1.26) against a Go server that accepts only X25519MLKEM768:

Client Version tested Where TLS is set PQ by default Evidence
crypto/tls Dialer, tls.Dial stdlib tls.Config Yes Tested
net/http default client, DefaultTransport.Clone() stdlib Transport.TLSClientConfig Yes Tested (also e2e)
gRPC-go v1.85.0 credentials.NewTLS(cfg) Yes; it doesn't set CurvePreferences Tested
AWS SDK for Go v2 v1.48.0 awshttp.NewBuildableClient() Yes, outside FIPS mode Tested (also e2e)
AWS SDK for Go v2, FIPS mode v1.48.0 same No: the SDK forces P-256/P-384/P-521 Tested (also e2e); fix in case H
pgx (and database/sql via pgx/v5/stdlib) v5.13.0 sslmode=require/verify-full, ConnConfig.TLSConfig Yes Tested with sslnegotiation=direct
go-redis v9.23.0 Options.TLSConfig Yes Tested
segmentio/kafka-go v0.4.51 Dialer.TLS, Transport.TLS Yes Tested
IBM/sarama v1.61.1 Config.Net.TLS.Config Yes Tested
lib/pq, mongo-go-driver, others — their tls.Config Probably, if they don't set CurvePreferences Unverified: grep them (case F)

Whether the far end accepts ML-KEM is a server question: see AWS service endpoints and Databases and data services. If a team insists on an explicit list, keep the hybrids in it; since Go 1.24 the order of CurvePreferences is ignored (Documented).

tr := http.DefaultTransport.(*http.Transport).Clone()
tr.TLSClientConfig = &tls.Config{MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS12} // no CurvePreferences
client := &http.Client{Transport: tr}

// Only if a list is mandatory (Go 1.26+ constants):
cfg := &tls.Config{CurvePreferences: []tls.CurveID{
    tls.X25519MLKEM768, tls.SecP256r1MLKEM768, tls.X25519, tls.CurveP256}}

B. Simple server

Who this is. http.ListenAndServeTLS, frameworks on net/http (chi, gin, echo), or a gRPC server with credentials.NewTLS, with no CurvePreferences.

What happens today. With 1.24+ and go ≥ 1.24, RSA and EC servers negotiate X25519MLKEM768 with a PQ client and X25519 with a classical one. The go line governs servers too: a 1.24 build with go 1.22 refused a PQ-only client until GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1 was set, with no rebuild (Tested). What to change. Nothing beyond the build. Side effects. None seen.

Server setup PQ client Classical client Evidence
net/http ListenAndServeTLS, default tls.Config X25519MLKEM768 and SecP256r1MLKEM768 negotiated Works (X25519, or P-256 on TLS 1.2) Tested, Go 1.26.11
CurvePreferences: {X25519, P256} (the pitfall) PQ-only client fails; hybrid-capable client silently gets X25519 Works Tested
PQ-only: MinVersion: TLS13, CurvePreferences: {X25519MLKEM768} Works Rejected; TLS 1.2 rejected Tested
gRPC server, credentials.NewTLS(default) X25519MLKEM768 with ALPN h2 Works Tested 
srv := &http.Server{Addr: ":8443", Handler: mux,
    TLSConfig: &tls.Config{MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS12}} // no CurvePreferences
log.Fatal(srv.ListenAndServeTLS("/etc/tls/tls.crt", "/etc/tls/tls.key"))

Behind an ALB or NLB TLS listener (e2e run r1117041043, the load balancer modelled as the client on the target leg): a net/http target built with Go 1.26 and go 1.26 negotiated X25519MLKEM768 under the PQ backend policy, on Fargate and ECS on EC2. Go 1.26 with go 1.23 stayed classical until GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1,tlssecpmlkem=1 was set in the task definition; Go 1.23 stayed classical (rebuild is the only fix). Under the classic backend policy every target negotiated classical TLS 1.2 (Tested, cases srv-go-*; see Load balancers).

C. Existing server certificates in your own files or keystore

Who this is. grep -rnE --include='*.go' 'LoadX509KeyPair|X509KeyPair|GetCertificate|pkcs12\.|DecryptPEMBlock|pkcs8\.' .

What happens today. The certificate store has no effect on key exchange. Every setup below negotiated X25519MLKEM768 with an OpenSSL PQ-only client and X25519 with a classical client (Tested).

Setup Code Which certificate is served Store change
RSA 2048 or EC P-256 PEM pair tls.LoadX509KeyPair(crt, key) RSA or EC None
RSA and EC together Certificates: {rsa, ec} The first the client supports: RSA to a default client, EC to an ECDSA-only client None; order picks the cert, not the group
EC and RSA together Certificates: {ec, rsa} EC to a default client, RSA to an RSA-only client None
Two hostnames Certificates: {a.test, b.test} Matched by SNI; unknown SNI gets the first None
Callback GetCertificate by ClientHelloInfo.ServerName Per SNI, with your fallback None
Legacy encrypted PEM (Proc-Type: 4,ENCRYPTED) x509.DecryptPEMBlock then tls.X509KeyPair RSA None
Encrypted PKCS#8 (BEGIN ENCRYPTED PRIVATE KEY) github.com/youmark/pkcs8 ParsePKCS8PrivateKey EC None
PKCS#12, OpenSSL 3 default (AES-256-CBC, PBKDF2, SHA-256 MAC) software.sslmate.com/src/go-pkcs12 v0.7.4 DecodeChain EC None
PKCS#12, legacy 3DES/SHA-1 golang.org/x/crypto/pkcs12 v0.59.0 Decode RSA None
Same x/crypto package on the OpenSSL 3 default file Decode Fails at load: pkcs12: unknown digest algorithm: 2.16.840.1.101.3.4.2.1 Switch to go-pkcs12 (also e2e)
Cert and key PEM in env vars (ECS secrets, Lambda env) tls.X509KeyPair([]byte(os.Getenv(...))) RSA None (also e2e, client side)

What to change. Nothing in the store: same files, formats, passwords, key types and chains. The tls.Config your loader fills must not set CurvePreferences without a hybrid, nor MaxVersion: tls.VersionTLS12. The one code change any team may need is the PKCS#12 import:

// Was golang.org/x/crypto/pkcs12 (frozen; can't read OpenSSL 3 default files)
import pkcs12 "software.sslmate.com/src/go-pkcs12"

key, leaf, chain, err := pkcs12.DecodeChain(p12Bytes, os.Getenv("TLS_KEY_PASS"))
c := tls.Certificate{PrivateKey: key, Leaf: leaf, Certificate: [][]byte{leaf.Raw}}
for _, ca := range chain { c.Certificate = append(c.Certificate, ca.Raw) }
cfg := &tls.Config{Certificates: []tls.Certificate{c}} // no CurvePreferences

The alternative, re-exporting with openssl pkcs12 -export -keypbe PBE-SHA1-3DES -certpbe PBE-SHA1-3DES -macalg sha1, keeps 3DES and SHA-1, so prefer the import change. x/crypto/pkcs12 is frozen and its docs point to go-pkcs12 (Documented).

Encrypted keys. The standard library can't read encrypted PKCS#8, and x509.DecryptPEMBlock is deprecated as insecure by design (Documented). In order of preference: keep the key unencrypted inside a secret store (Secrets Manager, Kubernetes Secret, ECS secrets) and load it with tls.X509KeyPair; decrypt at start-up with youmark/pkcs8, the passphrase from Secrets Manager; or move the key to KMS or an HSM (case I). All three loaders were tested above.

Side effects. None for PQ. If RSA is listed before EC, default clients get RSA; that was already true.

D. Custom CA trust

Who this is. grep -rnE 'SystemCertPool|AppendCertsFromPEM|RootCAs|SSL_CERT_FILE|SSL_CERT_DIR|AWS_CA_BUNDLE|WithCustomCABundle|sslrootcert' in code and deployment files.

What happens today. Trust and key exchange are independent (Tested; the first six rows also in e2e on Lambda and Fargate scratch):

Trust setup Result
SSL_CERT_FILE=<bundle> or SSL_CERT_DIR=<dir>, RootCAs nil PQ and fallback OK
Neither set, private CA not in the system store (or scratch with no bundle) x509: certificate signed by unknown authority (expected)
x509.SystemCertPool() + AppendCertsFromPEM PQ and fallback OK
RootCAs = new pool with only the private CA PQ and fallback OK
RootCAs = empty pool Unknown authority (expected)
AWS SDK AWS_CA_BUNDLE or config.WithCustomCABundle, default HTTP client PQ OK
AWS SDK CA bundle + a plain *http.Client Config error: unable to add custom RootCAs HTTPClient, has no WithTransportOptions, *http.Client
gRPC credentials.NewClientTLSFromFile(ca.pem, ...) PQ OK
pgx DSN sslrootcert= PQ OK

What to change. Nothing in the truststore. Two traps: SSL_CERT_FILE and SSL_CERT_DIR replace the default locations rather than adding to them, so the bundle must hold public roots too if the service calls public endpoints (Documented); and scratch or distroless static images have no bundle, so copy one in (COPY --from=build /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt /etc/ssl/certs/). The one code change is for the AWS SDK with your own client:

// Not: a plain *http.Client together with AWS_CA_BUNDLE / WithCustomCABundle (config error)
hc := awshttp.NewBuildableClient().WithTransportOptions(func(t *http.Transport) {
    t.TLSClientConfig = &tls.Config{RootCAs: pool} // no CurvePreferences
})
cfg, err := config.LoadDefaultConfig(ctx, config.WithHTTPClient(hc))

Side effects. None.

E. Mutual TLS (client certificates)

Who this is. grep -rnE --include='*.go' 'ClientAuth|ClientCAs|GetClientCertificate|Certificates:' .

What happens today. Client certificates don't change the group (Tested; client side also in e2e: GetClientCertificate, PEM from env vars, PKCS#12):

  • Go client with an RSA cert in Certificates or an EC cert from GetClientCertificate: X25519MLKEM768 to a PQ-only mTLS server, X25519 to a classical one.
  • Go server with ClientAuth: tls.RequireAndVerifyClientCert: RSA and EC clients over X25519MLKEM768, client_cn logged; classical clients over X25519.
  • No client cert: refused. In TLS 1.3 the client sees remote error: tls: certificate required on its first read; the server logs tls: client didn't provide a certificate.

What to change. Nothing. Side effects. None.

F. Explicit TLS settings that block PQ

Who this is. Run go-pq-lint.sh, or:

grep -rnE --include='*.go' 'CurvePreferences|MaxVersion|CipherSuites|tlsmlkem|tlskyber|fipsonly' . $(go env GOMODCACHE) | grep -v _test.go
grep -rnE 'GODEBUG|godebug' go.mod go.work Dockerfile* *.yaml *.json *.tf 2>/dev/null

In the tested dependency versions only the AWS SDK hit (its FIPS list, case H).

Setting Client to PQ-only server Client to classical As a server Change to Evidence
CurvePreferences: {X25519, P256} Fails: handshake failure X25519 PQ-only client fails; hybrid client silently gets X25519 Remove it, or add tls.X25519MLKEM768 (and tls.SecP256r1MLKEM768 on 1.26+) Tested (also e2e)
CurvePreferences: {X25519MLKEM768, X25519, P256} X25519MLKEM768 X25519 — Fine; but tlsmlkem=0 drops the hybrid from it on 1.24 to 1.26 Tested (also e2e)
MaxVersion: tls.VersionTLS12 Fails: protocol version not supported TLS 1.2, X25519 TLS 1.2 only, never PQ Remove it: the hybrids exist only in TLS 1.3 Tested (also e2e)
MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS13 X25519MLKEM768 X25519 (TLS 1.2-only servers refused) — Fine Tested (also e2e)
CipherSuites (a TLS 1.2 list) X25519MLKEM768 X25519 PQ on TLS 1.3 Keep: it doesn't touch TLS 1.3 Tested (also e2e)
GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0 at run time Fails X25519 — Remove it Tested (also e2e)
Go 1.23 with curve 4588 listed by number Fails even to classical: tls: CurvePreferences includes unsupported curve Fails — Upgrade the toolchain Tested (also e2e)

PQ-only enforcement (internal endpoints, last, after logs show no classical clients):

cfg := &tls.Config{MinVersion: tls.VersionTLS13,
    CurvePreferences: []tls.CurveID{tls.X25519MLKEM768, tls.SecP256r1MLKEM768}}

Side effects. Removing MaxVersion lets TLS 1.3 through; check peers that pin TLS 1.2 behaviour, such as inspection proxies.

G. FIPS mode

Who this is. go version -m shows GOFIPS140=, DefaultGODEBUG=fips140=on or GOEXPERIMENT=boringcrypto, or the environment sets GODEBUG=fips140=on|only.

What to change. Build with Go 1.26+ and GOFIPS140=v1.26.0 (or latest), not boringcrypto, and add the AWS SDK fix (case H). In FIPS mode crypto/tls silently drops non-approved groups (Documented). Groups the client offers (a server that prefers P-256 negotiates P-256):

Mode 1.24.13 1.25.14 1.26.11 1.27.4
fips140=on P-256, P-384, P-521: no PQ, no X25519 X25519MLKEM768, P-256, P-384, P-521 The three hybrids, P-256, P-384, P-521 Same as 1.26
fips140=only P-256, P-384, P-521 Every handshake fails: crypto/ecdh: use of X25519 is not allowed in FIPS 140-only mode Same as on Same as on
boringcrypto, no fipsonly Same as non-FIPS (PQ) Same Same Same
boringcrypto + import _ "crypto/tls/fipsonly" not run P-256, P-384, P-521, no PQ Same Same
boringcrypto + fips140=on Panics at start: FIPS 140-3 mode is incompatible with GOEXPERIMENT=boringcrypto Same Same Same

All cells Tested (e2e on Lambda, Fargate and EC2, and container builds). The TLS group policy follows the toolchain, not the module version: GOFIPS140=v1.0.0 built with Go 1.26 offered the SecP hybrids (Tested). Module v1.0.0 holds CMVP certificate #5247; v1.26.0 was "Pending Review" in the CMVP Modules In Process List as of 2026-04-28 (Go FIPS page; Documented). In FIPS mode a server that offers only X25519 can't be reached, whatever the PQ setting.

H. Libraries with their own TLS settings

Who this is. You pass a tls.Config to the AWS SDK, gRPC or a database driver, often to add a private CA or a client certificate.

What happens today. All three pass your tls.Config through unchanged, PQ included. Tested with Go 1.26.11, a config holding the private CA and an EC client cert, against a Go PQ-only mTLS server that logs the group and client CN, and OpenSSL PQ-only and classical mTLS servers:

Library How the config was passed Result Same config + CurvePreferences {X25519, P256} With GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0
aws-sdk-go-v2 v1.48.0, config v1.33.18 NewBuildableClient().WithTransportOptions(...) or a plain *http.Client via config.WithHTTPClient X25519MLKEM768, client_cn=cli-ec; classical fallback OK Fails to PQ-only Fails to PQ-only (also e2e)
grpc-go v1.85.0 credentials.NewTLS(cfg) or NewClientTLSFromFile X25519MLKEM768 Fails Fails
pgx v5.13.0 ConnConfig.TLSConfig, or DSN sslrootcert/sslcert/sslkey; sslnegotiation=direct X25519MLKEM768, client_cn=cli-ec Fails Fails

pgx with the default sslnegotiation=postgres (TLS after SSLRequest) uses the same tls.Config but was not run (Unverified). Outside Go's TLS: confluent-kafka-go uses librdkafka with its own OpenSSL, and anything you exec (curl, git, wget) uses the image's TLS library (not tested from Go; Unverified).

AWS SDK for Go v2 in FIPS mode is the one library default that blocks PQ: v1.48.0's aws/transport/http/client.go sets CurvePreferences to P-256, P-384 and P-521 when FIPS mode is on, so a PQ-only server fails (Tested, also e2e). Not sure which SDK release added this. Put the approved hybrids back (Tested, also e2e: PQ-only server then succeeded):

hc := awshttp.NewBuildableClient().WithTransportOptions(func(t *http.Transport) {
    if fips140.Enabled() {
        t.TLSClientConfig.CurvePreferences = []tls.CurveID{
            tls.SecP256r1MLKEM768, tls.X25519MLKEM768, tls.CurveP256, tls.CurveP384}
    }
})
cfg, _ := config.LoadDefaultConfig(ctx, config.WithHTTPClient(hc))

For gRPC, pgx, go-redis, kafka-go and sarama nothing changes: pass a tls.Config with ServerName or RootCAs and no curves, and avoid helpers that build a config with a fixed curve list.

I. Pinning, and HSM, PKCS#11 or KMS keys

Who this is. VerifyPeerCertificate or VerifyConnection with SPKI hashes; a tls.Certificate whose PrivateKey is a KMS, CloudHSM or PKCS#11 crypto.Signer.

What happens today.

  • SPKI pinning in VerifyConnection: the right pin passed over X25519MLKEM768 and X25519; a wrong pin failed with your error (Tested 1.24.13, 1.26.11; also e2e). Key exchange doesn't touch certificates, so pins stay valid until you move to ML-DSA certificates.
  • A server key behind an opaque crypto.Signer (only Public and Sign), RSA and EC: Sign was called once per handshake and X25519MLKEM768 was negotiated (Tested 1.24.13, 1.26.11). ML-KEM runs in software in crypto/tls; the HSM only signs. A real KMS, CloudHSM or PKCS#11 module was not tested (Unverified).

What to change. Nothing. Side effects. None.

J. Other cases

Case What decides Evidence
ALB HTTPS or NLB TLS listener The load balancer's security policy for the client-facing leg; your Go server only for the target leg (case B) Documented; target leg Tested
NLB TCP listener (passthrough) Your Go server: everything above applies Documented
API Gateway, CloudFront The front door; origin legs are classical Documented (see Edge and API front doors)
HTTPS_PROXY net/http tunnels TLS through CONNECT, so the Go client decides, unless the proxy intercepts TLS Unverified (see Network devices and egress)
HTTP/2 and gRPC servers ALPN h2 and X25519MLKEM768 work together Tested
WebSockets (gorilla/websocket, coder/websocket) They dial through net/http or a tls.Config; same rules Unverified
Service mesh sidecar If the app talks plaintext to the sidecar, the mesh decides See Service mesh
ML-DSA server or client certificates Go 1.23 to 1.27 don't negotiate ML-DSA: they get the ECDSA certificate from a dual-certificate server, fail an ML-DSA-only server (handshake failure) and can't load an ML-DSA client key (tls: failed to parse private key) Tested (e2e, every Go client case)

Solution and automation

  1. Inventory. go-pq-posture.sh over every built artifact (Lambda bootstrap, binaries copied out of images, EC2 binaries); go-pq-lint.sh over every repo; export GODEBUG from Lambda configurations, task definitions, pod specs and systemd units.
  2. Change. Raise the go line to 1.26 and rebuild with Go 1.26+. Remove blocking settings (case F). For FIPS, native GOFIPS140 and the AWS SDK fix (cases G, H). Where the go line can't move yet, GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=1,tlssecpmlkem=1.
  3. CI guardrails. Fail the build on go-pq-lint.sh findings before compiling, then run go-pq-posture.sh on the built binary (Tested on the e2e build: FAIL for every classical-by-build probe, WARN for boringcrypto). The lint is a file-level heuristic: a file that mentions ML-KEM anywhere passes its CurvePreferences check, so review hits by hand.

Packaging per compute type

Compute Build Optional GODEBUG goes in Full file
Lambda zip GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm64 CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -tags lambda.norpc -o bootstrap .; bootstrap at the zip root (AWS) Environment.Variables (SAM: BuildMethod: go1.x, Runtime: provided.al2023, Handler: bootstrap) configs/go/template.yaml, configs/go/lambda.tf
Lambda image golang:1.26 builder, public.ecr.aws/lambda/provided:al2023 final stage (AWS) Dockerfile ENV or the function environment configs/go/Dockerfile.lambda
Fargate, ECS on EC2, EKS golang:1.26 builder, distroless/static-debian12:nonroot or scratch (copy CA certificates) Task definition environment or pod env configs/go/Dockerfile, configs/go/taskdef.json, configs/go/deployment.yaml
EC2 Ship the binary (no OS dependency with CGO_ENABLED=0) Environment= in the systemd unit, via SSM or the AMI pipeline configs/go/etc
FIPS builds GOFIPS140=v1.26.0 (or latest); go version -m shows GOFIPS140= and DefaultGODEBUG=fips140=on — configs/go/Dockerfile.fips

The configs/go/Dockerfile, Dockerfile.fips and Dockerfile.lambda builds ran in e2e, and so did the GODEBUG value from template.yaml, lambda.tf and taskdef.json as function and task environment (Tested). The IaC files themselves were not deployed to real AWS.

How to test

Step Command or signal Pass when
1. Posture go version -m <binary> or go-pq-posture.sh <dir> go1.24+ and no tlsmlkem=0 (table below)
2. Code scan go-pq-lint.sh . Prints go-pq-lint: OK
3. Local client test gen-certs.sh and pq-servers.sh in debian:trixie-slim (OpenSSL 3.5): PQ-only on 8443, classical on 8444, mTLS versions on 8445 and 8446 Your client reaches 8443 (PQ) and 8444 (fallback)
4. Your server openssl s_client -brief -connect host:port -CAfile ca.pem -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3, then -groups X25519 Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768; classical still connects
5. Certificates and trust (cases C, D) Step 4 with -servername <each name> and -sigalgs ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256 or rsa_pss_rsae_sha256; client against 8443 with your CA setup, then with none Each certificate served over PQ; no CA fails with unknown authority
6. mTLS (case E) Client with its cert against 8445; s_client ... -cert client.pem -key client.key to your server Succeeds; without the cert, certificate required; server logs client_cn
7. Rollback Restart with GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0 8443 fails, 8444 works; remove it and 8443 works again
8. In-cloud signal Server tls_group= log; CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange X25519MLKEM768

For a TLS 1.2 probe of an ECDSA P-256 certificate, offer -groups X25519:P-256; with X25519 alone OpenSSL fails with wrong curve, a probe artifact.

Posture without running anything. go version -m reads the build info from any Go binary (Tested):

$ go version -m probe-go1.26-mod1.23
probe-go1.26-mod1.23: go1.26.11
        build   DefaultGODEBUG=...,tlsmlkem=0,tlssecpmlkem=0,...
$ go version -m probe-go1.26-mod1.26
probe-go1.26-mod1.26: go1.26.11            # no DefaultGODEBUG line = toolchain defaults = PQ
go version -m shows Meaning
go1.23.x or older No PQ possible: rebuild
tlsmlkem=0 in DefaultGODEBUG Classical: raise the go line or set tlsmlkem=1
tlssecpmlkem=0 X25519MLKEM768 only, no SecP hybrids (matters for FIPS peers)
GOEXPERIMENT=boringcrypto Check for crypto/tls/fipsonly (no PQ with it)
GOFIPS140= / fips140=on FIPS mode: Go 1.25+ for any PQ, 1.26+ for the SecP hybrids

Run-time GODEBUG isn't in the binary, so also check the function, task, pod or unit environment.

Log the group. Go 1.25+ exposes it as ConnectionState.CurveID (Go 1.25; Documented): log r.TLS.CurveID in HTTP handlers, resp.TLS.CurveID in clients and credentials.TLSInfo.State.CurveID in a gRPC interceptor (full server in cmd/server/main.go below). CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange covers only KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager and ACM calls (see AWS service endpoints and Detection).

grpc addr=:9446 tls_group=X25519MLKEM768
raw addr=:9449 handshake_error=tls: no key exchanges supported by both client and server

Rollout checklist

# Step Owner Done when
1 Inventory binaries: go-pq-posture.sh over Lambda bootstrap files, image binaries and EC2 hosts (SSM Run Command) Platform team Every Go workload has a toolchain, go line and FIPS mode recorded in the inventory
2 Inventory source and IaC: go-pq-lint.sh per repo; export GODEBUG from functions, task definitions, pod specs and units Service team Every CurvePreferences, MaxVersion, fipsonly and tlsmlkem=0 hit has a ticket
3 Classify each workload: rebuild only; go line; settings (F); FIPS (G, H); Go 1.23 that can't be rebuilt Service team Each workload has one fix from the decision guide, or an exception
4 Change: Go 1.26+ toolchain and go 1.26; fix settings; FIPS on native GOFIPS140 with the AWS SDK fix. Use GODEBUG only where the go line can't move Service team go-pq-lint.sh and go-pq-posture.sh pass in CI
5 CI gate: lint before build, posture after build, in warn mode first, then fail Platform team New Go builds can't ship classical
6 Canary clients first (they fall back, so they can't break a classical server): one Lambda alias weight, one ECS task set, one Deployment, or one EC2 host by tag Service team A day with no rise in handshake errors or p99 latency
7 Verify observed: CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange = X25519MLKEM768 for the workload's role; application tls_group= logs; Zeek on EC2-backed nodes Security / detection The workload's AWS API calls and logged handshakes show the hybrid
8 Servers next (they still accept classical clients); for load balancer targets, move the listener to a PQ policy Service team Target leg and server logs show PQ
9 Exceptions: peers that stay classical (third parties, AWS legs that don't support PQ), and every interim GODEBUG override, recorded with an owner and end date Service owner Exception list reviewed; no unowned override
10 Clean up: raise the go line and remove GODEBUG overrides; PQ-only (case F) last, per endpoint, after logs show no classical clients Service team No GODEBUG TLS overrides left; PQ-only only where planned

Pitfalls and rollback

Pitfall What happens Evidence
New toolchain, old go line The most common miss: Go 1.27 with go 1.23 stays classical. Check DefaultGODEBUG, not the toolchain Tested
CurvePreferences set anywhere One list without a hybrid makes that client or server classical, silently; check libraries (AWS SDK in FIPS mode) Tested
tlsmlkem=0 left set "temporarily" On 1.24 to 1.26 it beats even an explicit hybrid list Tested
FIPS on Go 1.24, or boringcrypto + fipsonly No PQ at all; Go 1.25 fips140=only fails every handshake Tested
Go 1.26 with go 1.25 X25519MLKEM768 works, SecP256r1MLKEM768 doesn't: the only hybrid some FIPS-oriented servers accept Tested
godebug tlssecpmlkem=1 in go.mod on a 1.24 or 1.25 toolchain Build fails: unknown godebug "tlssecpmlkem" Tested
Go 1.23 isn't "almost PQ" Its Kyber draft (0x6399) isn't ML-KEM; PQ-only servers refuse it, and no GODEBUG or Lambda layer fixes it Tested
Big ClientHello ML-KEM adds about 1.2 KB, so the ClientHello spans two TCP segments; broken middleboxes time out (tldr.fail). See Network devices and egress Documented
x/crypto/pkcs12 on OpenSSL 3 PKCS#12 files Fails at start-up with unknown digest algorithm; use go-pkcs12 Tested
AWS CA bundle with your own *http.Client AWS_CA_BUNDLE or WithCustomCABundle fails at config load Tested
SSL_CERT_FILE with only a private CA Public endpoints then fail with unknown authority: it replaces the system bundle Documented
mTLS errors in TLS 1.3 A missing client cert shows as certificate required on the first read, not on dial Tested
RSA listed before EC Default clients get RSA; not a PQ issue, but surprising during certificate moves Tested

Rollback, fastest first; no code change needed (Tested in e2e: GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0,tlssecpmlkem=0 on a Go 1.26 build turned it classical):

  1. Set GODEBUG=tlsmlkem=0,tlssecpmlkem=0 in the function, task, pod or unit environment, then redeploy or restart.
  2. Rebuild with godebug tlsmlkem=0 in go.mod.
  3. Redeploy the previous artifact.

Record any rollback as a temporary exception with an owner and an end date, and remove the override once the peer is fixed.

Test evidence

e2e suite, run r1117041043 (2026-11-16, LocalStack Pro on arm64; not real AWS): 123 Go cases, all pass — 118 client cases and 5 load balancer target cases. "Pass" means the observed behaviour matched the expectation in the case file, which is "classical" for baseline and negative cases. Each client case connects to the observer's hybrid, PQ-only, mTLS, EC-certificate, TLS 1.2 and three ML-DSA listeners and also checks the exact list of groups the client offered.

Compute Cases Modes covered What it proved
Lambda zip, provided.al2023 and provided.al2 75 baseline (10), go line (5), GODEBUG (9), settings (9), trust (7), mTLS (6), pinning (2), FIPS (16), AWS SDK (4), pq-tls layer (4), pq-egress extension (3) Go 1.24 to 1.27 PQ by default on both runtimes, 1.23 classical; go line < 1.24 classical on every toolchain; GODEBUG env, go.mod godebug and //go:debug fix it; F, G and H tables as stated; layer no effect; extension makes Go 1.23 PQ
Lambda container image 2 image configs/go/Dockerfile.lambda with golang:1.26 PQ; golang:1.23 classical

ECS on EC2 5 + 1 target baseline on four bases Same as Fargate
EC2, AL2023 and AL2 AMIs 14 baseline (8), host DEFAULT:PQ (2), distro golang package build (2), systemd GODEBUG (1), FIPS (1) Host policy and OS OpenSSL make no difference; the distro toolchain in the test AMIs (1.26.10) builds a PQ binary; systemd GODEBUG fixes an old go line
Load balancer target leg (server) 5 baseline, GODEBUG Go 1.26 target PQ under the PQ backend policy; go 1.23 line classical until GODEBUG; Go 1.23 classical; classic policy always TLS 1.2 classical

ML-DSA listeners: every Go case got the ECDSA certificate from the dual-certificate server and failed the ML-DSA-only and ML-DSA client-certificate listeners (case J).

Test code. Case definitions and expectations: e2e/cases/go.py; probe client: e2e/clients/go/probe.go and the other files in e2e/clients/go; builds (one static binary per toolchain, go line and FIPS mode): e2e/build/go.sh; load balancer target cases and server: e2e/cases/server.py, e2e/clients/server/go; results: e2e/results/latest/summary.md. The per-case list is in the Go test appendix.

Not tested: real AWS (all runs on LocalStack and Docker); EKS; a real KMS, CloudHSM or PKCS#11 key; pgx with sslnegotiation=postgres; lib/pq, mongo-go-driver and WebSocket libraries; HTTPS_PROXY through an intercepting proxy; the Microsoft and Red Hat Go builds; run-time tlssecpmlkem=1 on Go 1.24 and 1.25 (Documented as ignored); pq-egress for AWS SDK calls.

6.6 Python

Problem statement

A Python service on the wrong base image or runtime negotiates classical X25519 or P-256 with no ML-KEM, so traffic it sends or receives can be recorded now and decrypted later by a quantum computer (harvest-now-decrypt-later). Nothing fails, so nobody notices.

Python has no PQ switch of its own. The TLS library each package uses decides, and every package belongs to one of two families:

Family Packages Who decides the key exchange PQ when Evidence
ssl family: linked to the system OpenSSL through the ssl module ssl, http.client, urllib, requests, urllib3, boto3/botocore (default path), httpx, aiohttp, redis-py, PyMySQL, uvicorn, gunicorn, hypercorn, and Flask/Django/FastAPI behind them The OpenSSL the interpreter links (base image, Lambda runtime or host) plus its config (OPENSSL_CONF, the AL2023 crypto policy) OpenSSL 3.5+ and the config keeps ML-KEM in the group list Tested
Bundled-TLS wheels: carry their own TLS library grpcio (BoringSSL), awscrt (s2n-tls + AWS-LC), confluent-kafka (static OpenSSL 3.5.8 in librdkafka 2.16.0), psycopg [binary] and psycopg2-binary (own libpq + libssl), pyOpenSSL (the OpenSSL in the cryptography wheel) The wheel version Already PQ in current wheels on every base image tested, including Debian 12 with OpenSSL 3.0 Tested

So the first question for any service is which family each connection uses: a requests call on Debian 12 is classical while a grpcio call from the same container is PQ. The fix is almost always a base-image change, one file plus one environment variable, or a runtime upgrade. Certificates, key files, PKCS#12 stores and CA bundles do not change, because key exchange is negotiated apart from the certificate (cases C and D, Tested).

Python 3.15 (released 9 Oct 2026, python.org) adds SSLContext.set_groups(), get_groups() and SSLSocket.group() (3.15 ssl docs, Documented and Tested). Earlier versions have no group API; set_ecdh_curve("X25519MLKEM768") raises ValueError: unknown elliptic curve name on 3.12, 3.13 and 3.15 (Tested).

Quick start by compute type

Find your row, make the one change, verify, and keep the rollback. Everything here is for the ssl family; bundled-TLS wheels need only a current version on every row. All "Tested" rows ran in the e2e suite (run r1117041043) or in Lambda base images; none ran on real AWS.

Compute type Check PQ today? The one change Verify Roll back Evidence
Lambda zip, python3.12, 3.13, 3.14 Runtime in the IaC No: OpenSSL 3.5.8, but the AL2023 crypto policy omits ML-KEM Ship pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf in the package, set OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf. Fleet option: the pq-tls layer with AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper pq_probe.py against a PQ-only server succeeds; CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange = X25519MLKEM768 for KMS, STS, Secrets Manager calls Remove OPENSSL_CONF (layer: PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1) Tested (e2e)
Lambda zip, python3.15 (public preview) as above No, same as 3.14 Same file and variable; or in code ctx.set_groups("X25519MLKEM768:X25519"). Preview runtimes are outside the Lambda SLA (Lambda runtimes) as above, plus SSLSocket.group() in logs Remove the variable or the call Tested (Lambda 3.15 base image, not in e2e)
Lambda zip, python3.9, 3.10, 3.11 Runtime in the IaC No: Amazon Linux 2, OpenSSL 1.1.1zb, no setting helps Upgrade the runtime to python3.13. Bridge until then: the shim (PYTHONPATH=/var/task/pq-tls/pyshim plus pyOpenSSL, cryptography and idna), or the pq-tls layer. Covers urllib3, requests and boto3 only CloudWatch log line pq-tls: python legacy shim: urllib3 -> pyOpenSSL; PQ-only probe succeeds Remove PYTHONPATH (layer: PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1) Tested (e2e)
Lambda container image FROM public.ecr.aws/lambda/python:3.x No (3.12+), as the zip COPY the file and ENV OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf in the Dockerfile Run pq_probe.py in the built image in CI Redeploy the previous image digest, or override OPENSSL_CONF in the function variables Tested (e2e, 3.12 and 3.13)
ECS on Fargate FROM line; python3 -c "import ssl; print(ssl.OPENSSL_VERSION)" in the image python:*-slim-trixie, python:*-alpine: yes. -bookworm, Ubuntu 24.04, AL2023: no Move to python:3.13-slim-trixie or -alpine. On AL2023: ENV OPENSSL_CONF=/etc/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf (or the task definition) or update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ in the Dockerfile PQ-only probe in CI; server: probe-server.sh app:8443 Redeploy the previous image digest; remove the task-definition variable Tested (e2e)
ECS on EC2, EKS as Fargate: the image decides, not the node as Fargate as Fargate; on EKS the variable goes in the pod spec env as Fargate as Fargate ECS on EC2: Tested (e2e). EKS: not run; same images
EC2, Amazon Linux 2023 update-crypto-policies --show; python3.X -c "import ssl; print(ssl.OPENSSL_VERSION)" No: OpenSSL 3.5.8, policy DEFAULT omits ML-KEM One service: Environment=OPENSSL_CONF=/etc/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf in its systemd unit. Whole host: update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ through SSM, then restart services PQ-only probe on the host; CloudTrail Remove the drop-in, or update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT, then restart Tested (e2e, python3.11 to 3.14)
EC2, Amazon Linux 2 cat /etc/os-release No, and cannot be: python3.7 with OpenSSL 1.0.2k, TLS 1.2 only Migrate the host to AL2023 (AL2 end of life 30 Jun 2026, Lambda runtimes) n/a n/a Tested (e2e)
EC2, Ubuntu 24.04 or Debian 12 as above No: OpenSSL 3.0 Run the service in a trixie or alpine container, or move the OS PQ-only probe Previous AMI or image Debian 12: Tested. Ubuntu 24.04: Documented (ships OpenSSL 3.0)

Two rules apply on every row:

  • If the function, image or unit already sets OPENSSL_CONF, merge the Groups line from openssl-pq.cnf into that file. The layer leaves an existing OPENSSL_CONF alone, so such a function stays classical (Tested, e2e py-lambda-py312-layer-ownconf).
  • Don't use OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null. It does turn PQ on (Tested 2026-11-16, Lambda python3.12 and 3.15 images), but it drops the whole AL2023 crypto policy, not just the group list (in the Lambda python3.12 image the default context lost TLS_AES_128_CCM_SHA256 and the policy's TLS 1.3 maximum: tests/lambda/baseline-default.json against baseline-devnull.json).

Lambda deadlines: python3.9 was deprecated on 15 Dec 2025, python3.10 on 31 Oct 2026, and python3.11 is deprecated on 30 Jun 2027. Function creation is blocked from 29 Jul 2027 and updates from 31 Aug 2027 for all three (Lambda runtimes, Documented). After that no file, layer or variable can be added, so these functions move first.

Versions and native support

PQ depends on the OpenSSL under Python, so the same Python version gives different answers on Lambda, in a container and on EC2.

Version ssl-family OpenSSL PQ by default (client and server) Group API Proposed solution Evidence
3.9, 3.10, 3.11 Lambda: 1.1.1zb (AL2). AL2023 (EC2, containers): 3.5.8. trixie: 3.5.7 Lambda: no. AL2023: no. trixie: yes None; set_ecdh_curve takes one classical curve only Lambda: upgrade to python3.12+; bridge with the shim (requests, boto3 only). AL2023: the config or DEFAULT:PQ. Containers: trixie or alpine base Tested (Lambda 3.9 to 3.11, AL2023 3.11, trixie 3.11)
3.12, 3.13, 3.14 Lambda and AL2023: 3.5.8. trixie: 3.5.7. alpine 3.24: 3.5.9. bookworm: 3.0.22 Lambda, AL2023: no (crypto policy). trixie, alpine: yes. bookworm: no None Lambda and AL2023: OPENSSL_CONF=openssl-pq.cnf or DEFAULT:PQ. bookworm: move to trixie Tested
3.15 (Lambda: public preview) As 3.12 As 3.12 SSLContext.set_groups(), get_groups(), SSLSocket.group() Platform fix as 3.12; set_groups("X25519MLKEM768:X25519") also works without the file; log group() Tested (trixie 3.15, Lambda 3.15 image)
Bundled-TLS wheels, any version BoringSSL, s2n-tls, static OpenSSL 3.5.8, own libssl, cryptography's OpenSSL 4.0.3 Yes; grpcio server also yes Library-specific (ssl.curves.list, TlsCipherPref) Keep wheels current Tested

FIPS mode for the ssl family (AL2023 in FIPS mode, or a FIPS OpenSSL provider) was not tested; see case G.

By library (does the client offer ML-KEM to a PQ-only server?)

Library Lambda 3.11 (AL2) Lambda 3.14 Lambda 3.14 + conf trixie 3.13 / 3.15 bookworm 3.12 AL2023 3.12 AL2023 3.12 + conf
