All cells Tested on 2026-11-14 in containers (arm64) against an OpenSSL 3.5.7 s_server limited to X25519MLKEM768 and one limited to X25519 (tests/deep/python/run-python.sh, outputs in tests/deep/python/out). Every library also connected to the classical-only server, so fallback works. For grpcio, Kafka and Redis the server log confirmed Peer Temp Key: X25519MLKEM768; PostgreSQL drivers were tested against PostgreSQL 18.7 with ssl_groups = 'X25519MLKEM768'.

Gaps: on the Lambda python3.11 image pip found no confluent-kafka wheel. On Python 3.15 (trixie and the Lambda image) pip chose confluent-kafka 4.4.0b1, whose build failed to import, so Kafka is untested on 3.11 Lambda and on 3.15. The Lambda 3.15 image otherwise matched Lambda 3.14.

Cases: what to change

Unless a row says otherwise, results here were Tested on 2026-11-14 in containers (arm64) on Debian 13 trixie with Python 3.12.16 and 3.13.18 (OpenSSL 3.5.7, no setting), Amazon Linux 2023 with Python 3.12.15 and OPENSSL_CONF (OpenSSL 3.5.8), and the Lambda python:3.11 image with the shim (clients only). Test servers are OpenSSL 3.5.7 s_server instances: PQ-only (-groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3), classical-only (-groups X25519), and the same pair requiring a client certificate. A client that reaches the PQ-only server has proved it offered X25519MLKEM768. Python servers were probed with openssl s_client, which printed Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768. Scripts and outputs: tests/deep/python/v2 (run-v2.sh, cases.py, servers.py, out/d312.txt, out/d313.txt, out/al-conf.txt, out/l311-shim.txt).

A. Simple client

Who this is: code that calls requests, boto3, httpx, aiohttp, urllib or a DB/queue driver with default TLS settings; grep -rnE "ssl\.|SSLContext|verify=|cert=" src/ finds nothing unusual.

What to change: the platform fix only (Quick start). No code change. Code is needed only to pin the group list (3.15+) or to log the negotiated group.

Library (tested version) TLS stack What to set Evidence
ssl, http.client, urllib.request (3.12, 3.13, 3.15) System OpenSSL Nothing. On 3.15+ you may call ctx.set_groups("X25519MLKEM768:X25519"); "X25519MLKEM768" alone refuses classical servers Tested
requests 2.34.2, urllib3 2.8.0 System OpenSSL via ssl Nothing. To pin groups on 3.15, pass a context through an HTTPAdapter (below) Tested
boto3 / botocore 1.44.3 (default HTTP path) System OpenSSL via ssl Nothing. botocore builds its own SSLContext, so inject_into_urllib3() alone does not reach it; the Lambda shim patches both Tested
awscrt 0.38.1 (boto3[crt], S3 transfer manager) s2n-tls, bundled Nothing: the default policy offered ML-KEM; TlsCipherPref.PQ_DEFAULT also works. PQ_TLSv1_0_2021_05 raises AWS_IO_TLS_CIPHER_PREF_UNSUPPORTED. The FIPS policy TLSv1_2_2025_07 allows only P-256/P-384 per its docstring Tested; FIPS policy from the docstring
httpx 0.28.1 System OpenSSL via ssl Nothing. To pin on 3.15: httpx.Client(verify=ctx) Tested
aiohttp 3.14.5 System OpenSSL via ssl Nothing. To pin on 3.15: session.get(url, ssl=ctx) Tested
pyOpenSSL 26.4.0 cryptography wheel's OpenSSL (4.0.3) Nothing; PQ even on Debian 12 and AL2023 without the config Tested
grpcio 1.85.1 (client) BoringSSL, bundled Nothing. ClientHello listed X25519MLKEM768:x25519:secp256r1:secp384r1:secp521r1 on every image. No Python group setting; OPENSSL_CONF has no effect Tested
psycopg 3.3.9 [binary] (libpq 18), psycopg2-binary 2.9.13 (libpq 17) libpq + libssl bundled Nothing; use sslmode=verify-full. Connected to PostgreSQL 18 with ssl_groups = 'X25519MLKEM768' on every image Tested
psycopg or psycopg2 built from source, mysqlclient System libpq / client library and OpenSSL Platform fix, as for the ssl family Unverified (follows from linkage)
PyMySQL System OpenSSL via ssl.create_default_context (read from source) Platform fix; the MySQL server must also offer ML-KEM Source read; handshake not tested
redis-py 8.1.0 System OpenSSL via ssl Platform fix (ssl=True, ssl_ca_certs) Tested
confluent-kafka 2.x (librdkafka 2.16.0) OpenSSL 3.5.8, static in the wheel Nothing. ssl.curves.list=X25519MLKEM768:X25519 also works. The broker or Amazon MSK must also accept ML-KEM Tested (client); MSK not tested

Pin the group list for requests on Python 3.15+ (most services do not need this):

import ssl, requests
from requests.adapters import HTTPAdapter

class PQAdapter(HTTPAdapter):
    def init_poolmanager(self, *args, **kwargs):
        ctx = ssl.create_default_context()
        ctx.set_groups("X25519MLKEM768:X25519")  # 3.15+; omit X25519 to refuse classical
        kwargs["ssl_context"] = ctx
        return super().init_poolmanager(*args, **kwargs)

s = requests.Session()
s.mount("https://", PQAdapter())

Log the negotiated group once per dependency at startup (3.15+): log.info("tls", extra={"host": host, "group": tls_sock.group()}).

B. Simple server

Who this is: uvicorn, gunicorn, hypercorn or a grpcio server that terminates TLS in the Python process: an HTTPS target group, an NLB TCP passthrough, in-VPC service-to-service, or gRPC. If an ALB, NLB TLS listener, API Gateway or CloudFront terminates TLS, that service's security policy sets the client-facing key exchange, not Python (guide chapters: Load balancers, Edge and API front doors).

What to change: the platform fix only. Python servers accept ML-KEM whenever the linked OpenSSL is 3.5+ with ML-KEM in its group list; no server flag is needed. A server without ML-KEM still serves PQ-capable clients over X25519, so the failure is silent.

Server (tested version) TLS stack trixie 3.13 / 3.15 bookworm 3.12 AL2023 3.12 AL2023 + OPENSSL_CONF Group control in code
uvicorn 0.54.0 (--ssl-certfile) System OpenSSL PQ Classical only Classical only PQ 3.15+: build Config, call config.load(), then config.ssl.set_groups(...) (Tested)
gunicorn 27.0.1 (certfile) System OpenSSL PQ Classical only Classical only PQ 3.15+: ssl_context hook calling set_groups (Tested)
hypercorn 0.18.0 (--certfile) System OpenSSL PQ Classical only Classical only PQ No hook checked; platform only
grpcio 1.85.1 (ssl_server_credentials) BoringSSL, bundled PQ PQ PQ PQ None needed

All rows Tested with openssl s_client -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 and -groups X25519. "Classical only" means the PQ-only client was refused with a handshake alert; classical clients connected in every cell.

  • uvicorn --ssl-ciphers and gunicorn ciphers set TLS 1.2 cipher suites only; they do not affect groups (Tested).
  • Enforcing PQ-only (refusing classical clients) needs Python 3.15 set_groups("X25519MLKEM768"). Tested for gunicorn and uvicorn; on 3.12 and 3.13 the same hook is a no-op. Only do this on internal endpoints whose every client you control.
  • Flask app.run(ssl_context=ctx) takes an SSLContext (Werkzeug docs, Documented), so set_groups should work on 3.15; not tested. Not for production. Django runserver has no TLS; production Django and FastAPI run under the servers above.
  • Behind an ALB or NLB TLS listener (e2e, load balancer modelled as the client on the target leg): an ssl-module HTTPS target on AL2023 Python 3.12 or python:3.12-slim-bookworm stayed classical; OPENSSL_CONF, DEFAULT:PQ or python:3.13-slim-trixie gave X25519MLKEM768 with the same certificate. The classic backend leg stayed TLS 1.2 in every case (Tested, cases srv-python-*).

gunicorn.conf.py hook (full file at the end):

def ssl_context(conf, default_ssl_context_factory):
    ctx = default_ssl_context_factory()
    if hasattr(ctx, "set_groups"):  # Python 3.15+
        ctx.set_groups(os.environ.get("PQ_GROUPS", "X25519MLKEM768:X25519:secp256r1"))
    return ctx

C. Existing server certificates and key files

Who this is: a Python server that loads its own certificate and key. Find it with:

grep -rnE "load_cert_chain|ssl[-_]keyfile|ssl[-_]certfile|--keyfile|--certfile|keyfile *=|certfile *=|ssl_server_credentials|pkcs12|\.p12|\.pfx" .

What changes in the store: nothing. The certificate and key sign the handshake; ML-KEM changes only the key exchange. The same RSA or EC certificate, encrypted key and PKCS#12 file negotiated X25519MLKEM768 once the platform fix was in place. Do not re-issue certificates for PQ key exchange; ML-DSA certificates are a separate, later step (guide chapter: Certificates (ML-DSA)).

What to change: the platform fix only. Two exceptions, neither about PQ:

  • gunicorn with an encrypted key. gunicorn has no key-password option. The master reports Listening at: https://..., but every worker fails with OSError: [Errno 22] Invalid argument and clients get unexpected eof (Tested). Load the key in the ssl_context hook with the password from the environment (full gunicorn-enc.conf.py at the end):

    def ssl_context(conf, default_ssl_context_factory):
    ctx = ssl.SSLContext(ssl.PROTOCOL_TLS_SERVER)  # the factory loads the key without a password
    ctx.load_cert_chain(conf.certfile, conf.keyfile, password=os.environ["TLS_KEY_PASSWORD"])
    return ctx

  • PKCS#12 / PFX. The ssl module cannot read PKCS#12, and pyOpenSSL 26.4.0 no longer has crypto.load_pkcs12 (Tested). Convert with cryptography's pkcs12.load_key_and_certificates() at startup (p12_to_pem.py at the end) to a tmpfs path readable only by the service user, or once at build time with openssl pkcs12 -in server.p12 -nodes -out server.pem (key mode 0600; add -aes256 and load_cert_chain(..., password=...) to keep it encrypted).

Tested proof (out/d312.txt, out/d313.txt, out/al-conf.txt; each row: a PQ-only client negotiated X25519MLKEM768 and an X25519-only client still connected):

Server and store Cert served Result with the platform fix
ssl.SSLContext + load_cert_chain, RSA 2048 PEM / EC P-256 PEM RSA / EC X25519MLKEM768; classical client OK
load_cert_chain(..., password=...) (string or callable), encrypted RSA and EC RSA / EC X25519MLKEM768; classical client OK
RSA and EC in one context (two load_cert_chain calls) EC to an ECDSA-only client, RSA to an RSA-only client X25519MLKEM768 for both
SNI: sni_callback swaps to a second EC cert The cert for the requested name X25519MLKEM768
PKCS#12 converted to PEM with cryptography 50.0.2 EC X25519MLKEM768; classical client OK
uvicorn 0.54.0 --ssl-certfile/--ssl-keyfile, plain and --ssl-keyfile-password RSA / EC X25519MLKEM768; classical client OK
gunicorn 27.0.1 --certfile/--keyfile, plain key RSA / EC X25519MLKEM768; classical client OK
gunicorn --keyfile with an encrypted key none Workers fail (OSError: [Errno 22]); not a PQ issue
gunicorn encrypted key through the ssl_context hook EC X25519MLKEM768; classical client OK

On Debian 12 and on AL2023 without the config the same servers were classical-only (tests/deep/python/out/b312.txt, out/al.txt): the certificate never made a difference. grpcio servers were PQ on every image. Not tested: hypercorn with an encrypted key, HSM or KMS keys (case I), Windows.

D. Custom CA trust (private CA, custom bundle, system store)

Who this is: a client that trusts a private CA (AWS Private CA, an internal root, a TLS-inspecting proxy root). Find it with:

grep -rnE "REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE|SSL_CERT_FILE|SSL_CERT_DIR|AWS_CA_BUNDLE|CURL_CA_BUNDLE|cafile=|capath=|load_verify_locations|verify=['\"/]|certifi|truststore|ca_certs|root_certificates|GRPC_DEFAULT_SSL_ROOTS_FILE_PATH" . ; env | grep -E "CA_BUNDLE|SSL_CERT"

Also check Dockerfiles for update-ca-certificates, update-ca-trust or a PEM appended to certifi's cacert.pem.

What changes in the trust store: nothing. Trust decides which server certificates are accepted; key exchange is negotiated apart from it. Every method below kept working against the PQ-only server once the platform fix was in place.

What to change: the platform fix only. Two things that are not PQ issues often surface during the change:

  • requests ignores SSL_CERT_FILE and the OS store; it uses certifi unless REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE, verify= or truststore says otherwise. httpx 0.28.1 honours SSL_CERT_FILE but not the OS store (both Tested).
  • A base-image move (bookworm to trixie, or to AL2023) resets the OS store. Re-add the CA in the new Dockerfile: Debian/Ubuntu/Alpine COPY corp-root.pem /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/corp-root.crt + RUN update-ca-certificates; AL2023 COPY corp-root.pem /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/ + RUN update-ca-trust extract. A CA appended to certifi is lost on every pip install -U certifi; prefer REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE or truststore.

Tested proof, platform fix in place (Debian 13 3.12 and 3.13, AL2023 3.12 + OPENSSL_CONF). OK = reached both the PQ-only and the classical-only server:

Trust method Result
ssl.create_default_context(cafile=ca.pem), SSL_CERT_FILE=ca.pem, CA in the OS store OK
requests REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE=ca.pem, verify="ca.pem", certifi with the CA appended OK
requests with SSL_CERT_FILE only, or CA only in the OS store Fails verification on both servers: requests uses certifi (expected, not PQ)
truststore.inject_into_ssl() (0.10.5) + requests, CA in the OS store; truststore.SSLContext(...) OK
httpx verify=ssl.create_default_context(cafile=...), httpx with SSL_CERT_FILE OK
httpx with CA only in the OS store Fails verification (uses certifi; expected)
aiohttp ssl=ssl.create_default_context(cafile=...) OK
boto3 AWS_CA_BUNDLE=ca.pem (endpoint pointed at the test server) TLS OK on both servers (the API call then times out: the server is not STS)
Negative control: requests with stock certifi Fails verification (proves verification was on)

On Lambda python3.9 to 3.11 with the shim, REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE, verify=, an appended certifi and boto3 AWS_CA_BUNDLE all keep working, but truststore.inject_into_ssl() turns the shim off for requests (case J).

E. Mutual TLS (client certificates)

Who this is: a client that presents a certificate, or a server that requires one. grep -rnE "cert=\(|load_cert_chain|client_cert|ssl-cert-reqs|cert_reqs|CERT_REQUIRED|ssl_channel_credentials|require_client_auth" .

What to change: the platform fix only; client certificates and keys are unaffected. With the fix every row completed mTLS against a PQ-only server requiring a client certificate and against a classical-only one (Tested).

Client or server Setting Result
requests cert=("client.pem", "client.key"), verify=ca OK; without a cert: TLSV13_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED
ssl, httpx 0.28.1, aiohttp 3.14.5 ctx.load_cert_chain(crt, key[, password=...]) passed as the context OK, including an encrypted key
boto3 Config(client_cert=(crt, key)) + AWS_CA_BUNDLE TLS OK (the API call then times out on the test server)
grpcio 1.85.1 client grpc.ssl_channel_credentials(root, key, chain) Server log: Peer Temp Key: X25519MLKEM768, Verification: OK
ssl server verify_mode=CERT_REQUIRED + load_verify_locations(ca) X25519MLKEM768 with the client cert
uvicorn / gunicorn --ssl-cert-reqs 2 --ssl-ca-certs ca.pem / --cert-reqs 2 --ca-certs ca.pem X25519MLKEM768 with the client cert

requests cannot take a password for an encrypted client key; build an ssl context or use httpx. PKCS#12 client stores: convert as in case C.

F. Explicit TLS settings that block PQ

Who this is: code or config that sets TLS versions, curves or ciphers by hand. Find it with ci/pq-tls-lint.sh (full file at the end) or:

grep -rnE "set_ecdh_curve|maximum_version|ssl_maximum_version|OP_NO_TLSv1_3|PROTOCOL_TLSv1|set_ciphers|ssl[-_]ciphers|set_groups|ssl\.curves\.list|OPENSSL_CONF" .
Setting PQ-only peer Classical peer Change it to Evidence
ctx.set_ciphers("ECDHE+AESGCM:..."), uvicorn --ssl-ciphers, gunicorn ciphers OK, X25519MLKEM768 OK Nothing (TLS 1.2 suites only) Tested
ctx.minimum_version = TLSVersion.TLSv1_3 OK OK Nothing Tested
ctx.maximum_version = TLSVersion.TLSv1_2, ctx.options |= ssl.OP_NO_TLSv1_3, ssl.SSLContext(ssl.PROTOCOL_TLSv1_2), urllib3 ssl_maximum_version=TLSv1_2 (client) Fails: TLSV1_ALERT_PROTOCOL_VERSION OK, TLS 1.2 Remove it; use ssl.create_default_context() or PROTOCOL_TLS_CLIENT / PROTOCOL_TLS_SERVER Tested
ctx.maximum_version = TLSv1_2 (server) Fails Fails for a TLS 1.3-only client Remove it Tested
ctx.set_ecdh_curve("prime256v1") (client or server) Fails: handshake failure Fails against an X25519-only peer too Remove it Tested
ctx.set_ecdh_curve("X25519") Fails OK Remove it Tested
ctx.set_ecdh_curve("X25519MLKEM768") ValueError: unknown elliptic curve name same Use set_groups on 3.15+, else the platform Tested
ctx.set_groups(...) (3.15+) or confluent-kafka ssl.curves.list without an ML-KEM group Fails OK Put X25519MLKEM768 first, keep X25519 set_groups replacing the whole list Tested (tests/deep/python/out/t315.txt); the "X25519"-only string not run
A custom OPENSSL_CONF without a Groups line on AL2023 or Lambda Classical OK Merge the Groups line from openssl-pq.cnf Tested (e2e py-lambda-py312-layer-ownconf)
OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null on AL2023 or Lambda OK, X25519MLKEM768 OK Point it at openssl-pq.cnf instead: /dev/null drops the whole crypto policy Tested 2026-11-16 (Lambda python3.12 and 3.15 images)

set_ecdh_curve is the trap: on OpenSSL 3 it replaces the whole group list with one classical curve, so it removes ML-KEM on every platform, client or server. The Python docs say it "doesn't apply to client sockets" (ssl docs), but in the tests it did change the client's offer. Old code often has it from TLS 1.2 tuning. Removing these settings keeps TLS 1.2-only peers working, because the defaults still allow TLS 1.2.

G. FIPS mode

Who this is: AL2023 with FIPS mode enabled (fips-mode-setup --check), images with a FIPS OpenSSL provider, awscrt with a FIPS policy, or FIPS AWS endpoints.

What happens today: not tested for the ssl family; whether the AL2023 FIPS provider offers ML-KEM is not confirmed here (guide chapter: Hosts and OS). awscrt's FIPS policy TLSv1_2_2025_07 lists only P-256 and P-384 in its docstring, so no PQ. Whether FIPS AWS endpoints accept ML-KEM was not checked here: not sure.

What to change: nothing in Python. Treat FIPS paths as classical until the platform team confirms ML-KEM in the FIPS provider, and record them as exceptions.

H. Libraries with their own TLS stack

Who this is: grpcio, awscrt, confluent-kafka, psycopg [binary], psycopg2-binary, pyOpenSSL. pip list | grep -Ei '^(grpcio|awscrt|confluent-kafka|psycopg|psycopg2-binary|pyopenssl|cryptography) '.

What happens today: PQ on every base image, including Debian 12 and AL2023 without the config (Tested, library table). The platform fix has no effect on them, and OPENSSL_CONF does not reach BoringSSL or s2n.

What to change: keep the wheels current. Building from source (psycopg[c], psycopg2, mysqlclient) links the system OpenSSL and follows the ssl family (Unverified, from linkage). grpcio custom trust goes through root_certificates= or GRPC_DEFAULT_SSL_ROOTS_FILE_PATH, not SSL_CERT_FILE.

Side effect: one process can be PQ on gRPC and classical on requests at the same time. Inventory by library, not by service.

I. Pinning, HSM/PKCS#11 and KMS-backed keys

  • Certificate pinning: unaffected. A pin on the SHA-256 of the peer certificate matched on the PQ-only server (Tested). Re-pin only when certificates change, for example to ML-DSA.
  • HSM / PKCS#11 / KMS keys: the ssl module cannot use them without an OpenSSL provider (for example pkcs11-provider) or a TLS-terminating proxy. The key only signs; ML-KEM is done in software, so PQ should not change this (Unverified, not tested).

J. Other cases

Lambda 3.9 to 3.11 shim with custom trust or mTLS (Lambda python:3.11 image, OpenSSL 1.1.1zb, PYTHONPATH=/kit/pyshim; Python 3.11.18, cryptography 50.0.2 with OpenSSL 4.0.3, pyOpenSSL 26.4.0, truststore 0.10.5; Tested 2026-11-14, out/l311-shim.txt):

Path PQ-only server Classical server Note
requests with REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE, verify=, appended certifi, or cert=(crt, key) OK OK Shim log: urllib3 -> pyOpenSSL (OpenSSL 4.0.3 ...)
boto3 AWS_CA_BUNDLE, Config(client_cert=...) TLS OK TLS OK Shim log: botocore -> pyOpenSSL
grpcio mTLS OK (Peer Temp Key: X25519MLKEM768) BoringSSL; no shim needed
truststore inject_into_ssl() + requests Fails OK truststore puts requests back on the stdlib context (OpenSSL 1.1.1). Use REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE instead
raw ssl, http.client, httpx, aiohttp Fails OK Not covered by the shim, also when loaded by the layer (e2e py-lambda-py311-layer-stdlib). Upgrade to python3.12+
urllib3 ssl_maximum_version=TLSv1_2 Fails OK Case F applies through the shim too

stdlib clients on Lambda 3.9 to 3.11. The interim option is the pq-egress extension: the function sends plain HTTP to http://127.0.0.1:2779/<route>/<path> and the extension makes the TLS connection with X25519MLKEM768 (Tested in e2e on python3.9 to 3.14, and for http.client on 3.11 through the combined layer). Not tested for AWS SDK calls (SigV4 signs the host name) or on real AWS. Source: e2e/lambda/extension/pq-egress.

HTTPS proxy (HTTPS_PROXY). With a CONNECT proxy TLS runs end to end, so the client's PQ setting applies. A TLS-inspecting proxy terminates TLS and decides both legs (guide chapter: Network devices and egress). Not tested.

WebSockets, HTTP/2. websockets, aiohttp WebSockets and h2/httpx HTTP/2 use the ssl module: platform fix only (Unverified for websockets).

Service mesh sidecar. If Envoy or Linkerd originates mTLS for the pod, the sidecar decides (guide chapter: Service mesh). The app's own TLS still follows this chapter.

Child processes. OPENSSL_CONF is inherited by subprocess, the AWS CLI and curl. Programs that link GnuTLS (Debian curl and git builds) get no ML-KEM at all (Tested 2026-11-14).

Solution and automation

Step 1: inventory and detect

  1. Platform per service, inside the deployed image with the deployed env:

    python3 -c "import ssl,os; print(ssl.OPENSSL_VERSION, os.environ.get('OPENSSL_CONF'))"
cat /etc/os-release | head -2; (update-crypto-policies --show 2>/dev/null || true)
pip list | grep -Ei '^(grpcio|awscrt|confluent-kafka|psycopg|psycopg2-binary|pyopenssl|cryptography|truststore|certifi) '

  2. Cases C to F: run the grep line from each case over the app repo and the deployment repo (Dockerfiles, task definitions, Helm values).

  3. Live: pq_probe.py against a PQ-only test server from inside the image (How to test).

Step 2: change (packaging per compute type)

The Quick start table gives the change per row. Details that matter when you build it:

Lambda zip, python3.12+. Put the file in the package and set the variable (full SAM, CloudFormation and Terraform templates at the end):

build/
  app.py
  pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf      # keeps the AL2023 policy, changes only the group list
  <your dependencies>
      Runtime: python3.13
      Environment:
        Variables:
          OPENSSL_CONF: /var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf

Fleet option without repackaging: the pq-tls layer with AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper sets OPENSSL_CONF on python3.12+ and the shim on 3.9 to 3.11. It leaves a function's own OPENSSL_CONF alone, and chains an existing wrapper (Datadog, OpenTelemetry) through PQ_TLS_NEXT_WRAPPER (both Tested, e2e).

Lambda zip, python3.9 to 3.11 bridge. Add pq-tls/pyshim/sitecustomize.py and install pyOpenSSL, cryptography and idna inside the Lambda image of the same Python version and architecture:

docker run --rm --entrypoint sh -v "$PWD/build:/out" public.ecr.aws/lambda/python:3.11 \
  -c 'pip install --target /out cryptography==50.0.2 pyOpenSSL==26.4.0 idna==3.20'

Set PYTHONPATH=/var/task/pq-tls/pyshim. For a layer, install into python/lib/python3.X/site-packages.

  • idna is required. pyOpenSSL's urllib3 integration imports it and the Lambda runtime does not ship it. Without it the shim still logs urllib3 -> pyOpenSSL, then every urllib3, requests and boto3 request raises No module named 'idna' (Tested, e2e py-lambda-py311-shim-noidna).
  • Build once per Python version. The cryptography wheel pip picks on 3.11 fails to import on 3.9 and 3.10 (PyType_GetName), and pip install --platform manylinux2014_... hides cryptography 50.x. Installing inside each version's image avoids both (Tested).
  • Size: the bridge adds about 18 MB unpacked, 6 MB zipped (cryptography, cffi, pycparser, pyOpenSSL, idna; measured from the e2e probe-shim-py311.zip: 17,983,187 bytes unpacked against 28,647 for the probe alone).

Lambda container image (full Dockerfile.lambda at the end):

COPY pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf ${LAMBDA_TASK_ROOT}/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf
ENV OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf

Fargate, ECS on EC2, EKS. Pick the base image first; the Python version matters much less.

Base image OpenSSL ssl family PQ out of the box Action Evidence
python:3.x-slim-trixie (Debian 13) 3.5.7 Yes None Tested (3.11, 3.12, 3.13, 3.15)
python:3.x-alpine (Alpine 3.24) 3.5.9 Yes None Tested (3.12)
python:3.x-slim-bookworm (Debian 12) 3.0.22 No Move to trixie or alpine; nothing adds ML-KEM to OpenSSL 3.0 Tested
amazonlinux:2023 + python3.11 to python3.14 3.5.8 No: crypto policy omits ML-KEM ENV OPENSSL_CONF=/etc/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf with the file, or update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ in the Dockerfile Tested (e2e)
Ubuntu 24.04 3.0.x No Move to one of the above Documented

The variable can live in the task definition (environment) or pod spec (env) instead of the image. Add a build-time guard so an image regression fails CI:

RUN python -c "import ssl,sys; sys.exit(0 if ssl.OPENSSL_VERSION_INFO >= (3,5) else 'OpenSSL < 3.5')"

On AL2023 the version guard is not enough (3.5.8 is classical by default): also run pq_probe.py against a PQ-only server in CI.

EC2. AL2023: one service (Environment=OPENSSL_CONF=/etc/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf in its systemd unit) or the whole host (SSM document running update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ, then restart services); both files at the end, host-wide details in the guide chapter Hosts and OS. On AL2023 EC2 python3.11 links the system OpenSSL 3.5.8, unlike Lambda's python3.11, so the same fix works (Tested, e2e). AL2, Ubuntu 24.04 and Debian 12 hosts cannot be fixed in place.

When you change the base image, carry over the OS trust-store step (case D) and any key-password plumbing (case C).

Step 3: CI guardrails

  • Lint: sh ci/pq-tls-lint.sh src/ fails the build on the case F settings (full file at the end). Tested: it flagged maximum_version=TLSv1_2, OP_NO_TLSv1_3, PROTOCOL_TLSv1_2, set_ecdh_curve and ssl_maximum_version, and passed set_groups("X25519MLKEM768:X25519"). Mark a reviewed line with # pq-tls: allow.
  • Image gate: the OPENSSL_VERSION_INFO >= (3,5) line above; on AL2023 also RUN test "$OPENSSL_CONF" = /etc/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf.
  • Handshake gate: start pq-harness.sh as a service container and run pq_probe.py pqsrv:8443 and, for servers, probe-server.sh app:8443 inside the built image. Both must pass.
  • Policy: deny base images python:*-bookworm, ubuntu:24.04 and amazonlinux:2 and OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null in your image and pod policy (guide chapter: Enforcement and guardrails).

How to test

Run in the same image, env and user as the service. Steps 1 to 6 need no AWS account.

  1. Posture. Step 1 commands above. ssl family: OpenSSL 3.5 or later, and on AL2023 OPENSSL_CONF set or update-crypto-policies --show = DEFAULT:PQ. Bundled wheels: compare versions with the library table.
  2. Start the harness. pq-harness.sh (full file at the end) starts a PQ-only server on 8443, a classical-only one on 8444 and the mTLS pair on 8451/8452, signed by a throw-away CA. Run it in python:3.13-slim-trixie or debian:trixie-slim on the same Docker network.
  3. Client (cases A, D). SSL_CERT_FILE=/certs/ca.pem python3 pq_probe.py pqsrv:8443 must connect (on 3.15 it prints RESULT pqsrv:8443 TLSv1.3 group=X25519MLKEM768, Tested); before 3.15 the connection itself is the proof. pqsrv:8444 must also connect (fallback). Repeat with your real trust setting by pointing your client code at the harness.
  4. Server (cases B, C). Start the server with its real cert and key, then sh probe-server.sh app:8443: expect Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768 on the first check and a connection on the second.
  5. mTLS (case E). python3 -c "import requests; print(requests.get('https://pqsrv:8451/', verify='/certs/ca.pem', cert=('/certs/client.pem','/certs/client.key')).status_code)" must print 200. For a server that requires client certs: sh probe-server.sh app:8443 -cert client.pem -key client.key.
  6. Settings (case F). sh ci/pq-tls-lint.sh . must print clean.
  7. Rollback test. Remove OPENSSL_CONF (or redeploy the old image) in a test environment and rerun step 3: the PQ-only probe must now fail and the classical one pass. This proves the probe detects a regression.
  8. In the cloud. CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange shows X25519MLKEM768 on the workload's calls to KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager, ACM and Payment Cryptography (other services don't log it). On 3.15 log SSLSocket.group(). On Lambda with the shim, look for pq-tls: python legacy shim: urllib3 -> pyOpenSSL and botocore -> pyOpenSSL in CloudWatch Logs.

Rollout checklist

Owner: the service team, unless noted.

  1. Inventory (platform team). List every Python workload with compute type, runtime or base image, OpenSSL version and OPENSSL_CONF (Step 1). Tag each with its Quick start row.
  2. Library check. For each service, list which connections use the ssl family and which use bundled-TLS wheels. Bundled wheels: confirm current versions; nothing else to do.
  3. Settings sweep. Run ci/pq-tls-lint.sh and the case C to F greps. Remove set_ecdh_curve, TLS 1.2 maximums and OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null; merge Groups into any existing OPENSSL_CONF file.
  4. Plan the legacy Lambdas first. python3.9 to 3.11 functions: schedule the runtime upgrade before 29 Jul 2027 (create blocked) and 31 Aug 2027 (update blocked). Use the shim or layer only as a bridge, with idna in the package and no truststore.
  5. Make the one change from the Quick start row in a branch: file plus variable, base image, or crypto policy. Carry over CA and key-password steps if the base image changes.
  6. Add CI gates: lint, image guard, and the PQ-only and classical-only probe against pq-harness.sh.
  7. Canary. Deploy to one task, pod, host or a 10% weighted Lambda alias. Watch error rate and latency for a day.
  8. Confirm the signal. CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange = X25519MLKEM768 for that role's KMS, STS or Secrets Manager calls, or the group() log on 3.15. No signal means not done.
  9. Check the peers. For servers, confirm what terminates TLS in front (ALB, NLB, API Gateway, CloudFront) and whether its policy is PQ. For Kafka, PostgreSQL, MySQL and Redis, confirm the server side offers ML-KEM.
  10. Widen by account or OU. Record FIPS paths and anything left classical as exceptions with an owner and date.
  11. Keep the rollback (table below) with the change ticket; run the rollback test (How to test, step 7) once in a test environment.
  12. Clean up: remove the shim and its packages when a function reaches python3.12+.

Pitfalls and rollback

  • Silent fallback. Every failure mode still connects with X25519. Only a PQ-only test or a logged group proves the change.
  • One process, two answers. On Debian 12 or AL2023 without the config, requests and boto3 are classical while grpcio, awscrt, confluent-kafka and psycopg binary are PQ (Tested).
  • A version guard is not enough on AL2023. OpenSSL 3.5.8 there is classical until the config or policy changes.
  • Building from source changes the answer. psycopg[c], psycopg2 and mysqlclient link the system libraries. New Python versions can lag on wheels: on 3.15 pip picked confluent-kafka 4.4.0b1, which failed to import.
  • set_ecdh_curve removes ML-KEM, client or server, on every platform, and rejects X25519MLKEM768 (Tested). Remove the call; use set_groups on 3.15+.
  • set_groups replaces the whole list. set_groups("X25519MLKEM768") refuses classical peers (Tested). Keep X25519 (and secp256r1 if peers need it) unless you mean to enforce.
  • TLS 1.2 maximums block PQ: maximum_version, OP_NO_TLSv1_3, PROTOCOL_TLSv1_2, urllib3 ssl_maximum_version (Tested).
  • OPENSSL_CONF is process-wide and inherited by child processes. If it is already set, merge the Groups line; the layer will not override it. /dev/null turns PQ on but drops the crypto policy.
  • DEFAULT:PQ on a host changes every OpenSSL, GnuTLS and NSS program. Prefer the per-service systemd Environment= when only one service should change.
  • Legacy Lambda shim covers urllib3, requests and boto3 only, needs idna, must be built per Python version, adds about 18 MB unpacked, and is turned off for requests by truststore.inject_into_ssl() (all Tested).
  • gunicorn with an encrypted key looks healthy (Listening at) but every worker fails. Use the ssl_context hook (Tested).
  • pyOpenSSL has no load_pkcs12. Convert PKCS#12 with cryptography (Tested on 26.4.0).
  • Base-image moves reset the OS trust store, and certifi upgrades drop an appended CA. requests and httpx do not read the OS store by default (Tested).
  • FIPS paths (awscrt TLSv1_2_2025_07, a FIPS provider) are classical until proven otherwise.
  • Python 3.15 on Lambda is a public preview (announced 25 Aug 2026), outside the Lambda SLA (Lambda runtimes). Don't move production to it for set_groups alone.

Rollback (each is one change, no code):

Change Roll back Evidence
OPENSSL_CONF in Lambda, task definition, pod spec or systemd Remove the variable and redeploy or restart Tested (baseline cases)
pq-tls layer PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1, or remove AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER Tested (e2e py-lambda-py312-layer-disabled)
Legacy shim Remove PYTHONPATH=/var/task/pq-tls/pyshim, or set PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 Tested (baseline cases)
AL2023 DEFAULT:PQ policy update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT, then restart the services Documented
Base image moved to trixie or alpine Redeploy the previous image digest n/a
set_groups in code Set PQ_GROUPS to include X25519, or remove the call Tested

Test evidence

e2e suite, run r1117041043 (2026-11-16, LocalStack Pro 2026.10.6): 65 Python cases, all PASS: 59 client cases (Lambda 35, ECS on Fargate 13, ECS on EC2 3, EC2 8) and 6 load balancer target cases. PASS means the observed result matched the expectation in the case file; for a baseline case the expectation is "classical". Each client case connects to observer listeners: hybrid, PQ-only, mTLS, EC certificate, TLS 1.2-only, and three ML-DSA certificate listeners.

What the suite also showed:

  • The TLS 1.2-only listener stayed classical in every case: ML-KEM needs TLS 1.3.
  • ML-DSA certificates were accepted with DEFAULT:PQ, on trixie and alpine, and through the shim, but not with openssl-pq.cnf alone on AL2023 or Lambda: that file changes only Groups, so ML-DSA also needs SignatureAlgorithms (guide chapter: Certificates (ML-DSA)). The extension cases did not run the ML-DSA listeners.

Not covered by e2e: Lambda python3.15 (covered by the container tests below), EKS (ECS on EC2 uses the same images), real AWS, FIPS mode, and the library-level and case C to F matrices, which ran in containers instead.

The earlier tests/lambda/run-envonly.sh covers the same question through the Lambda emulator.

6.7 Node.js

Problem statement

Official Node.js 22.20+ and 24.5+ negotiate hybrid post-quantum key exchange (X25519MLKEM768) with no change. Four groups stay classical (X25519 or P-256 alone), so traffic recorded today can be decrypted once a large quantum computer exists ("harvest now, decrypt later"):

Group Why it is classical Fix
Lambda nodejs22.x, nodejs24.x (zip and AWS base image) The bootstrap sets OPENSSL_CONF to a file whose Groups omits ML-KEM Ship openssl-pq-node.cnf and point OPENSSL_CONF at it
Official Node 20 and older, 22.0–22.19, 24.0–24.4; Lambda nodejs18.x, nodejs20.x Bundled OpenSSL has no ML-KEM (3.0.x; Lambda 18 = 3.1.8, Lambda 20 = 3.2.6) Upgrade; no setting helps
Distro Node on Amazon Linux 2023 (dnf install nodejs20/22/24) Links the system OpenSSL 3.5.8, whose DEFAULT crypto policy omits ML-KEM DEFAULT:PQ policy, or OPENSSL_CONF = openssl-pq-node-system.cnf
Code that pins groups or caps TLS at 1.2 ecdhCurve / tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE without X25519MLKEM768, maxVersion: 'TLSv1.2', --tls-max-v1.2 Remove the setting, or put X25519MLKEM768 first

What decides, in order: the OpenSSL inside the node process (bundled in official builds, the system library in distro builds), the OpenSSL config file Node reads, then TLS options in code. The OS crypto policy matters only for distro-built Node. Node reads the nodejs_conf section of the file named by --openssl-config or OPENSSL_CONF (the flag wins); --openssl-shared-config makes it read openssl_conf instead; both flags are allowed in NODE_OPTIONS (Node CLI docs, Documented).

Certificates, keys, PFX files, CA bundles and client certificates take no part in the key-exchange choice: every existing-certificate, custom-trust and mutual-TLS setup tested kept working unchanged (cases C, D, E). Every client library tested (fetch, undici, axios, got, node-fetch, AWS SDK v3, grpc-js, pg, mysql2, ioredis, kafkajs) inherits Node's defaults.

Quick start by compute type

One change per workload. "Verify" means a PQ-only test server (openssl s_server -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3) accepts the client and a classical one still does; for AWS API calls, CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange shows X25519MLKEM768. pq-check.js and the full files are under Full configuration files and in configs/nodejs.

Lambda nodejs22.x and nodejs24.x (e2e: 31 zip cases on 22 and 24, 6 of them the main fix)

Step What to do
Check aws lambda get-function-configuration --function-name <fn> --query 'Environment.Variables.OPENSSL_CONF' returns nothing: classical (the bootstrap's file)
One change Package pq-tls/openssl-pq-node.cnf and set OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq-node.cnf. No code change: attach the pq-tls layer with AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper
Verify node pq-check.js <pq-only-host>:443 from the function reports "pq":true; CloudTrail shows X25519MLKEM768 for KMS, STS or Secrets Manager calls from the function's role
Roll back Remove OPENSSL_CONF (layer: set PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1), or shift the alias back to the previous version

Plan the move to nodejs24.x: nodejs22.x is deprecated on 2027-05-30 and blocked from updates on 2027-08-31 (Lambda runtimes, Documented).

Versions and native support

Tested 2026-11-14 to 2026-11-16 against an OpenSSL 3.5.7 PQ-only server (-groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3) and a classical-only server (-groups X25519), and in the e2e suite. "Yes" = the PQ-only server was accepted with no setting, and the classical server still worked.

Node 20 included: it links the system library Tested (e2e Fargate, ECS on EC2, EC2)

Alpine 3.22 apk add nodejs (22.23.5) System OpenSSL 3.5.9 n/a / Yes / n/a Yes not tested nothing Tested (alpine:3.22, e2e)
Deno 2.9.8 / Bun 1.4.4 rustls / BoringSSL n/a / Yes (client) / Yes not tested not tested nothing Tested (client)

The API to set groups is the same everywhere: the ecdhCurve option, tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE, or OpenSSL Groups via OPENSSL_CONF / --openssl-config. On OpenSSL 3.0 builds a PQ-first ecdhCurve throws ERR_CRYPTO_OPERATION_FAILED.

Version floor. Node's release notes put the OpenSSL 3.5 upgrade in 22.20.0 ("now bundles OpenSSL 3.5.2", v22.20.0) and 24.5.0 (v24.5.0), both Documented; 22.20.0 and 24.5.0 negotiated X25519MLKEM768 here and 22.19.0 and 24.4.1 did not (Tested). AWS's SDK guide names v22.20 and v24.9.0 (AWS SDK PQ TLS guide). Use 24.9+ as the policy floor to match AWS, but don't flag 24.5–24.8 as classical.

Lambda nodejs26.x (public preview). The preview base image (public.ecr.aws/lambda/nodejs:26, sha256:c805b481…, Node 26.13.1, OpenSSL 3.5.9, pulled 2026-11-16) no longer ships the classical /var/runtime/openssl_conf/openssl.cnf. Its bootstrap sources resolve-tls-profile.sh, which maps AWS_LAMBDA_TLS_PROFILE to a file under /var/runtime/tls-profiles/ and keeps Node's defaults when the variable is unset. Run in Docker with that script sourced, against PQ-only and classical servers (test and output):

AWS_LAMBDA_TLS_PROFILE Profile file PQ-only server Classical server
unset none (Node defaults) X25519MLKEM768 X25519
aws (and every other partition name) commercial-2025.conf (no ML-KEM in Groups) handshake failure X25519
commercial/2026 commercial-2026.conf (ML-KEM groups first, ML-DSA signatures) X25519MLKEM768 X25519
cnsa cnsa-1.0.conf (Groups = secp384r1) handshake failure handshake failure (test server offers X25519 only)
aws + OPENSSL_CONF=…/openssl-pq-node.cnf, or NODE_OPTIONS=--openssl-config=… yours (the script keeps a customer value) X25519MLKEM768 X25519

The profile map's comment says the sandbox sends the partition (aws) as AWS_LAMBDA_TLS_PROFILE, which would make a real nodejs26.x function classical. I'm not sure: no AWS account was available, and a preview can change before GA. Treat nodejs26.x like 22 and 24 (set OPENSSL_CONF) until a real function proves otherwise. Lambda lists nodejs26.x as preview, not for production (Lambda runtimes, Documented).

FIPS. Node CLI docs, --enable-fips: "a configured provider named fips must be available" (Documented). The OpenSSL 3.5.4 FIPS module was submitted for FIPS 140-3 validation in October 2025 (OpenSSL); I found no issued certificate, so a FIPS-validated ML-KEM path for Node is not confirmed.

Beyond compute type (Quick start covers the runtime):

  • Distro Node linked to a system OpenSSL 3.5 (AL2023 nodejs20, Debian 13 nodejs 20.19) is fixable without an upgrade. Decide by process.versions.openssl, not the Node major.
  • Code that sets ecdhCurve, tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE, maxVersion: 'TLSv1.2', secureProtocol: 'TLSv1_2_method' or --tls-max-v1.2: change it (case F), on every compute type.
  • Your own certificates, keys, PFX files, CA bundles and client certificates: no change (cases C, D, E). The one exception: legacy RC2/3DES PFX files fail on every current runtime, PQ or not; re-export them (case C).

Cases: what to change

Find your situation below. Unless a row says otherwise, "Tested" means four runtimes gave the same result: node:22 (22.23.5, node@sha256:e13726ca…), node:24 (24.22.3, node@sha256:9d266751…), and public.ecr.aws/lambda/nodejs:22 / :24 (22.23.5 / 24.22.3) with OPENSSL_CONF set to openssl-pq-node.cnf. The same Lambda images with AWS's own file (/var/runtime/openssl_conf/openssl.cnf) were the "before" baseline.

A. Simple client (default fetch, HTTP client, AWS SDK, DB driver)

Who this is: you call fetch, axios, got, node-fetch, the AWS SDK v3, a database or cache driver, and pass no TLS options. grep -rnE "ecdhCurve|DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE|maxVersion|secureProtocol|tls-max-v1" src/ finds nothing.

What happens today: PQ on official Node 22.20+/24.5+ in containers and EC2; classical on Lambda nodejs22/24 until the config is overridden; classical on official Node 20 and older (distro Node 20 on a system OpenSSL 3.5, such as AL2023 nodejs20, takes the config fix). Every tested library inherits Node's defaults (Tested).

What to change: nothing, except on Lambda (config file), AL2023 dnf Node (system fix) and old runtimes (upgrade): see Quick start.

Side effects: the ClientHello grows by about 1.2 KB (the ML-KEM key share). Classical-only servers still negotiate X25519 (Tested).

Client libraries against the same servers. Versions tested: undici 8.12.4, axios 1.21.1, got 16.2.0, node-fetch 3.3.2, @aws-sdk/client-s3 3.1169.0 with @smithy/node-http-handler 4.14.2, @grpc/grpc-js 1.14.7, pg 8.23.1, mysql2 3.24.6, ioredis 6.0.0, kafkajs 2.2.4. Images: node:20/22/24 slim and alpine, node:22.20-alpine, node:24.5-alpine.

B. Simple server (https, http2, Express, Fastify, grpc-js)

Who this is: https.createServer, http2.createSecureServer, tls.createServer, Express or Fastify over HTTPS, or a grpc-js server, configured with only a key and certificate.

What happens today: on 22.20+/24.5+ the server accepts X25519MLKEM768 and still serves classical and TLS 1.2 clients. Inside a Lambda function with AWS's config, a Node TLS server is classical too: PQ-only clients were refused on all servers in the case C matrix (Tested, baseline run).

What to change: nothing on 22.20+/24.5+; AL2023 dnf Node takes the system fix.

Tested with openssl s_client (OpenSSL 3.5.7) against each server on node:24-slim, node:22-alpine, node:24.5-alpine and node:20-slim:

Server PQ-only client Classical client Default OpenSSL 3.5 client gets Evidence
https.createServer({ key, cert }) X25519MLKEM768 X25519 X25519MLKEM768 Tested
same + ecdhCurve: 'X25519:P-256' handshake failure X25519 X25519 (silent downgrade) Tested
same + ecdhCurve: 'X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256:P-384' X25519MLKEM768 X25519 X25519MLKEM768 Tested

Behind a load balancer the client-facing handshake belongs to the load balancer (see Load balancers). With an NLB TCP (pass-through) listener, Node terminates TLS and the table above applies. On the target leg, with the load balancer modelled as the client (e2e r1117041043, Fargate and ECS on EC2): an https.createServer target on node:24-slim negotiated X25519MLKEM768 with no change; node:20-slim and unfixed AL2023 nodejs22 stayed classical; AL2023 nodejs20 with OPENSSL_CONF, and nodejs22 with OPENSSL_CONF or DEFAULT:PQ, gave X25519MLKEM768 (cases srv-node-*). Whether a real ALB offers ML-KEM to an HTTPS target was not checked on AWS. For nginx or Envoy in front of Node see Web servers and proxies; for mesh sidecars see Service mesh.

A Node server has no API for the negotiated group (How to test, step 3); probe it with s_client.

C. Existing server certificates, keys and PFX files

Who this is: your server loads its own certificate and key, from files, a mounted secret, Secrets Manager, or env vars that point at files. Find it with:

grep -rnE "pfx|passphrase|SNICallback|addContext|createSecureContext|(key|cert)[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*(fs\.)?readFileSync|\.(pem|key|crt|pfx|p12)['\"]" --include='*.js' --include='*.mjs' --include='*.cjs' --include='*.ts' src/

Node has no global key store: there is no equivalent of javax.net.ssl.keyStore. Certificates always reach tls.createSecureContext as PEM or PFX buffers, whether a framework reads them for you (Fastify https, NestJS httpsOptions) or your code does. So the tests below cover every source: they differ only in where the bytes come from.

What changes in the store: nothing. The key exchange group is chosen separately from the certificate. The same files served X25519MLKEM768 to a PQ client and X25519 to a classical client, with no edit.

What happens today (Tested): each row is one Node server, probed with openssl s_client 3.5.7 as a PQ-only client (-groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3), a classical client (-groups X25519:secp256r1) and a TLS 1.2 client. All four runtimes gave identical results; with AWS's Lambda file every PQ-only probe failed (handshake failure) and every classical probe passed.

Server setup (code excerpt) PQ-only client Classical / TLS 1.2 client Change needed
RSA 2048 PEM: { key, cert } X25519MLKEM768, RSA cert X25519, RSA cert none
EC P-256 PEM X25519MLKEM768, EC cert X25519, EC cert none
RSA + EC in one context: { key: [rsaKey, ecKey], cert: [rsaCert, ecCert] } X25519MLKEM768, EC cert by default; RSA cert to an RSA-only client (-sigalgs RSA-PSS+SHA256:RSA+SHA256) X25519, EC cert none
Encrypted PKCS#8 EC key: { key, cert, passphrase } X25519MLKEM768 X25519 none
Encrypted traditional RSA PEM (Proc-Type/DEK-Info) + passphrase X25519MLKEM768 X25519 none
PFX, RSA or EC, OpenSSL 3 default encryption (AES-256, PBKDF2): { pfx, passphrase } X25519MLKEM768 X25519 none
Legacy PFX (RC2-40 / 3DES, as exported by older Windows and Java tools) server fails to start: ERR_CRYPTO_UNSUPPORTED_OPERATION Unsupported PKCS12 PFX data same Re-export the PFX (below). Not caused by the PQ change: it fails the same with AWS's file and on Node 20
SNICallback picking RSA (a.test), EC P-256 (b.test), EC P-384 (c.test), RSA default right cert per name, X25519MLKEM768 right cert, X25519 none
server.addContext('b.test', { pfx, passphrase }) with an RSA default right cert per name, X25519MLKEM768 right cert, X25519 none
Express 5.3.0 on https.createServer (EC) X25519MLKEM768 X25519 none
Fastify 5.12.8 fastify({ https: { pfx, passphrase } }) X25519MLKEM768 X25519 none
http2.createSecureServer({ key, cert, allowHTTP1, SNICallback }) X25519MLKEM768 for every name X25519 none (see note) 
// Existing patterns that need no change (all Tested with X25519MLKEM768)
https.createServer({ key: fs.readFileSync(process.env.TLS_KEY), cert: fs.readFileSync(process.env.TLS_CERT) }, app);
https.createServer({ key: [rsaKey, ecKey], cert: [rsaCert, ecCert] }, app);           // RSA + EC
https.createServer({ key: encryptedPem, cert, passphrase: process.env.TLS_KEY_PASS }, app);
https.createServer({ pfx: fs.readFileSync('/etc/app/tls/server.pfx'), passphrase }, app);
const ctx = { 'a.test': tls.createSecureContext(rsa), 'b.test': tls.createSecureContext(ec) };
https.createServer({ ...rsa, SNICallback: (name, cb) => cb(null, ctx[name]) }, app);   // SNI

Legacy PFX fix (Tested: the converted file loads on all four runtimes; the original loads on official Node only with --openssl-legacy-provider, and not on the Lambda nodejs24 image even with that flag ("Unable to load legacy provider")):

openssl pkcs12 -in old.pfx -legacy -nodes -passin env:PFX_PASS -out tmp.pem
openssl pkcs12 -export -in tmp.pem -out new.pfx -passout env:PFX_PASS && shred -u tmp.pem

Note on http2 with SNICallback: on Node 22.23.5 the RSA context returned for a.test was not used and the EC default was served; Node 24.22.3 served the RSA cert. This was the same with and without the PQ config, so it is not PQ-related. Not investigated further.

Side effects: none observed. The passphrase, PFX, SNI and multi-certificate behaviour is identical before and after the change.

Proof: srv.cjs and run-cases.sh (Full configuration files); outputs out-node22.txt, out-node24.txt, out-lambda22-pq.txt, out-lambda24-pq.txt, baseline out-lambda2{2,4}-aws.txt, legacy PFX out-legacy-pfx.txt.

D. Custom CA trust (private CA, bundles, system store)

Who this is: your client trusts a private CA. Find it with:

grep -rnE "NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS|SSL_CERT_FILE|SSL_CERT_DIR|use-openssl-ca|use-system-ca|NODE_USE_SYSTEM_CA|setDefaultCACertificates|[^a-zA-Z]ca[[:space:]]*:" src/ Dockerfile* *.tf *.yaml *.json 2>/dev/null

What changes in the trust store: nothing. The PQ change touches only the key-exchange group list. The Lambda override file holds nothing but a Groups line, so it cannot change trust. Every trust method below verified the private-CA server and negotiated X25519MLKEM768.

What happens today (Tested): client connections to the PQ-only and classical servers, whose certificate chains to a private CA that is not in Node's bundled Mozilla list. Clients tried: tls.connect, https.get (default agent), global fetch, and an npm undici Agent.

Trust method node:22, node:24 Lambda 22/24 with openssl-pq-node.cnf Change needed
ca: fs.readFileSync('ca.pem') in tls.connect, https.Agent, undici connect PQ, verified PQ, verified none
NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS=/path/ca.pem (CA alone, or a bundle with several CAs) PQ, verified PQ, verified; also under the Runtime Interface Emulator with the real bootstrap none
--use-openssl-ca (flag or in NODE_OPTIONS) + SSL_CERT_FILE=bundle.pem PQ, verified PQ, verified none
--use-system-ca + SSL_CERT_FILE PQ, verified PQ, verified none
NODE_USE_SYSTEM_CA=1 + SSL_CERT_FILE PQ, verified PQ, verified none
CA added to the OS store (update-ca-certificates on Debian, update-ca-trust on AL2023), then --use-system-ca or --use-openssl-ca, no SSL_CERT_FILE PQ, verified (Debian store) fails UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE, before and after the PQ change Lambda only: also set SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt (see below)
tls.setDefaultCACertificates([...tls.getCACertificates('default'), pem]) at startup PQ, verified (tls and fetch) PQ, verified none
No private CA anywhere (negative test) fails verification, as expected fails verification n/a

The Lambda system-store finding: the Lambda Node binary links AWS's own shared OpenSSL, whose default certificate directory is a build path. Node prints Cannot open directory /local/p4clients/pkgbuild-const/workspace/src/Openssl/build/private/install/ssl/certs to load OpenSSL certificates. and tls.getCACertificates('system') returns 0 certificates. With SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt it returns 136 (Tested, nodejs24 image). This is independent of PQ. The real runtime may differ from the base image here; I'm not sure, as no AWS account was available.

Lambda bootstrap and NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS: the bootstrap keeps your value. It sets its own value (/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt) only when the variable is unset and the region name has three dashes (read from /var/runtime/bootstrap in both images). Under the emulator with the real bootstrap (Tested, out-lambda-rie.txt):

nodejs22 and nodejs24 PQ-only server, CA via NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS PQ-only server with mTLS Classical server
OPENSSL_CONF = PQ file + NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS OK, X25519MLKEM768 OK, X25519MLKEM768 OK, X25519
AWS default config + NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS handshake failure handshake failure OK, X25519
PQ file, no NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS (negative) UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE SELF_SIGNED_CERT_IN_CHAIN UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE

Flag availability (checked, not assumed): --use-system-ca, --use-openssl-ca and --use-bundled-ca all appear in node --help on 22.23.5 and 24.22.3, and --use-system-ca works on Linux on both. The docs say --use-system-ca was added in v23.8.0, with non-Windows/macOS support in v23.9.0, and list no v22 backport; NODE_USE_SYSTEM_CA=1 was added in v24.6.0 and v22.19.0 (Node CLI docs, Documented). So on 22.x, check that your exact version has the flag (node --help | grep use-system-ca). NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS is read only at process start, and is ignored when code passes ca: explicitly (Documented, same page).

// Existing trust patterns that need no change (Tested with X25519MLKEM768)
https.request({ host, ca: fs.readFileSync('/etc/app/ca/private-ca.pem') });   // ca option
new undici.Agent({ connect: { ca: privateCa } });                              // undici / fetch dispatcher
// NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS=/etc/app/ca/private-ca.pem node server.js               // env var
// SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/app/ca/bundle.pem node --use-openssl-ca server.js        // OpenSSL store
tls.setDefaultCACertificates([...tls.getCACertificates('default'), privateCaPem]);

Side effects: none from the PQ change. Note ca: replaces the default roots for that connection; that is existing Node behaviour, unchanged.

Proof: cli.cjs groups trust-none, trust-ca-option, trust-env, trust-api; run-cases.sh; run-lambda-rie.sh + fn/index.js; outputs as in case C plus out-lambda-rie.txt.

E. Mutual TLS (client certificates)

Who this is: your client sends a certificate (key/cert or pfx in tls.connect, https.Agent, undici connect, an AWS SDK NodeHttpHandler agent), or your server sets requestCert: true.

What happens today (Tested): against an OpenSSL PQ-only server that requires and verifies a client certificate (-Verify 1 -verify_return_error), and a classical one:

Client PQ-only server, cert required Classical server, cert required Without a cert
tls.connect({ key, cert, ca }), EC P-256 client cert X25519MLKEM768 X25519 ERR_SSL_TLSV13_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED
same with an RSA client cert X25519MLKEM768
encrypted PKCS#8 key + passphrase X25519MLKEM768
pfx + passphrase X25519MLKEM768
https.Agent({ key, cert, ca }) + https.get 200, X25519MLKEM768, server saw the cert
npm undici Agent({ connect: { key, cert, ca } }) with undici.request or undici.fetch 200
Global fetch with an npm undici 8.12.4 Agent as dispatcher fails UND_ERR_INVALID_ARG: invalid onRequestStart method Not PQ: Node bundles undici 6.29.1 (22) / 7.30.2 (24). Use fetch from the same undici package as the Agent
AWS SDK v3 S3Client with requestHandler: new NodeHttpHandler({ httpsAgent: new https.Agent({ key, cert, ca }) }) 200 (GetObject) ERR_SSL_TLSV13_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED

Server side: https.createServer({ key, cert, ca, requestCert: true, rejectUnauthorized: true }): a PQ client with a valid cert got X25519MLKEM768 and the app saw cn=client; a PQ client without a cert got alert certificate required; a classical client with a cert got X25519 (Tested).

Under the Lambda emulator, mTLS to the PQ-only server worked with the PQ file and failed with AWS's file (table in case D).

What to change: nothing in the certificates, keys or agents.

Side effects: TLS 1.3 behaviour to know: the client's secureConnect fires before the server checks the client cert, so a missing cert shows up as an error on the first read, not on connect (Tested: the no-cert tls.connect first reported success, then CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED). Health checks that only wait for secureConnect can report false success. This is TLS 1.3, not PQ.

// mTLS client patterns that need no change (Tested with X25519MLKEM768)
const mtls = { key: fs.readFileSync('client.key'), cert: fs.readFileSync('client.pem'), ca: privateCa };
https.get({ host, agent: new https.Agent(mtls) });
const { Agent, fetch } = require('undici');            // fetch and Agent from the same package
await fetch(url, { dispatcher: new Agent({ connect: mtls }) });
new S3Client({ requestHandler: new NodeHttpHandler({ httpsAgent: new https.Agent(mtls) }) });

F. Explicit TLS settings that block PQ

Who this is: code or config sets ecdhCurve, tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE, maxVersion, secureProtocol, tls.DEFAULT_MAX_VERSION, ciphers, or a --tls-* flag in NODE_OPTIONS. Find them with ci/check-node-tls.sh (below) or the grep in How to test.

What happens today (Tested, client and server, all four runtimes):

Setting Client → PQ-only server Client → classical server Server ← PQ-only client Change to
ecdhCurve: 'X25519:P-256' handshake failure X25519 handshake failure; every PQ client silently downgraded to X25519 remove, or 'X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256:P-384'
tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE = 'X25519:P-256' handshake failure (also global fetch) remove
ecdhCurve: 'auto' PQ X25519 PQ nothing (follows the OpenSSL config; on Lambda with AWS's file it stays classical)
ecdhCurve: 'X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256:P-384' PQ, even with AWS's Lambda file X25519 PQ recommended when a list is required
server ecdhCurve: 'X25519MLKEM768', minVersion: 'TLSv1.3' PQ only; classical and TLS 1.2 clients refused only for deliberate PQ-only enforcement
ciphers with only TLS 1.2 suites, or --tls-cipher-list=... PQ (TLS 1.3 suites are separate) X25519 PQ nothing
ciphers: 'TLS_AES_256_GCM_SHA384' PQ X25519 nothing
maxVersion: 'TLSv1.2', tls.DEFAULT_MAX_VERSION = 'TLSv1.2', --tls-max-v1.2 protocol version alert TLS 1.2, X25519 protocol version alert remove the cap, or maxVersion: 'TLSv1.3'
secureProtocol: 'TLSv1_2_method' protocol version alert TLS 1.2 protocol version alert replace with minVersion: 'TLSv1.2'
secureProtocol: 'TLS_method' PQ X25519 nothing
secureProtocol: 'TLSv1_3_method' throws ERR_TLS_INVALID_PROTOCOL_METHOD throws use minVersion: 'TLSv1.3'
minVersion: 'TLSv1.3', --tls-min-v1.3 PQ X25519 (server supports 1.3) nothing
sigalgs: 'ECDSA+SHA256:RSA-PSS+SHA256' PQ X25519 nothing (no effect on groups)

The common mistake, and the fix:

// BAD: pins classical groups. A PQ-only server rejects it; a hybrid server is downgraded to X25519.
const agent = new https.Agent({ ecdhCurve: 'X25519:P-256' });
// GOOD: omit ecdhCurve. If a policy forces a list, put the hybrid group first.
const agent = new https.Agent({ keepAlive: true }); // or ecdhCurve: 'X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256:P-384'

A PQ-first ecdhCurve string throws ERR_CRYPTO_OPERATION_FAILED: Failed to set ECDH curve on Node 20 and other OpenSSL 3.0 builds (Tested). Code that must run on both should use src/tls-pq.js (full file below): it feature-detects before adding the group.

tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE is documented as the server default (Node TLS docs). In these tests it also changed client connections: set to X25519:P-256 it broke PQ for tls.connect and https.get; set PQ-first it restored PQ on Lambda even with AWS's config file (Tested). There is no --tls-default-ecdh-curve flag (checked with node --help on 24.22.3).

G. FIPS mode

Who this is: you start Node with --enable-fips or --force-fips, call crypto.setFips(true), or point OPENSSL_CONF at a config that activates a FIPS provider.

What happens today: official Node builds include no FIPS provider; FIPS mode needs one you install and configure (Node CLI docs, Documented). Whether X25519MLKEM768 is negotiated under a FIPS provider was not tested here. A FIPS-validated OpenSSL 3.5 module with ML-KEM is not confirmed (submitted October 2025, OpenSSL).

What to change: if you already set OPENSSL_CONF for FIPS, don't replace it with the kit file (the layer leaves your value alone). Add the Groups line to a system_default section of your own file, then test with the PQ-only server. Unverified.

Side effects: for AWS endpoints in FIPS mode, see the AWS service endpoints chapter: the FIPS endpoints' PQ support is separate.

H. Libraries and agents with their own TLS stack

Component TLS stack PQ follows Node? Evidence
fetch/undici, axios, got, node-fetch, AWS SDK v3 (NodeHttpHandler), @grpc/grpc-js, pg, mysql2, ioredis, kafkajs node:tls yes Tested (case A table)
Native addons with their own OpenSSL or TLS: node-rdkafka (librdkafka), oracledb Thick mode (Oracle Client), aws-crt (AWS CRT, s2n-tls on Linux), older Prisma engines their own not necessarily Not tested. Find them with find node_modules -name '*.node' and probe each against the PQ-only server
Child processes: curl, git, wget from child_process the binary's library GnuTLS builds have no ML-KEM; OpenSSL-linked curl works Tested (GnuTLS builds on Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04; see Hosts and OS)
APM and tracing exporters (dd-trace, OpenTelemetry OTLP/HTTP) node:http(s) expected yes Unverified
Deno, Bun rustls / BoringSSL own defaults (PQ) Tested (client)

I. Certificate or key pinning, HSM and KMS-backed keys

Pinning: an SPKI SHA-256 pin checked in checkServerIdentity keeps working with X25519MLKEM768, and a wrong pin is still rejected (Tested, all four runtimes, pin.cjs). The key exchange doesn't change the server's certificate or key, so existing pins stay valid. Pins on ML-DSA certificates are a separate change (see Certificates (ML-DSA)).

HSM / PKCS#11 / KMS: the server's private key only signs the handshake; the ML-KEM key exchange is ephemeral and doesn't use it. Node can load keys through OpenSSL engines (privateKeyEngine, privateKeyIdentifier, clientCertEngine); engines are deprecated in OpenSSL 3 and this path was not tested. Node has no hook for a KMS-held TLS key: terminate TLS at an ALB/NLB with ACM instead (see Load balancers). Unverified for HSM-backed keys.

J. Other cases

  • HTTPS proxy (HTTPS_PROXY, CONNECT tunnel): the TLS session runs end to end from Node through the tunnel, so the PQ outcome is Node's. A TLS-inspecting proxy re-terminates and its own stack decides (see Network devices and egress). Not tested here.
  • HTTP/2 and gRPC servers: covered in B and C (Tested).
  • WebSockets (wss:// with ws): uses node:tls, expected to follow Node. Not tested.
  • Service mesh sidecar: the app speaks plaintext to the sidecar; the mesh decides (see Service mesh).
  • NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0 or rejectUnauthorized: false: doesn't affect PQ, but turns off the trust checks that cases D and E rely on. Remove it while you are in the code.

Solution and automation

  1. Inventory. List Lambda runtimes and overrides, report every Node process on EC2 with the SSM document, and scan repositories with the CI check (How to test, steps 5 and 6).
  2. Change, per compute type (below). Lambda zip and image need the config file; containers and EC2 on official Node need nothing.
  3. Guardrails in CI and the base-image pipeline.
  4. Canary rollout with a rollback switch (Rollout checklist).

Lambda zip (nodejs22.x, nodejs24.x)

The bootstrap sets OPENSSL_CONF=/var/runtime/openssl_conf/openssl.cnf when the variable is unset, and that file's Groups omits ML-KEM. Its own comment says "Customers can opt-in to PQC by setting OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null" (read from /var/runtime/bootstrap in public.ecr.aws/lambda/nodejs:22 and :24).

Method Setting Result Evidence
Ship the PQ config (recommended) OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq-node.cnf PQ; classical fallback OK Tested (e2e, RIE; 22 and 24)
Command-line flag NODE_OPTIONS=--openssl-config=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq-node.cnf PQ Tested (e2e, RIE)
pq-tls layer, no package change AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper PQ; leaves a function's own OPENSSL_CONF alone; chains an existing wrapper via PQ_TLS_NEXT_WRAPPER Tested (e2e)
AWS's opt-in OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null PQ (OpenSSL built-in defaults) Tested (e2e, RIE)
In code tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE = 'X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256:P-384' before the first connection PQ, even with AWS's file Tested (e2e, preload via NODE_OPTIONS=--require)
No change bootstrap default PQ-only server fails Tested (e2e, RIE)

Prefer the shipped file: it keeps AWS's structure, so review sees any future AWS setting, and check-lambda-node-conf.sh (below) catches drift. The main change:

function.zip
├── index.mjs
├── node_modules/...
└── pq-tls/openssl-pq-node.cnf
# SAM / CloudFormation (Terraform: environment { variables = { OPENSSL_CONF = "..." } })
Environment:
  Variables:
    OPENSSL_CONF: /var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq-node.cnf

nodejs18.x and nodejs20.x have no in-process fix: change Runtime. For calls that must be PQ before then, the pq-egress extension makes the outbound TLS connection with X25519MLKEM768; the function sends plain HTTP to http://127.0.0.1:2779/<route>/<path> (Tested in e2e on 18, 20, 22 and 24; not tested for AWS SDK calls, which sign the host name). See Solutions by compute type.

Lambda container image

On public.ecr.aws/lambda/nodejs:22 or :24, copy the file and set ENV OPENSSL_CONF; the bootstrap keeps a value that is already set (Tested, e2e). Full Dockerfile.lambda below.

Fargate, ECS, EKS

Official node:22, node:24 and node:26 images (Debian or Alpine) need nothing: they set no OPENSSL_CONF and bundle OpenSSL 3.5 (Tested). Pin the image by digest and keep it at 22.20+/24.9+.

For an AL2023 image with dnf install nodejs20/22/24, run update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ in the Dockerfile (AL2023 PQ guide, Documented; Tested here), or set OPENSSL_CONF to openssl-pq-node-system.cnf in the task definition or pod spec. Keep NODE_OPTIONS free of --tls-max-v1.2.

EC2

Official Node 22.20+/24.9+ binaries are PQ by default; the crypto policy is not involved. With AL2023's nodejs20/22/24 packages, set the host policy (update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ, then restart services) or add Environment=OPENSSL_CONF=... to the unit. Debian 13 packages are PQ by default. Host-wide policy is on Hosts and OS.

Guardrails

ci/check-node-tls.sh fails the build on classical ecdhCurve / DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE lists, TLS 1.2 caps, nodejs18.x/nodejs20.x, OPENSSL_CONF pointing at AWS's file, and node base images below 22.20 / 24.5, and warns on node_modules that set these options. Tested against fixtures: 9 findings in the bad set, none in the good set (ci-check.out). A reviewed exception carries pq-tls-ok on the same line. Also add RUN node pq-check.js in Dockerfiles, and check-lambda-node-conf.sh in the pipeline that bumps the Lambda base image.

- name: PQ TLS guardrail (Node.js)
  run: bash ci/check-node-tls.sh .

How to test

  1. Posture: node -p "process.version + ' ' + process.versions.openssl" must show 22.20+/24.5+ and 3.5+ (or a distro build linked to 3.5+). On Lambda also check OPENSSL_CONF and NODE_OPTIONS. pq-check.js prints these and whether the runtime can parse X25519MLKEM768. Capable is not enabled: Lambda with AWS's file reports capable and still sends a classical list.
  2. Live test (client): node pq-check.js pq-only.example.internal:443 classical.example.internal:443. The PQ-only target must report "pq":true; the classical one must connect and report "pq":false (the script then exits 1 by design).
  3. Live test (server): openssl s_client -connect host:443 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 -brief must print Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768; -groups X25519 must still connect. A Node server has no API for the negotiated group (getEphemeralKeyInfo() returns null on server sockets, Node TLS docs).
  4. Observed signal: in a client, socket.getEphemeralKeyInfo() returns {"type":"TLSGroup","name":"X25519MLKEM768"} on Node 24 and 26, {} on Node 22 (TLS 1.3 with no EC key, here the hybrid group), and {"type":"ECDH","name":"X25519",...} when classical (Tested, also in every e2e client case). For AWS calls, CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange: see AWS service endpoints.
  5. Code and dependency scan: grep -rnE "ecdhCurve|DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE|maxVersion|secureProtocol|TLSv1_2_method|tls-max-v1\.2" --include='*.js' --include='*.mjs' --include='*.cjs' --include='*.ts' ., including node_modules.
  6. Fleet inventory: aws lambda list-functions --query "Functions[?starts_with(Runtime,'nodejs')].[FunctionName,Runtime,Environment.Variables.OPENSSL_CONF]" --output text. For EC2, the pq-node-posture.json SSM document. Blocking old runtimes is on Enforcement and guardrails.

Case tests (scripts in Full configuration files; Docker on a laptop or CI runner, no AWS account):

  1. bash gen-certs.sh inside an OpenSSL 3.5 container: private CA, RSA/EC server certs, encrypted keys, PFX files, client certs. Swap in copies of your own files to test your real store.
  2. bash start-ossl.sh: PQ-only 4431, classical 4432, PQ-only with client cert required 4433, classical with client cert 4434.
  3. bash run-cases.sh node24 node@sha256:<digest> -, and for Lambda bash run-cases.sh lambda24-pq public.ecr.aws/lambda/nodejs@sha256:<digest> /k/openssl-pq-node.cnf. Expect every PQ-only probe X25519MLKEM768, every classical probe X25519, the case F blockers to fail and the legacy PFX to fail.
  4. Your own server (case C): probe with openssl s_client ... -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 -brief, then -groups X25519, then -tls1_2. Add -servername <name> for SNI and -sigalgs RSA-PSS+SHA256:RSA+SHA256 to see the RSA cert of an RSA+EC store.
  5. Your trust setup (case D): node cli.cjs trust-env with your NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS / SSL_CERT_FILE / flags; trust-none must fail.
  6. Your client cert (case E): node cli.cjs mtls; the no-cert rows must fail with CERTIFICATE_REQUIRED.
  7. Lambda with the real bootstrap: bash run-lambda-rie.sh (Runtime Interface Emulator, host port 18661+).
  8. Rollback: rerun step 3 or 7 without OPENSSL_CONF. PQ-only targets must fail and classical ones pass.

Results, 2026-11-14: about 170 checks on each of 6 runtime setups (node:22, node:24, Lambda 22 and 24 with and without the PQ file), 6 emulator invocations, 8 pinning checks, 4 PFX checks and the CI fixtures. All matched except three findings not caused by PQ: legacy PFX, global fetch with an npm undici 8 dispatcher, and the Lambda image's empty system store.

Rollout checklist

# Step Done when
1 Inventory: Lambda functions by runtime and OPENSSL_CONF; container images by Node version and OpenSSL; EC2 services by binary (official or dnf) Every Node workload has a row and a fix from Quick start
2 Move nodejs18.x and nodejs20.x to nodejs24.x first (update block 2027-08-31); pq-egress only where a call must be PQ sooner No 18/20 functions, or a recorded exception
3 Run ci/check-node-tls.sh in warn mode on every repo; fix pinned ecdhCurve, DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE and TLS 1.2 caps No findings, or pq-tls-ok with a reviewer
4 Add openssl-pq-node.cnf to the shared Lambda build template; add check-lambda-node-conf.sh to the base-image bump pipeline Template release published
5 Re-export legacy RC2/3DES PFX files (case C); on Lambda images set SSL_CERT_FILE if you rely on the OS store (case D) run-cases.sh passes with your files
6 Canary: Lambda, publish a version and shift 10% with alias weighted routing (Lambda alias routing, Documented); ECS, deployment circuit breaker with rollback; EKS, rolling update with a readiness probe; EC2, one instance per Auto Scaling group Errors and latency flat for the canary period
7 Verify on the canary: CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for KMS, STS, Secrets Manager calls from its role; pq-check.js for third-party targets X25519MLKEM768 observed
8 Roll out to 100%, then the launch template or base image All workloads on the fix
9 Turn the CI check to fail mode and add the runtime guardrail (Enforcement and guardrails) New code can't regress

Pitfalls and rollback

Pitfall Effect Fix Evidence
Classical ecdhCurve on a client PQ-only server rejects; hybrid servers downgrade to X25519 remove it, or put X25519MLKEM768 first Tested
Classical ecdhCurve on a server every client silently gets X25519 same Tested
PQ-first ecdhCurve on Node 20 / OpenSSL 3.0 throws at startup; a server process crashes upgrade, or feature-detect (tls-pq.js) Tested
tls.DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE set to a classical list clients and servers lose PQ, even with the PQ file set remove it Tested (e2e node-lambda-n24-code-ecdh-classical)
--tls-max-v1.2, maxVersion: 'TLSv1.2', secureProtocol: 'TLSv1_2_method' no TLS 1.3, so no ML-KEM remove the cap Tested (e2e -tls-max-12 on Lambda and Fargate)
Lambda bootstrap config nodejs22/24 stay classical ship openssl-pq-node.cnf Tested
Lambda nodejs26.x preview with AWS_LAMBDA_TLS_PROFILE=aws commercial-2025 profile, classical set OPENSSL_CONF as for 22/24 Tested (Docker, base image); real sandbox value not sure
Function already sets OPENSSL_CONF (FIPS, custom engine) the layer leaves it alone, so the function stays classical merge the Groups line into their file Tested (e2e node-lambda-n24-layer-ownconf); FIPS config not tested

"Node 20 is never PQ" wrong for Debian 13's 20.19.2 (OpenSSL 3.5.7) and AL2023 nodejs20 20.20.2 (3.5.8) once fixed check process.versions.openssl, not the major Tested

Lambda runtime pinned in Manual update mode could stay below 22.20 check the runtime version ARN Unverified
Larger ClientHello (about 1.2 KB more key share) some middleboxes drop it live test through the real network path not observed here
Legacy PFX (RC2-40/3DES) Unsupported PKCS12 PFX data; the Lambda nodejs24 image can't load the legacy provider re-export (case C) Tested (e2e on Lambda 20, 22, 24 and Fargate)
Global fetch with a dispatcher from a different undici major UND_ERR_INVALID_ARG: invalid onRequestStart method import fetch and Agent from the same undici package Tested (undici 8.12.4 on Node 22/24)
Lambda image: CA in the OS store, --use-system-ca/--use-openssl-ca without SSL_CERT_FILE no system CAs load; private CA not trusted SSL_CERT_FILE=/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt, or NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS Tested (base image; real runtime not checked)
TLS 1.3 mTLS without a client cert secureConnect fires, error arrives on first read check the first response, not just the connect Tested
secureProtocol: 'TLSv1_3_method' throws ERR_TLS_INVALID_PROTOCOL_METHOD minVersion: 'TLSv1.3' Tested
Node TLS server inside a Lambda function AWS's file governs it too: PQ-only clients refused same OPENSSL_CONF fix Tested

Rollback is a config change each time: remove OPENSSL_CONF or NODE_OPTIONS from the function, or set PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 with the layer still attached (Tested, e2e node-lambda-n24-layer-disabled: back to classical); remove the ENV line from a Lambda image; redeploy the previous image digest for containers; update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT on AL2023. On a server, TLS_ECDH_CURVE=X25519:P-256:P-384 in server.js (below) is a temporary classical-only switch.

Test evidence

The LocalStack end-to-end suite (run r1117041043, 2026-11-16) deploys each Node.js workload into LocalStack Pro, on AWS's own Lambda images (public.ecr.aws/lambda/nodejs), official node images and AL2023 2023.12.20261109, and grades what it negotiates against a TLS observer. 100 Node.js cases, 100 pass: 93 client cases and 7 load balancer target cases. "Pass" means the behaviour matched this chapter, which for a baseline is "classical". Each client case runs eight targets: hybrid, PQ-only, mTLS, EC certificate, TLS 1.2, and three ML-DSA targets.

Compute Cases What it proved
Lambda zip, nodejs22.x / nodejs24.x 31 Baseline classical. Fixed by the bundled file, --openssl-config, the layer, /dev/null and a PQ-first DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE. Layer leaves a function's own config alone, chains an existing wrapper, rolls back with PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1. Pitfalls reproduced: classical DEFAULT_ECDH_CURVE, --tls-max-v1.2. NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS, global fetch and modern PFX work with the fix; legacy PFX fails mTLS only
Lambda zip, nodejs18.x / nodejs20.x 13 Classical with every in-process method (file, flag, layer), startup unaffected; the pq-egress extension (alone or in the combined layer) gives X25519MLKEM768 on the hybrid, PQ-only, mTLS and EC-certificate targets
Lambda container image, nodejs 22 / 24 4 Base image classical; COPY + ENV OPENSSL_CONF gives PQ
ECS on Fargate 28 client, 6 target Official images: 20 and 22.19 / 24.4 classical, 22.20 / 24.5 / 26 PQ (slim and alpine). Debian 13 and Alpine 3.22 distro Node PQ. AL2023 dnf nodejs20/22/24: classical, PQ with OPENSSL_CONF or DEFAULT:PQ. Target leg behind a PQ ALB policy: PQ for node:24-slim and fixed AL2023 servers, classical for node:20-slim and unfixed AL2023
ECS on EC2 5 client, 1 target Same results as Fargate for node:24-slim, node:20-slim and AL2023 nodejs22/24
EC2 12 AL2023 dnf nodejs20/22/24: classical, PQ with a per-service OPENSSL_CONF or host DEFAULT:PQ. Official 22 and 24 binaries PQ; host DEFAULT:PQ does not change them

ML-DSA (separate track). Every client on OpenSSL 3.5 with TLS 1.3 allowed (official 22.20+/24.5+/26, Debian 13, Alpine 3.22) accepted an ML-DSA server certificate, an ML-DSA-only server and ML-DSA mutual TLS, including Lambda 22/24 with AWS's classical file (that file restricts groups, not signatures). AL2023 dnf Node does so only after DEFAULT:PQ or the system file. Unfixed AL2023 Node, Node 20 / 22.19 / 24.4 and --tls-max-v1.2 get ECDSA from a dual-certificate server and fail ML-DSA-only. The pq-egress cases did not run the ML-DSA targets.

Not tested: real AWS (LocalStack and Docker on arm64 only); EKS; Lambda nodejs26.x in the suite (Docker only, above); FIPS providers; pq-egress for AWS SDK calls; native addons with their own TLS (case H).

Code and results: e2e/cases/node.py (client cases and expectations), e2e/cases/server.py (target cases), e2e/clients/node/index.js (probe), e2e/build/node.sh (packages and images, built from configs/nodejs/), e2e/lambda/extension/pq-egress, e2e/results/latest/summary.md. Earlier case tests (C to I, client libraries, servers, emulator) with outputs: tests/deep/node and tests/deep/node/v2. The per-case list is in Appendix A.
