6.8 Java

Problem statement

Java services on JDK 8 to 21 negotiate classical key exchange only (X25519 or P-256), so traffic recorded today can be decrypted later by a quantum computer. That covers outbound calls to AWS APIs, databases and partners, and any TLS the JVM terminates itself.

What decides PQ is the JVM's TLS provider (JSSE), not the OS. Apache HttpClient, OkHttp, the JDK HttpClient, HttpsURLConnection, JDBC drivers, Kafka, Tomcat, Jetty and Spring Boot all hand TLS to JSSE, so the base image's OpenSSL and crypto policy make no difference to them.

JDK PQ by default? Fix
25.0.5 and later, 27 Yes, client and server, X25519MLKEM768 first (Tested on Corretto and Temurin 25.0.5). SecP256r1MLKEM768 and SecP384r1MLKEM1024 are also supported (Documented, Oracle 25.0.5 release notes, JDK-8314323) Nothing
25.0.0 to 25.0.4 No (Documented) Patch to 25.0.5+
8, 11, 17, 21 No: SunJSSE has no ML-KEM group Kit v2: Bouncy Castle JSSE (BCJSSE) 1.87 as the TLS provider, loaded by pq-tls-agent.jar; or move to 25.0.5+

Why other pages say "JDK 27": JEP 527 (hybrid ML-KEM key exchange in TLS 1.3) shipped in JDK 27 (released 2026-09-15), and the AWS SDK guide names only JDK 27 (AWS SDKs and Tools guide). The October 2026 update 25.0.5 carries the same feature as a backport (Documented). Oracle plans PQ TLS for 21 and 17, then 11 and 8 (Oracle Java blog, 2026-08); secondary reports put 21 and 17 in H1 2027 and 11 and 8 in H2 2027. Claimed, not shipped; we could not open Oracle's own table, so the dates are not sure. Remove the kit when your vendor ships it.

Three stacks bypass JSSE, so the JDK version does not matter for them:

  • Netty with netty-tcnative-boringssl-static (SslProvider.OPENSSL; gRPC-Java, Reactor Netty or Vert.x with native SSL): BoringSSL decides. PQ by default (Tested 2.0.85 on JDK 17 and 21).
  • AWS SDK for Java v2 CRT client (AwsCrtHttpClient): s2n-tls on Linux decides. PQ by default from SDK 2.35.11 (Documented, AWS SDKs and Tools guide; Tested 2.59.1 PQ, 2.35.10 classical). On macOS and Windows it uses the OS TLS library.
  • Tomcat with the OpenSSL implementation (Tomcat Native or the FFM connector): the linked OpenSSL decides (Tomcat 11.0.29 on OpenSSL 3.5.5 Tested PQ in the Web servers and proxies chapter).

Certificates and stores do not change. JKS and PKCS#12 keystores, cacerts, javax.net.ssl.trustStore and mutual TLS keep working. Under BCJSSE one security property matters: ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX, which kit v2 sets. Without it BCJSSE cannot use EC certificates, as a server or as an mTLS client (Tested).

Quick start by compute type

Kit v2 = the pq-tls/ directory (pq-tls-agent.jar v2, pq-tls.security, three Bouncy Castle 1.87 jars) plus JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS with both flags. Source: kit/java-v2. Below, KIT stands for -Djava.security.properties=<dir>/pq-tls/pq-tls.security -javaagent:<dir>/pq-tls/pq-tls-agent.jar, where <dir> is /var/task on Lambda and /opt elsewhere. Append KIT to an existing JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS; don't replace it (APM agents live there).

Java 8 adds two flags. -Dorg.bouncycastle.jsse.client.assumeOriginalHostName=true everywhere: without it every HttpsURLConnection handshake fails under BCJSSE. -Djdk.tls.client.protocols=TLSv1.3,TLSv1.2 on Lambda only: the Lambda Java 8 runtime limits clients to TLS 1.2. The pq-tls layer adds both (Tested).

Clients

Compute Check One change Verify Roll back
Lambda java8.al2, java11, java17, java21 (zip) Runtime in the function configuration pq-tls/ at the zip root and JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=KIT (Java 8: plus both Java 8 flags). Or the pq-tls layer with AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper Log line pq-tls agent v2: BCJSSE first, groups=X25519MLKEM768,... ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX; CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange on the role's KMS or STS calls Delete the variable, or set PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 with the layer; the next cold start is classical

ECS on EC2, EKS As for Fargate As for Fargate; EKS sets the variable in spec.containers[].env As for Fargate, plus Zeek on mirrored node ENIs As for Fargate

Or kit v2 in /opt/pq-tls/ and a systemd drop-in Environment="JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=KIT" journalctl -u <service> | grep 'pq-tls agent v2'; CloudTrail; Zeek Remove the drop-in, systemctl daemon-reload, restart

Notes, all Tested in e2e unless marked:

  • Lambda zip, static java.security registration does not work (the bootstrap class path is /var/runtime/lib only); use the agent.
  • Lambda container image with JDK 8: the bundled kit needs both Java 8 flags. Tested only with the layer's files in /opt plus the wrapper; the bundled form with both flags is Unverified.
  • Containers, JDK 17 and 21, without an agent: bc-static.security plus the BC jars on -cp also gives PQ (Tested, Fargate and ECS on EC2).
  • EC2 with several Corretto packages: on AL2023, java on PATH resolved to 25 once 25 was installed (alternatives picks the highest). Check which binary the unit starts.
  • JDK 8 on ECS on EC2 and on EC2 with the full fix were not run; Fargate JDK 8 was.

Servers (the JVM terminates TLS)

If an ALB, NLB TLS listener, API Gateway or CloudFront terminates TLS in front of the JVM, set the PQ policy there; the JVM's own TLS matters for the target leg (ALB re-encryption), NLB TCP pass-through and internal services. See the Load balancers chapter.

Server situation One change Verify Evidence
JDK 25.0.5+, any certificate Nothing. One keystore with several certificates for SNI: also add pq-tls.security (PKIX), or the wrong certificate is served openssl s_client -connect <host>:8443 -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -brief prints Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768; -groups X25519 still connects Tested
JDK 8 to 21, RSA certificate Kit v2 (Java 8: plus the host-name flag) As above Tested (8, 17, 21 in e2e)
JDK 8 to 21, EC certificate Kit v2, which sets ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX. Remove any hard-coded SunX509 (code or Tomcat keyManagerAlgorithm) or every handshake fails As above, plus -servername for each SNI name Tested 17, 21; JDK 8 and 11 Unverified
Any JDK, Netty with tcnative Nothing As above Tested 21

By JDK and compute type

PQ = connected to a PQ-only server; every PQ cell also connected to a classical-only server, so fallback works. Evidence is the LocalStack e2e run r1117041043 (2026-11-16) unless marked; see Test evidence below.

JDK Lambda zip Lambda image Fargate ECS on EC2 / EKS EC2
25.0.5 PQ, runtime java25 PQ, lambda/java:25 PQ, amazoncorretto:25; AL2023 dnf Corretto 25 PQ, amazoncorretto:25 PQ, AL2023 Corretto 25 package
21.0.13 Classical; kit or layer gives PQ; static registration does not Classical; kit gives PQ Classical; kit or static gives PQ (Corretto, Temurin, AL2023 dnf) Classical; kit gives PQ Classical; kit gives PQ (AL2023)
17.0.21 Classical; kit or layer gives PQ Classical; kit gives PQ Classical; kit or static gives PQ Static gives PQ; kit not run Classical; kit gives PQ (AL2, AL2023)
11.0.33 Classical; kit or layer gives PQ Classical; kit gives PQ Classical; kit gives PQ Not run Classical; kit gives PQ (AL2, AL2023)
8u512 TLS 1.2 classical; kit + both Java 8 flags, or the layer, gives PQ TLS 1.2 classical; layer files + wrapper give PQ TLS 1.3 classical; kit + host-name flag gives PQ (protocols flag not needed) Kit without the host-name flag fails every handshake; full fix not run Not run

The pq-egress Lambda extension also gave PQ on java8.al2 to java25 (it originates the TLS itself).

Decision guide

  • JDK 25.0.5 or later: change nothing; look for pins (case F).
  • JDK 8 to 21: roll out kit v2 and plan the upgrade to 25.
  • Already shipped kit v1 (agent only)? Add the pq-tls.security flag or swap in the v2 jar before any JVM with an EC certificate, server or mTLS client, gets it.
  • Netty tcnative or the CRT client: those connections are already PQ; everything else in the JVM still needs the kit.
  • FIPS JVM: don't add the kit (case G).

Cases: what to change

Deep tests (marked "Tested" in this section unless an e2e case is named): 2026-11-14, containers on arm64, eclipse-temurin:17-jre 17.0.21, :21-jre 21.0.13, :25-jre 25.0.5, BC 1.87, peers OpenSSL 3.5.7 on debian:trixie-slim. "PQ" means OpenSSL printed Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768; every PQ row also connected with a classical-only peer unless it says PQ-only. Outputs: tests/deep/java/out/ and tests/deep/java/v2/out/.

A. Simple client (default HTTP client, AWS SDK, JDBC driver, no TLS code)

Who: no SSLContext, KeyManager or TrustManager code (grep -rE 'SSLContext|KeyManagerFactory|TrustManagerFactory|namedGroups' src/ finds nothing). Today: classical on 8 to 21, PQ on 25.0.5+. Change: nothing in code; JDK 25.0.5+ or kit v2. Side effects: the ClientHello grows by about 1.2 KB; BCJSSE becomes the TLS provider for the whole JVM.

Client TLS engine JDK 17 / 21 17 / 21 + kit 25.0.5 What to keep Evidence

Apache HttpClient 5.6.5, 4.5.14 JSSE No PQ PQ TLS 1.3 in setTlsVersions; no new String[]{"TLSv1.2"} Tested
OkHttp 4.12.0 JSSE No PQ PQ ConnectionSpec.MODERN_TLS Tested
Netty 4.2.20, SslProvider.JDK JSSE No PQ PQ n/a Tested

AWS SDK 2.59.1 ApacheHttpClient JSSE No PQ PQ n/a Tested
AWS SDK 2.59.1 NettyNioAsyncHttpClient with tcnative BoringSSL PQ PQ PQ Without tcnative it uses JSSE (not run) Tested

Unverified rows use standard JSSE APIs, so they should follow the JDK rows; run the live test in "How to test" for your build before you rely on them. Server settings for databases, Redis and Kafka brokers are in the Databases and data services chapter.

B. Simple server (framework default TLS)

Who: Spring Boot server.ssl.* or an SSL bundle, Tomcat server.xml, Jetty, HttpsServer, Quarkus or Micronaut terminating TLS in the JVM. Today: classical on 8 to 21, PQ on 25.0.5+. Change: JDK 25.0.5+, or kit v2 (kit v1 breaks EC certificates, case C). Side effects: clients that don't offer ML-KEM still connect (Tested).

Server JDK 21 default JDK 17 / 21 + kit v2 JDK 25.0.5 default Evidence
Tomcat 11.0.29 embedded (JSSE) Classical PQ with RSA or EC certificate; EC fails without PKIX PQ, preferred Tested
Tomcat 11.0.29 groups="X25519MLKEM768,x25519" Classical (name unknown to SunJSSE 21, ignored) n/a PQ Tested
Tomcat 11.0.29 groups="x25519,secp256r1" n/a n/a Classical (the pin removes it) Tested
Jetty 12.1.15 Classical PQ; EC fails without PKIX PQ Tested
JDK HttpsServer Classical PQ; EC fails without PKIX PQ Tested; e2e target leg (RSA) on 8, 17, 21, 25
Spring Boot 3.5.16 SSL bundle, EC PKCS#12 Not run PQ on 21; fails with the agent's PKIX step switched off Not run Tested (21 only)
Netty 4.2.20 + tcnative-boringssl-static 2.0.85 PQ n/a PQ Tested
Netty 4.2.20, JDK provider Classical n/a PQ Tested on 25
Undertow; Quarkus; Micronaut Follow the JSSE rows (JDK engine) or the tcnative row (native SSL) Unverified; confirm the engine from the startup log

Order of preference for a JVM that terminates TLS: (1) JDK 25.0.5+; (2) Netty with tcnative on any JDK; (3) JDK 8 to 21 with kit v2; (4) otherwise a PQ-capable proxy in front (nginx or Envoy on OpenSSL 3.5, Web servers and proxies chapter) or an ALB with a PQ policy.

Group settings by framework:

  • Spring Boot has no named-group property (checked against the 4.1.2 SSL reference); keep enabled-protocols: [TLSv1.3, TLSv1.2] in the bundle. To reorder groups, set -Djdk.tls.namedGroups=X25519MLKEM768,x25519,secp256r1,secp384r1; embedded Tomcat's groups defaults to it. The TomcatConnectorCustomizer in the full files is Unverified.
  • Standalone Tomcat: SSLHostConfig groups arrived in 11.0.12 (Tomcat 11 HTTP connector; Documented) and, per a mirrored changelog, 10.1.48; the 9.0 line is not sure. It defaults to jdk.tls.namedGroups and picks only among groups the engine knows. With the OpenSSL implementation it should pass to OpenSSL (not tested). On JDK 17 and 21, bin/setenv.sh adds kit v2 through CATALINA_OPTS.
  • Jetty, Undertow, HttpsServer: no group setting; they follow the JDK and jdk.tls.namedGroups.
  • Netty server (gRPC-Java, Reactor Netty, Vert.x): SslContextBuilder.forServer(kmf).sslProvider(SslProvider.OPENSSL) with tcnative on the class path. When jdk.tls.namedGroups is set, Netty uses that list for BoringSSL too, so a classical pin turns PQ off even with tcnative (Tested). Full code: NettyPq.java.

Behind an ALB or NLB TLS listener (e2e): with the load balancer modelled as the client on the target leg, an HttpsServer target with an RSA PKCS#12 keystore stayed classical on Corretto 8 and 21, negotiated X25519MLKEM768 with kit v2 on Corretto 8, 17 and 21 (Fargate; 21 also on ECS on EC2) and with no change on Corretto 25.0.5. With the ALB's classic backend policy every target negotiated classical TLS 1.2.

C. Existing server certificates in a custom keystore or cert files

Who: the JVM loads its own certificate and key. Find it:

grep -rnE 'javax\.net\.ssl\.keyStore|KeyManagerFactory|KeyStore\.getInstance|certificateKeystore|server\.ssl\.(key-store|bundle)|spring\.ssl\.bundle|keyManagerAlgorithm|setKeyStorePath' \
  src/ conf/ *.yml *.yaml *.properties Dockerfile* helm/ 2>/dev/null
keytool -list -v -keystore server.p12 -storetype PKCS12 | grep -E 'Alias name|Entry type|Signature algorithm|Subject Public Key Algorithm'

What changes in the store: nothing. Keep the RSA or EC key, PKCS#12 or JKS, aliases, passwords and the system properties that point at the store.

What to change (JDK 8 to 21 only): ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX, shipped in pq-tls.security and also set by agent v2 when the value is still the JDK default SunX509. The agent fallback covers apps whose own -Djava.security.properties replaces the kit's flag (Tested). Code that names SunX509 must change:

// Fails with an EC certificate under BCJSSE (JDK 8-21 + kit):
KeyManagerFactory kmf = KeyManagerFactory.getInstance("SunX509");
// Works everywhere, and picks up the kit's PKIX default:
KeyManagerFactory kmf = KeyManagerFactory.getInstance(KeyManagerFactory.getDefaultAlgorithm());
// Tomcat server.xml: drop keyManagerAlgorithm="SunX509", or set keyManagerAlgorithm="PKIX"

Why: BCJSSE asks the key manager for key type EC/secp256r1, and SunJSSE's SunX509 key manager returns no alias for it. RSA keys never needed the fix (BC asks for RSA, which SunX509 answers).

Tested proof (tests/deep/java/v2/out/t17-v2kit.txt, t21-v2kit.txt, t17-v1nofix.txt, t21-v1nofix.txt, t25-none.txt, t25-file.txt, tomcat-sb.txt):

Keystore situation JDK 17/21 + kit v1 JDK 17/21 + kit v2 JDK 25.0.5, no kit
EC P-256 only, PKCS#12 Fails (handshake_failure, also for classical clients) PQ; classical client connects PQ
RSA only, PKCS#12 PQ PQ PQ
RSA + EC in one PKCS#12 PQ, always serves RSA PQ, serves EC; a client offering only RSA signature algorithms gets RSA PQ, serves EC
Two EC certificates, SNI per host name Fails PQ, right certificate per SNI name PQ, but SNI ignored (served alt-host's certificate for ks-java); with pq-tls.security: right certificate per name
EC in a legacy JKS Fails PQ PQ
Store from -Djavax.net.ssl.keyStore* Fails PQ PQ
App passes its own -Djava.security.properties Fails PQ (agent v2 fallback) PQ
Code hard-codes KeyManagerFactory.getInstance("SunX509") Fails Still fails: change the code PQ
Tomcat 11.0.29 default keyManagerAlgorithm (EC) Fails PQ PQ
Tomcat keyManagerAlgorithm="SunX509" (EC) Fails Still fails Not run
Tomcat keyManagerAlgorithm="PKIX", kit fix switched off n/a PQ (explicit PKIX is enough) Not run
Spring Boot 3.5.16 spring.ssl.bundle.jks, EC PKCS#12 (JDK 21) Fails PQ; classical client connects Not run
  • Spring Boot SSL bundles call KeyManagerFactory.getDefaultAlgorithm() (SslManagerBundle, DefaultSslManagerBundle, checked with javap), so the kit property reaches them; key.alias still selects the alias.
  • PEM pairs (spring.ssl.bundle.pem, Tomcat certificateFile) become an in-memory keystore with the same key manager, so the same rule applies. Not run separately. Separate JKS key passwords go through the same KeyManagerFactory.init; not run separately.
  • BC is stricter on chains: it applies the client's signature_algorithms to the whole chain. A client offering only rsa_pss_rsae_sha256 was refused an RSA certificate whose CA signed with PKCS#1; adding rsa_pkcs1_sha256 fixed it (tests/deep/java/ks/). Modern clients offer both.
  • Side effects: an RSA + EC store starts serving EC under kit v2 (check client pins, case I). On JDK 25, PKIX also turns on SNI-based certificate selection.

D. Custom CA trust (private CA)

Who: grep -rnE 'javax\.net\.ssl\.trustStore|TrustManagerFactory|X509TrustManager|truststore|trust-store|keytool .*-importcert|cacerts' src/ conf/ Dockerfile* helm/ *.yml *.yaml *.properties

What to change: nothing, on any JDK. BCJSSE uses the JDK's trust settings: javax.net.ssl.trustStore* when set, else jssecacerts or cacerts. ssl.TrustManagerFactory.algorithm stays PKIX; the kit doesn't touch it.

Trust setup (PQ-only OpenSSL 3.5 server, private-CA certificate) JDK 17/21 + kit JDK 25.0.5 Evidence
No private CA configured Fails certificate_unknown(46) (correct) Fails PKIX path building failed (correct) Tested; e2e java-lambda-j21-sysprops-notrust-bundled
-Djavax.net.ssl.trustStore=trust.p12 (+ password, type) PQ PQ Tested; e2e Lambda sysprops cases
App's own TrustManagerFactory from a truststore PQ PQ e2e (the probe does this in every case)
CA imported into cacerts with keytool -importcert -cacerts PQ Not run Tested (JDK 21)

Spring bundle truststores use TrustManagerFactory.getDefaultAlgorithm() (javap), so trust is the same. A custom X509TrustManager is called by BCJSSE as by SunJSSE (not run; trust-all code is a finding in its own right). Netty tcnative and the CRT client take their own trust inputs; neither was run with a private CA.

E. Mutual TLS (client certificates)

Who: grep -rnE 'clientAuth|client-auth|certificateVerification|setNeedClientAuth|setWantClientAuth', or a client loading its own certificate with -Djavax.net.ssl.keyStore or a KeyManagerFactory.

Today with kit v1 (or a hard-coded SunX509): a JVM client with an EC client certificate silently sends no certificate, and the server answers certificate_required(116). RSA client certificates work. Change: kit v2; no change to client or server stores.

Direction (kit v2 on 17 and 21; JDK 25 without a kit gives the same) Result Evidence
JVM client, EC client certificate, to a PQ mTLS server PQ; server verified the certificate Tested; e2e Lambda, Fargate and EC2 *-ec cases
Same with kit v1 behaviour, or code naming SunX509 mTLS refused; RSA certificate with SunX509 still works e2e java-lambda-j21-ks-ec-v1, -j17-ks-ec-sunx509, -j17-ks-rsa-sunx509
Function passes its own -Djava.security.properties (layer) PQ, EC mTLS works (agent v2 sets PKIX) e2e java-lambda-j21-layer-ownsec, -j11-layer-ownsec
BCJSSE server, needClientAuth, EC or RSA client certificate PQ; server logged the client CN Tested
Same server, no client certificate, or one from an unknown CA Refused (correct) Tested
BCJSSE PQ-only server (jdk.tls.namedGroups=X25519MLKEM768), X25519-only client Refused (correct) Tested

Side effect: a harmless Broken pipe after the response when the server closes first (test artefact).

F. Explicit TLS settings that block PQ

Who: run ci/java-pq-check.sh (full file below), or:

grep -rnE 'jdk\.tls\.namedGroups|setNamedGroups|groups="|OpenSslContextOption\.GROUPS|SSLContext\.getInstance\("TLSv1\.2"\)|TLS\.V_1_2|protocols="TLSv1\.2"|enabled-protocols|ssl\.enabled\.protocols|tlsVersions|jdk\.tls\.client\.protocols|KeyManagerFactory\.getInstance\("SunX509"|keyManagerAlgorithm' .
Setting Effect Change to
jdk.tls.namedGroups without X25519MLKEM768 Classical on every JDK including 25, and for Netty tcnative. The agent leaves an existing value alone (e2e java-lambda-j25-pin, -j21-layer-pin) X25519MLKEM768,x25519,secp256r1,secp384r1
Tomcat groups="x25519,secp256r1" Classical on 25 (Tested) Add X25519MLKEM768 first
SSLContext.getInstance("TLSv1.2"), TLS.V_1_2 only, enabled-protocols: [TLSv1.2], Kafka ssl.enabled.protocols=TLSv1.2, Connector/J tlsVersions=TLSv1.2 No TLS 1.3, so no ML-KEM "TLS", or TLSv1.3,TLSv1.2
KeyManagerFactory.getInstance("SunX509"), Tomcat keyManagerAlgorithm="SunX509" EC certificates fail under BCJSSE (Tested) getDefaultAlgorithm() or "PKIX"
-Djava.security.properties==file (double =) Replaces all of java.security; the kit line is lost Single =, or add the line to that file
Cipher lists (ciphers=, setEnabledCipherSuites) No effect if a TLS 1.3 suite stays Keep at least one TLS 1.3 suite

Library fixes, one line each (full code in "Netty and AWS SDK code" below):

Library Change
Any JSSE client SSLContext.getInstance("TLS"), not "TLSv1.2"
Apache HttpClient 5 custom strategy .setTlsVersions(TLS.V_1_3, TLS.V_1_2)
Netty with BoringSSL .sslProvider(SslProvider.OPENSSL); the OpenSslContextOption.GROUPS list is optional
AWS SDK v2 .httpClient(AwsCrtHttpClient.create()) on 2.35.11+. Before 2.35.11 the builder needed .postQuantumTlsEnabled(true) (AWS Security Blog); upgrade instead. Covers AWS calls only
JVM flag Effect
-Djdk.tls.namedGroups=X25519MLKEM768,x25519,secp256r1,secp384r1 Group order; a list without X25519MLKEM768 turns PQ off on 25 and for tcnative (Tested)
-Djdk.tls.client.protocols=TLSv1.3,TLSv1.2 Required on the Lambda Java 8 runtime (it sets TLSv1.2,TLSv1.1,TLSv1, and BCJSSE honours it: e2e java-lambda-j8-bundled-noproto stays TLS 1.2); not needed on Corretto 8u512 elsewhere
-Dorg.bouncycastle.jsse.client.assumeOriginalHostName=true Java 8 with BCJSSE: JDK 8's HttpsURLConnection opens the socket without a host name and BCJSSE (1.78+, after CVE-2024-34447, bc-java#1656) then fails endpoint identification. JDK 11+ not affected
-javaagent:<dir>/pq-tls-agent.jar JDK 8 to 21: BCJSSE first + PQ-first group list
-Djava.security.properties=<dir>/pq-tls.security Kit v2: the PKIX key manager
-Djava.security.properties=<dir>/bc-static.security Static BC registration (containers and EC2, not Lambda); includes the PKIX line

G. FIPS mode

Who: bc-fips/bctls-fips, BouncyCastleFipsProvider in java.security, a vendor FIPS JDK, or Corretto/RHEL with the OS in FIPS mode (/proc/sys/crypto/fips_enabled = 1). Today: not tested. Change: don't add the kit: it puts non-FIPS bcprov/bctls first. Whether bctls-fips offers X25519MLKEM768 is not sure: the BC FIPS 2.1 release notes we found cover bc-fips and do not mention hybrid TLS groups. Track JDK 25 with your FIPS provider and compliance team. The PKIX key-manager point is Unverified for bctls-fips. For AWS API calls use the FIPS endpoints; PQ on them depends on the service (AWS service endpoints chapter).

H. Libraries with their own TLS stack, and agents

Library Stack Kit effect What to do Evidence
Netty + tcnative-boringssl-static 2.0.85 (gRPC-Java, Reactor Netty, Vert.x/Quarkus native, Lettuce with OpenSSL) BoringSSL None Nothing; don't pin jdk.tls.namedGroups without ML-KEM Tested
AWS SDK v2 CRT client s2n-tls None SDK 2.35.11+ Tested; Documented
AWS SDK v2 Apache, UrlConnection, Netty without tcnative; JDBC; Kafka; Jedis JSSE Applies Follow the JDK row; keep TLS 1.3 allowed Tested (Apache, PostgreSQL); others Unverified
APM agents (Datadog, New Relic, OpenTelemetry) JSSE for exporters Share JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS Append the kit, don't replace the variable Not run
Conscrypt, wolfJSSE Own JSSE provider The one at position 1 wins Check Security.getProviders() order at startup Not run
Amazon Corretto Crypto Provider (ACCP) JCA provider, not JSSE No conflict expected Check provider order Unverified
Tools the JVM shells out to (curl, git, wget) Their own libraries n/a GnuTLS builds have no ML-KEM; OpenSSL 3.5 builds do (Hosts and OS chapter) Tested in that chapter

I. Certificate pinning, HSM/PKCS#11 and KMS-backed keys

  • Client pins (OkHttp CertificatePinner, custom trust managers) keep working: the kit doesn't change what the peer serves. Exception: a server with RSA and EC in one store serves EC under kit v2 (case C), which breaks clients pinning the RSA leaf. Pin the CA or both keys first, or keep one key type per store.
  • PKCS#11/HSM keys (SunPKCS11, CloudHSM JCE): BCJSSE signs through the PrivateKey, so the key must work through JCA Signature. Not tested; test on the real HSM, or terminate at an ALB/NLB with an ACM certificate, or move to JDK 25.
  • ACM and KMS keys can't back a JVM KeyManager; terminate at the AWS front door. ACM exportable certificates are ordinary keystores (case C).

J. Other cases

  • HTTPS proxy (https.proxyHost, SDK HTTPS_PROXY): with CONNECT, TLS is end to end, so the JVM and origin decide; a TLS-inspecting proxy decides itself (Network devices and egress chapter). Not run.
  • HTTP/2, gRPC, WebSockets use the same handshake; gRPC-Java uses Netty (case H).
  • Service mesh sidecar: if the app speaks plain HTTP to the sidecar, the mesh decides (Service mesh chapter).
  • Lambda SnapStart: the agent runs before the snapshot, so the provider order should be captured in it. Not tested.
  • Session resumption and 0-RTT under BCJSSE: not tested.

Solution and automation

  1. Inventory. JDK per workload (java -version in the image or on EC2, Lambda Runtime, ECR image labels). Kit presence: JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS containing pq-tls-agent.jar and pq-tls.security; on a running JVM, jcmd <pid> VM.system_properties | grep -E 'java.version|jdk.tls.namedGroups|java.security.properties'. Keystore key types (case C), mTLS (case E), blocking settings (CI check). Traffic: CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange.
  2. Change. JDK 25.0.5+ where you can; elsewhere kit v2 (Quick start). Kit v1 deployments: add the pq-tls.security flag or swap the jar. Fix code from case F.
  3. Package per compute type (full files below):
Compute JDK 25.0.5+ JDK 8 to 21: kit v2 JDK 17 and 21: static java.security
Lambda zip Runtime java25 pq-tls/ at the zip root, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=KIT (/var/task) Doesn't work (e2e java-lambda-j21-static)
Lambda layer Not needed (the wrapper makes no change on 25) pq-tls layer v2 + AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper; the wrapper adds pq-tls.security unless the function passes its own security file, and the Java 8 flags on java8* Doesn't work
Lambda container image public.ecr.aws/lambda/java:25 COPY pq-tls/ ${LAMBDA_TASK_ROOT}/pq-tls/ + ENV JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=KIT Not tested
Fargate, ECS on EC2, EKS amazoncorretto:25, eclipse-temurin:25 COPY pq-tls/ /opt/pq-tls/ + ENV JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=KIT, or the task definition or pod spec bc-static.security + BC jars on -cp (e2e, including an EC client certificate)
EC2 dnf install java-25-amazon-corretto-headless systemd drop-in, or the SSM document Same file via ExecStart

Rules everywhere:

  • Append to JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS; distroless images work too (the JVM reads the variable itself).
  • java -jar ignores -cp: for static registration, use the manifest Class-Path or -cp ... MainClass.
  • Pin images by digest and record the JDK patch level; the PQ default arrived in a patch release (25.0.5).
  • If the app passes its own -Djava.security.properties on the command line, that wins over the kit's flag; agent v2 then sets PKIX itself (Tested), or add the line to the app's file.
  • If the app ships its own Bouncy Castle, the agent's 1.87 jars load first on the system class path; align versions.
  1. CI guardrail. ci/java-pq-check.sh <repo> (kit/java-v2/ci/java-pq-check.sh) fails on group pins without ML-KEM, TLS 1.2-only pins, a hard-coded SunX509 and java.security.properties==; it warns on JDK below 25, kit v1 packaging, tcnative/CRT/Conscrypt and FIPS providers. Tested on a fixture: 3 FAIL + 2 WARN on the bad tree (exit 1), 1 WARN on the good one (exit 0), tests/deep/java/v2/out/ci-check.txt. Add a registry or admission rule: JDK below 25 without the kit is non-compliant.
  2. Fleet rollout: see the Rollout checklist.

How to test

  1. Posture. java -version; startup log Picked up JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS: -Djava.security.properties=.../pq-tls.security -javaagent:... and pq-tls agent v2: BCJSSE first, groups=X25519MLKEM768,..., ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX. ...=SunX509 means the fix is switched off.
  2. Client, local. Start a PQ-only and a classical-only OpenSSL 3.5 server (openssl s_server -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 and -groups X25519, or nginx ssl_ecdh_curve X25519MLKEM768 with $ssl_curve in the log format). Run your client image against both; both must succeed.
  3. Server. openssl s_client -connect <host>:8443 -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -brief must print Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768; -groups X25519 must still connect.
  4. Your keystore (case C). Start the server with a copy of your real store under kit v2, run step 3 with -servername <each SNI name> and check the Peer certificate CN. For RSA + EC stores also run -sigalgs rsa_pss_rsae_sha256:rsa_pkcs1_sha256.
  5. Your truststore (case D). Against a PQ-only server with a private-CA certificate: connects with your truststore, fails without it.
  6. mTLS (case E). Server: openssl s_client ... -cert client.crt -key client.key -groups X25519MLKEM768 connects and is refused without -cert. Client: against openssl s_server -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -Verify 1 -CAfile ca.crt, the server log shows depth=0 CN=<your client cert>.
  7. In AWS. CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange = X25519MLKEM768 on the role's KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager, ACM and Payment Cryptography calls; for servers, the load balancer or peer logs the group.
  8. Rollback. Remove the flags (or PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 with the layer) and restart: step 2 against the PQ-only server must now fail and the classical one still work.

Handshake traces: SunJSSE -Djavax.net.debug=ssl:handshake shows the key_share group; BCJSSE logs through java.util.logging at FINE on org.bouncycastle.jsse. The harness for cases C to E is run-v2.sh (full file below).

Rollout checklist

# Step Done when
1 Inventory every JVM: JDK patch level, compute type, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS, existing agents Each workload has a row in the inventory
2 Classify: 25.0.5+ (nothing), 8 to 21 (kit v2 or upgrade), FIPS (exception), Netty tcnative / CRT only (already PQ for those calls) Each row has a target
3 Find EC keystores, mTLS client certificates, hard-coded SunX509, group and TLS 1.2 pins (case C, E, F greps; CI check) CI check passes, or findings fixed
4 Check RSA + EC stores for client pinning (case I); test PKCS#11/HSM keys on the real HSM Owners confirmed
5 Java 8: both flags on Lambda, the host-name flag elsewhere Present in IaC
6 Ship kit v2 (never v1) through the shared build template; append to JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS Startup log shows algorithm=PKIX
7 Canary one task, pod, alias (10% weighted) or instance per service No new handshake_failure, certificate_required or certificate_unknown errors; ALB TargetTLSNegotiationErrorCount flat
8 Run "How to test" steps 2 to 6 against the canary All pass
9 Widen dev → qa → prod Dashboards show the workload's AWS calls as X25519MLKEM768
10 Plan the move to JDK 25.0.5+ and remove the kit after it Kit removed; CI check has no JDK warning
11 Lambda Java on Amazon Linux 2 (java8.al2, java11, java17): move before AWS blocks updates on 2027-08-31 Runtime upgraded

Pitfalls and rollback

Pitfall Effect Avoid it
Kit v1 (agent only) Every EC server certificate fails; EC client certificates silently not sent (Tested; e2e java-lambda-j21-ks-ec-v1) Kit v2 only
Hard-coded SunX509 (code or Tomcat) Beats the kit; EC fails (Tested; e2e -j17-ks-ec-sunx509) getDefaultAlgorithm() or PKIX; CI check
A pinned jdk.tls.namedGroups Classical on any JDK, 25 included; the agent leaves it alone (e2e) Add X25519MLKEM768 first; grep launch scripts, Helm values, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS
TLS 1.2 pins No ML-KEM Allow TLS 1.3 (case F)
Java 8 without the host-name flag Every HttpsURLConnection handshake fails, PQ and classical (e2e) Add assumeOriginalHostName=true
Java 8 on Lambda without the protocols flag Stays classical TLS 1.2 (e2e) Add jdk.tls.client.protocols=TLSv1.3,TLSv1.2, or use the layer
JDK 25 multi-certificate store SunX509 ignores SNI and serves the wrong certificate (Tested) pq-tls.security (PKIX) on 25 too
Served certificate type changes RSA + EC stores serve EC under kit v2 Check client pins
BC chain strictness RSA-PSS-only clients refused a PKCS#1-signed chain Modern clients offer both
BCJSSE is the provider for the whole process Untested areas: PKCS#11 keys, session resumption, FIPS Test before rollout
Another agent reorders providers or replaces SSLContext.getDefault() Undoes the kit Check the startup log and provider order
FIPS JVM Non-FIPS provider put first Don't add the kit
PQ-only server too early Classical clients fail (expected) Enforce only when logs show no classical clients
Larger ClientHello (about 1.2 KB more) Middleboxes that mishandle a split ClientHello break the first flight; Java doesn't retry classical Test through the real network path

Rollback. Remove both -Djava.security.properties=.../pq-tls.security and -javaagent: from JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS and restart (removing only the agent leaves the harmless PKIX property). Lambda: delete the variable, or PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 with the layer (e2e java-lambda-j21-layer-disabled: classical); the next cold start is classical. JDK 25: -Djdk.tls.namedGroups=x25519,secp256r1,secp384r1 (e2e java-lambda-j25-pin). CRT client: swap back to ApacheHttpClient.

Test evidence

The LocalStack e2e suite, run r1117041043 (2026-11-16, LocalStack Pro on arm64, not real AWS), ran 96 Java client cases and 7 load balancer target cases; all 103 passed. Pass means the observed behaviour matched the case's expectation, which for a baseline case is "classical". Cases: e2e/cases/java.py. Client: e2e/clients/java/Probe.java, an app with its own SSLContext, truststore and client keystore over HttpsURLConnection, compiled for Java 8, with no PQ settings in code. Server: e2e/clients/server/java/Server.java (HttpsServer, RSA PKCS#12, default key manager). Build and pinned images: e2e/build/java.sh. Results: e2e/results/latest/summary.md.

Each client case connects to eight listeners: hybrid, PQ-only, mTLS, EC server certificate, TLS 1.2-only and three ML-DSA listeners.

Compute Cases What it proved
Lambda zip, java8.al2 to java25 46 Baselines: classical on 8 to 21 (TLS 1.2 on 8), PQ on 25. Kit v2 in the zip and the pq-tls layer: PQ on 8 to 21, including EC client certificates via own SSLContext and via -Djavax.net.ssl.*. Java 8: PQ only with both flags. Negative and regression: kit v1 behaviour and hard-coded SunX509 drop EC mTLS; group pins stay classical; PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1 rolls back; no private CA fails; static registration doesn't work. pq-egress extension: PQ on every runtime
Lambda container image, lambda/java:8.al2023 to :25 11 Baselines classical on 8 to 21, PQ on 25; Dockerfile.lambda kit PQ on 11, 17, 21; JDK 8 PQ with the layer files and wrapper
ECS on Fargate 21 Corretto 8 to 21 and AL2023 dnf Corretto classical, 25 PQ; kit v2 via task definition or image ENV PQ on 8, 11, 17, 21 (8 needs the host-name flag, not the protocols flag); static registration PQ on Corretto 17 and 21 and Temurin 21, including an EC client certificate
ECS on EC2 5 Corretto 21 classical, 21 + kit PQ, 25 PQ, 17 static PQ; JDK 8 without the host-name flag fails
EC2 (AL2023 and AL2) 13 Corretto 11, 17, 21 classical and PQ with kit v2 (EC client certificate on 21); 25 PQ; the default java resolved to 25
Load balancer target leg (server) 7 Fargate: Corretto 8 and 21 classical, 8, 17, 21 + kit v2 PQ, 25 PQ; ECS on EC2: 21 + kit PQ. ALB classic backend policy: classical TLS 1.2 on all

Across all client cases: every working client still connected to the TLS 1.2-only listener (classical fallback); against a dual-certificate server every Java client received ECDSA, and the ML-DSA-only listeners refused all of them. No JDK negotiates ML-DSA in TLS yet.

Not tested in e2e: real AWS; JDK 8 with the full fix on ECS on EC2 and EC2; JDK 11 servers; servers with EC certificates (the deep tests cover them on 17, 21 and 25); Temurin 25 (deep tests only); FIPS; SnapStart; PKCS#11. Earlier deep tests (libraries, servers, keystores, CRT versions) are in tests/deep/java, with outputs in out/ and v2/out/.

6.9 .NET

Problem statement

.NET has no TLS library of its own. SslStream does every handshake (HttpClient, gRPC, Kestrel, Npgsql, MySqlConnector, StackExchange.Redis, the AWS SDK for .NET) and calls the operating system's library: OpenSSL (libssl.so.3) on Linux, Schannel on Windows, Apple TLS on macOS. So the image or host decides PQ, not the .NET version.

Where it runs OS TLS library Why it is classical today
Default mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet tags (8.0 = Debian 12, 10.0 = Ubuntu 24.04), -noble, Azure Linux 3.0 OpenSSL 3.0 to 3.3 No ML-KEM below OpenSSL 3.5
Lambda dotnet8, dotnet10, provided.al2023; Amazon Linux 2023 images and EC2 OpenSSL 3.5.8 The AL2023 crypto policy leaves ML-KEM out of the group list
Windows Schannel ML-KEM groups ship disabled

The result is classical X25519 or P-256 key exchange, which traffic recorded today does not survive once a quantum computer can break it (harvest now, decrypt later).

What follows from that:

  • No .NET API picks the key-exchange group. SslClientAuthenticationOptions, SslServerAuthenticationOptions and CipherSuitesPolicy have no groups setting, and the native shim never calls SSL_CTX_set1_groups (Documented: pal_ssl.c, release/10.0). Code can only block PQ, by pinning TLS 1.2.
  • OPENSSL_CONF is honoured. .NET initialises OpenSSL with OPENSSL_INIT_LOAD_CONFIG (Documented: openssl.c; Tested on Lambda, Fargate, ECS on EC2 and EC2 in the e2e suite).
  • Which libssl loads. .NET tries libssl.so.3, then 1.1. CLR_OPENSSL_VERSION_OVERRIDE (.NET 8, 9) or DOTNET_OPENSSL_VERSION_OVERRIDE (.NET 10+) forces a version (Documented: opensslshim.c); it cannot help when the image only has OpenSSL 3.0.
  • Rule of thumb: OpenSSL 3.5+ with X25519MLKEM768 in the default group list = PQ. OpenSSL 3.0 to 3.4 = never PQ. AL2023 has 3.5 but needs the config file or DEFAULT:PQ.
  • Certificates, keystores and CA bundles don't change. PFX, PEM, encrypted PEM, RSA, EC, SNI, X509Store, private CAs, custom trust code and client certificates all kept working with PQ on (Tested, cases C, D, E).
  • One exception: Confluent.Kafka; librdkafka ships its own OpenSSL (case H).

Quick start by compute type

Find your row, make the one change, verify, and keep the rollback ready. Verify with a PQ-only test server (openssl s_server -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3): the call must succeed, and must also still succeed against a classical server (How to test).

Compute Check One change Verify Roll back Evidence
Lambda zip dotnet8, dotnet10 Runtime: in SAM/CFN/Terraform Ship pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf (CopyToPublishDirectory), set OPENSSL_CONF=/var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf. Fleet option: pq-tls layer + AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper PQ-only call succeeds; CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for the role's KMS/STS calls shows X25519MLKEM768 Remove OPENSSL_CONF (layer: PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1) Tested (e2e)
Lambda image public.ecr.aws/lambda/dotnet:8, :10 FROM line COPY pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf /var/task/pq-tls/ + ENV OPENSSL_CONF=... Same Redeploy the previous image (or rebuild without the two lines) Tested (e2e)
Lambda Native AOT (provided.al2023) PublishAot in the csproj Same file and variable; AOT loads the system libssl.so.3 Same Remove OPENSSL_CONF Tested (e2e, .NET 10 AOT)

One service: Environment=OPENSSL_CONF=... in its systemd unit PQ-only call from the host succeeds update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT, or remove the unit line; restart Tested (e2e)
EC2 Amazon Linux 2, Ubuntu 22.04/24.04 openssl version < 3.5 No setting helps: move to AL2023 or a PQ container image — — Documented (OpenSSL 3.5 is the first with ML-KEM)
Windows (IIS, HTTP.sys, Kestrel, HttpClient) Get-TlsEccCurve; Get-HotFix July 2026 cumulative update or later, then Enable-TlsEccCurve -Name 'X25519_MLKEM768' -Position 0 (or GPO "ECC Curve Order"), reboot Get-TlsEccCurve lists it first; PQ-only call succeeds Disable-TlsEccCurve -Name 'X25519_MLKEM768' or remove it from the GPO; reboot Documented; not tested

Everywhere: remove SslProtocols = Tls12 caps (case F). ML-KEM needs TLS 1.3. That is the only code change PQ ever needs.

The main change on Lambda (SAM; full template.yaml and Function.csproj at the end, or configs/dotnet/):

OrdersFunction:
  Type: AWS::Serverless::Function
  Properties:
    Runtime: dotnet10
    Handler: Orders::Orders.Function::Handler
    Environment:
      Variables:
        OPENSSL_CONF: /var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf   # file shipped by Function.csproj

The kit file configs/dotnet/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf is for the Amazon Linux 2023 family only: it includes the AL2023 crypto-policy file. It adds ML-KEM key exchange, not ML-DSA signatures (Test evidence).

Deadlines. .NET 8 and 9 left Microsoft support on 2026-11-10; .NET 10 is supported to 2028-11-14 (Microsoft). Lambda dotnet8 was deprecated on 2026-11-10; function creation is blocked from 2027-07-29 and updates from 2027-08-31, after which no file or variable can be added (AWS). Apply the PQ fix to dotnet8 now, and move to dotnet10 before those dates.

Cases: what to change

Unless a row says e2e, the tests below ran on 2026-11-14 on arm64 with these images (run by digest): aspnet:8.0 (Debian 12, OpenSSL 3.0, .NET 8.0.34), aspnet:8.0-alpine (8.0.34), aspnet:10.0-alpine (10.0.15), aspnet:10.0-resolute (Ubuntu 26.04, OpenSSL 3.5.5, 10.0.15), and amazonlinux:2023 + aspnetcore-runtime-8.0 (OpenSSL 3.5.8, 8.0.33) with OPENSSL_CONF=openssl-pq.cnf. The PQ-only server is openssl s_server -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3, the classical one -groups X25519, both OpenSSL 3.5.7 (debian:trixie-slim). Kestrel was probed with openssl s_client, which prints the negotiated group. Scripts and output: tests/deep/dotnet/v2/run-cases.sh, results-v2.txt.

Result in one line: on the four PQ images every certificate, trust and mTLS case negotiated X25519MLKEM768 and still worked with a classical client. On Debian 12 the same cases all worked classically and none was PQ: the image decides, not the certificate setup.

A. Simple client

  • Who this is: HttpClient, IHttpClientFactory, AWS SDK for .NET, Npgsql, MySqlConnector, StackExchange.Redis, Grpc.Net.Client, with no TLS code. Check: grep -rn "SslProtocols\|SslOptions\|ClientCertificates" --include=*.cs . returns nothing.
  • What happens today: classical on default images, Lambda and AL2023; PQ on -alpine/-resolute. Tested (tests/deep/dotnet/results.txt, e2e).
  • What to change: the image or OPENSSL_CONF only. No code.
  • Side effects: none seen; every PQ client still reached the classical-only server.
Client TLS stack What to set Evidence
HttpClient / SocketsHttpHandler SslStream → OS Leave SslOptions.EnabledSslProtocols at None, or include Tls13 Tested
HttpClientHandler.SslProtocols SslStream → OS Same: None or include Tls13 Tested (e2e hch-p12-clientcert, alpine8-callback-trust)
SslStream directly OS SslClientAuthenticationOptions.EnabledSslProtocols = None Tested
gRPC Grpc.Net.Client HttpClient Nothing; HTTP/2 over TLS follows the OS Documented; not run
gRPC Grpc.Core (legacy C-core) Own bundled BoringSSL Migrate to Grpc.Net.Client; Grpc.Core is out of support Documented deprecation; its ML-KEM support not confirmed
AWS SDK for .NET (v3, v4) HttpClient Nothing in code; the OS needs OpenSSL 3.5+ with ML-KEM enabled Documented
Npgsql 9 SslStream (source) Nothing; SSL Mode=Require or stricter Tested against PostgreSQL 18.7 with ssl_groups = 'X25519MLKEM768': 10.0-alpine, 10.0-resolute and AL2023+conf connect; 10.0 (Ubuntu 24.04) fails
MySqlConnector SslStream (source) Don't pin TlsVersion=TLS 1.2 Documented (source code)
StackExchange.Redis SslStream (source) Don't set SslProtocols=Tls12 Documented (source code)
Confluent.Kafka 2.16 librdkafka with its own statically linked OpenSSL 3.5.8 Nothing; optional ssl.curves.list See case H 
// HttpClient: the only TLS setting that matters for PQ is the protocol version.
var handler = new SocketsHttpHandler();
handler.SslOptions.EnabledSslProtocols = SslProtocols.None; // OS default: TLS 1.2 + 1.3
// BAD: handler.SslOptions.EnabledSslProtocols = SslProtocols.Tls12;  -> never PQ
var http = new HttpClient(handler);
var channel = GrpcChannel.ForAddress("https://orders.internal:8443",
    new GrpcChannelOptions { HttpHandler = handler });   // gRPC: same handler

B. Simple server

  • Who this is: Kestrel (WebApplication, UseKestrel) with the default HTTPS endpoint, or behind an ALB/NLB that terminates TLS.
  • What happens today: Kestrel follows the image: PQ on -alpine, -resolute, AL2023 + OPENSSL_CONF or DEFAULT:PQ; classical on 8.0, 10.0, Azure Linux. Tested. Behind a load balancer the client-facing handshake is the listener policy's (Load balancers chapter).
  • What to change: the image or OPENSSL_CONF. Keep SslProtocols at None or include Tls13.
  • Side effects: none; X25519-only clients still connect (Tested).
Server TLS stack PQ setting Evidence
Kestrel on Linux SslStream → OpenSSL None in Kestrel: PQ image or OPENSSL_CONF; keep SslProtocols at None or include Tls13 Tested
Kestrel on Windows Schannel Enable X25519_MLKEM768 in Schannel Documented (Microsoft, Kestrel demo); not tested
IIS, HTTP.sys (UseHttpSys) Schannel via HTTP.sys Same Schannel setting; machine-wide Documented (applies to all Schannel callers); not tested
ASP.NET Core gRPC server Kestrel or HTTP.sys Follows the server above; gRPC needs HTTP/2 Unverified (Kestrel test had Http1AndHttp2, no gRPC call made)
Behind ALB, NLB or API Gateway The AWS front door The listener policy decides the client-facing leg Load balancers chapter

Kestrel excerpt (appsettings.json; full Program.cs and file at the end):

"Kestrel": {
  "Endpoints": {
    "Https": {
      "Url": "https://0.0.0.0:8443",
      "SslProtocols": [ "Tls12", "Tls13" ],
      "Certificate": { "Path": "/certs/server.pfx", "Password": "..." }
    }
  }
}

The same endpoint set to Tls12 only rejected the PQ client with a protocol_version alert (Tested). SslProtocols set in ConfigureHttpsDefaults, UseHttps(o => ...) or SslServerAuthenticationOptions.EnabledSslProtocols has the same effect.

Load balancer target leg (e2e, run r1117041043). With the observer acting as an ALB/NLB connecting to an HTTPS target, an SslStream server with default options (what Kestrel calls) stayed classical on runtime:8.0 and AL2023 .NET 8, and negotiated X25519MLKEM768 on runtime:10.0-alpine (Fargate and ECS on EC2), aspnet:10.0-resolute, and AL2023 with OPENSSL_CONF or DEFAULT:PQ (cases srv-dotnet-*). Against the classic backend policy every target fell back to classical TLS 1.2, as expected.

C. Existing server certificates: PFX, PEM, encrypted keys, SNI, X509Store

  • Who this is: Kestrel loads your own certificate. Find it with: grep -rnE "Certificates|Certificate\"|KeyPath|ServerCertificate|X509Store|UseHttps\(" --include=*.cs --include=appsettings*.json . and in env vars or Helm values: Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Path, ASPNETCORE_Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Password.
  • What happens today: the certificate does not affect key exchange; it only signs the handshake. Tested: same cert, PQ on PQ images, classical on Debian 12.
  • What changes in the store or files: nothing. Keep the same PFX, PEM, password, key type, store and SNI map. ML-KEM is key exchange; your RSA or ECDSA certificate stays valid. ML-DSA certificates are a separate, later step (Certificates chapter).
  • What to change: only the image or OPENSSL_CONF (case A/B).
  • Side effects: none found. Unlike Java with Bouncy Castle, there is no key-manager layer between the store and OpenSSL.

Tested proof (Kestrel server, openssl s_client as client). "PQ" = -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 printed Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768 and got the HTTP 200 body. "Classical" = -groups X25519 got HTTP 200. Chain verified with -CAfile ca.crt -verify_return_error in both.

# How the certificate is loaded Key Signature seen 8.0-alpine 10.0-alpine 10.0-resolute AL2023 + conf 8.0 (Debian 12)
C1 Kestrel:Certificates:Default:Path + Password (PFX, AES-256/PBKDF2 from OpenSSL 3.5) EC P-256 ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256 PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
C2 Same, PFX RSA 2048 rsa_pss_rsae_sha256 PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
C3 PEM pair: Path (leaf + CA chain) + KeyPath EC ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256 PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
C4 PEM pair with encrypted PKCS#8 key: Path + KeyPath + Password RSA rsa_pss_rsae_sha256 PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
C5 ServerCertificateSelector in code: ec.test → EC PFX, rsa.test → RSA PFX EC and RSA right cert per SNI PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
C6 Endpoint Sni config section, one entry per host name EC and RSA right cert per SNI PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
C7 X509Store CurrentUser/My: PFX imported with PersistKeySet, endpoint uses Subject + Store: My + Location: CurrentUser EC ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256 PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only

All 90 probes (9 setups x 2 clients x 5 images) behaved as shown (Tested, results-v2.txt). Config used, as env vars (same keys as appsettings.json):

# C1/C2 PFX
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Path=/certs/server-ec.pfx
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Password=<from Secrets Manager>
# C3/C4 PEM pair (Password only when the key is encrypted)
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Path=/certs/server-rsa-chain.crt
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__KeyPath=/certs/server-rsa-enc.key
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Password=<key password>
# C6 SNI map in config
Kestrel__Endpoints__Https__Sni__ec.test__Certificate__Path=/certs/server-ec.pfx
Kestrel__Endpoints__Https__Sni__rsa.test__Certificate__Path=/certs/server-rsa.pfx
# C7 X509Store (AllowInvalid only because the test CA is not in the system store)
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Subject=pq-server-ec
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Store=My
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__Location=CurrentUser
Kestrel__Certificates__Default__AllowInvalid=true

Notes for this case:

  • RSA and EC: both work with PQ. The key type changes only the signature (RSA-PSS or ECDSA).
  • Several certificates: Kestrel serves one certificate per endpoint unless you use ServerCertificateSelector (https.ServerCertificateSelector = (ctx, name) => ... in ConfigureHttpsDefaults) or the Sni section. Both select by SNI and both worked with PQ. There is no "RSA+EC in one store, pick by client" behaviour as in Java; use SNI or two endpoints.
  • X509Store on Linux: CurrentUser/My lives under $HOME/.dotnet/corefx/cryptography/x509stores/my/; persist it (volume or image layer) or re-import at start. LocalMachine/My is not writable on Linux; Root/CA stores are a read-only view of the OpenSSL trust files (discussion).
  • X509Store on Lambda: the passwd home (/home/sbx_user…) is read-only, so an import fails with CryptographicException: The X509 certificate could not be added to the store (UnauthorizedAccessException) before any handshake. Fix: an exec wrapper in the package that exports HOME=/tmp (AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/var/task/home-wrapper; Tested, e2e dotnet-lambda-dotnet8/10-store-clientcert), or load the PFX/PEM directly (E1, E2). HOME=/tmp as a function variable: LocalStack drops it, so e2e could not prove it (*-store-clientcert-nohome records that failure); AWS's own base image under the Runtime Interface Emulator honours it. Unverified on real AWS. Setting HOME in code with Environment.SetEnvironmentVariable did not help in an earlier e2e run.
  • Windows certificate store (Schannel): the curve order is machine-wide and separate from the certificate. Not tested.
  • HSM, TPM or KMS keys: case I.

D. Custom CA trust: system store, SSL_CERT_FILE / SSL_CERT_DIR, validation callbacks, CustomTrustStore

  • Who this is: your service calls internal endpoints signed by a private CA (ACM Private CA, an on-prem CA). Find it with: grep -rnE "ServerCertificateCustomValidationCallback|RemoteCertificateValidationCallback|CertificateChainPolicy|CustomTrustStore|X509Chain" --include=*.cs . and in Dockerfiles: update-ca-certificates, update-ca-trust, SSL_CERT_FILE, SSL_CERT_DIR.
  • What happens today: trust is checked after key exchange and is independent of it. Every trust method below worked with PQ on the PQ images; on Debian 12 the PQ-only server refused the handshake before any certificate was seen.
  • What changes in the store or bundle: nothing. Same CA file, hashed directory, callback and X509ChainPolicy.
  • What to change: only the image or OPENSSL_CONF. On AL2023 the kit openssl-pq.cnf changes only Groups; /etc/pki/ca-trust and update-ca-trust keep working (Tested, D5, and e2e dotnet-fargate-al2023-sysca-conf).
  • Side effects: moving from Debian/Ubuntu to Alpine, update-ca-certificates needs apk add ca-certificates first (Tested, e2e dotnet-fargate-alpine-sysca).

Tested proof (.NET client, HttpClient). PQ = request to the PQ-only server returned 200. Classical = request to the X25519-only server returned 200.

# Trust method 8.0-alpine 10.0-alpine 10.0-resolute AL2023 + conf 8.0 (Debian 12)
D0 No private CA anywhere (control) Fails UntrustedRoot (expected) Same Same Same Same on classical; PQ fails earlier at handshake
D1 SSL_CERT_FILE=/certs/ca.crt PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
D2 SSL_CERT_DIR=/certs/hashdir (openssl rehash) PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
D3 HttpClientHandler.ServerCertificateCustomValidationCallback building the chain against the CA PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
D4 SocketsHttpHandler.SslOptions.CertificateChainPolicy with CustomRootTrust + CustomTrustStore PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
D5 CA added to the system store (update-ca-certificates or update-ca-trust) PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only

60 requests, all as expected (Tested, results-v2.txt). D0 to D3 were repeated on Lambda dotnet8/dotnet10 with the fix (e2e).

// D4: trust only your private CA for this client (.NET 7+). Unchanged by PQ.
var ca = X509Certificate2.CreateFromPem(File.ReadAllText("/certs/ca.crt"));
var handler = new SocketsHttpHandler();
handler.SslOptions.CertificateChainPolicy = new X509ChainPolicy
{
    TrustMode = X509ChainTrustMode.CustomRootTrust,
    CustomTrustStore = { ca },
};

System store per OS family: Debian/Ubuntu COPY ca.crt /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/corp-ca.crt + RUN update-ca-certificates; Alpine the same after apk add --no-cache ca-certificates; AL2023 COPY ca.crt /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/corp-ca.crt + RUN update-ca-trust.

Notes for this case:

  • Pitfall found while testing: X509Certificate2.CreateFromPemFile("ca.crt") with no key path throws CryptographicException: The key contents do not contain a PEM..., because it looks for a private key in the same file. Load a CA with X509Certificate2.CreateFromPem(File.ReadAllText(path)) or X509CertificateLoader (.NET 9+). Not PQ-related, but it appears when teams add custom trust code.
  • Callbacks that return true: they hide trust errors; PQ does not change them, but the CI check warns on them.

E. Mutual TLS (client certificates)

  • Who this is: HttpClientHandler.ClientCertificates, SocketsHttpHandler.SslOptions.ClientCertificates, ClientCertificateContext, or Kestrel ClientCertificateMode. Grep: grep -rnE "ClientCertificate" --include=*.cs --include=appsettings*.json .
  • What happens today: the client certificate is sent and checked after key exchange; PQ does not touch it. Tested.
  • What changes in the store or files: nothing.
  • What to change: the image or OPENSSL_CONF on both sides.
  • Side effects: none found. A missing or foreign client certificate is still refused with PQ on.
# Test 8.0-alpine 10.0-alpine 10.0-resolute AL2023 + conf 8.0 (Debian 12)
E0 .NET client, no client cert, server requires one Refused certificate required (expected) Same Same Same Same (classical)
E1 HttpClientHandler.ClientCertificates from a PFX PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
E2 SocketsHttpHandler.SslOptions.ClientCertificates from a PEM pair (CreateFromPemFile(crt, key)) PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
E3 Client cert from X509Store CurrentUser/My PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical PQ + classical Classical only
E4 Kestrel ClientCertificateMode.RequireCertificate + ClientCertificateValidation with CustomTrustStore; s_client -cert PQ + classical, app sees CN=pq-client Same Same Same Classical only
E5 Same server, client sends no cert Handshake completes (PQ group shown), request refused Same Same Same Same (classical)
E6 Same server, cert from another CA Request refused Same Same Same Same (classical)

Servers in E0–E3: openssl s_server -Verify 1 -CAfile ca.crt -verify_return_error, PQ-only and classical-only. All 70 results as expected (Tested, results-v2.txt). E1 to E3 were repeated in e2e on Lambda and Fargate, E3 also on EC2 (E3 on Lambda: see the X509Store on Lambda note in case C).

// E4: Kestrel requires and verifies client certificates against your CA. Unchanged by PQ.
builder.WebHost.ConfigureKestrel(k => k.ConfigureHttpsDefaults(https =>
{
    https.ClientCertificateMode = ClientCertificateMode.RequireCertificate;
    https.ClientCertificateValidation = (cert, chain, errors) =>
    {
        using var c = new X509Chain();
        c.ChainPolicy.TrustMode = X509ChainTrustMode.CustomRootTrust;
        c.ChainPolicy.CustomTrustStore.Add(clientCa);
        return c.Build(cert);
    };
}));

In TLS 1.3 a refused client certificate shows up after the handshake (the server sends an alert and closes). Logs that count "handshake succeeded" count E5/E6 as handshakes; count HTTP results instead.

F. Explicit TLS settings that block PQ

  • Who this is: code or config that sets protocols or ciphers. Find it with the CI check (check-dotnet-pq.sh, full file at the end) or: grep -rnE "SslProtocols|EnabledSslProtocols|CipherSuitesPolicy|TlsVersion=|SecurityProtocol" --include=*.cs --include=*.json --include=*.config .
# Setting Result Evidence
F1 Client SslOptions.EnabledSslProtocols = SslProtocols.Tls12 PQ-only server refuses (protocol version alert); classical TLS 1.2 works Tested, all 5 images; e2e on Lambda dotnet10 and Fargate 10.0-resolute
F1b Client Tls12 | Tls13 PQ Tested (e2e dotnet-lambda-dotnet10-tls12-tls13)
F2 Client CipherSuitesPolicy with TLS 1.3 AES-GCM suites only PQ still works; groups are separate from cipher suites Tested, 4 PQ images
F3 Kestrel SslProtocols = Tls12 PQ clients refused (protocol version) Tested, all 5 images
F3b Same, EC certificate, client offering only X25519 Refused (handshake failure): in TLS 1.2 the client's group list also limits the ECDSA cert curve; with X25519:P-256 it connects; an RSA cert works with X25519 only Tested (run-f3.sh, results-f3.txt, 10.0-alpine)
F4 Kestrel CipherSuitesPolicy (via OnAuthenticate) TLS 1.3 AES-GCM only PQ + classical Tested, 4 PQ images
F5 CipherSuitesPolicy on Windows Throws NotSupportedException Documented
F6 MySqlConnector TlsVersion=TLS 1.2, Redis SslProtocols=Tls12, ServicePointManager.SecurityProtocol = Tls12 Same effect as F1 Documented (source code); not run
  • What to change: remove the protocol setting (SslProtocols.None = OS default, Microsoft's recommendation) or use Tls12 | Tls13. In appsettings.json use "SslProtocols": [ "Tls12", "Tls13" ] or drop the key. A CipherSuitesPolicy can stay if it lists TLS 1.3 suites.
  • Side effects: allowing TLS 1.3 may change the cipher suite; check any compliance rule that names TLS 1.2 suites.

G. FIPS mode

  • .NET has no FIPS switch of its own on Linux; it uses whatever OpenSSL provider is active. On AL2023 in FIPS mode use update-crypto-policies --set FIPS:PQ, which AWS documents as enabling ML-KEM hybrids under the FIPS policy (AL2023). Documented; not run (no FIPS-enabled host).
  • Don't use the kit openssl-pq.cnf on a FIPS host: whether the FIPS provider stays active with it was not tested. Use FIPS:PQ.
  • Windows legacy FIPS mode (FipsAlgorithmPolicy): the ML-KEM groups are not available (Documented: supported groups).
  • Lambda has no FIPS switch for the managed runtime; FIPS endpoints (*-fips) are a separate topic (AWS service endpoints chapter).

H. Libraries with their own TLS stack

  • Confluent.Kafka (librdkafka). Ships its own statically linked OpenSSL, so the OS image, crypto policy and OPENSSL_CONF don't apply (whether it reads OPENSSL_CONF is not confirmed); ssl.curves.list controls the groups. Tested on aspnet:8.0 (Debian 12, OS OpenSSL 3.0): librdkafka 2.16.0 logged Using statically linked OpenSSL version OpenSSL 3.5.8. The saved output was cut before the handshake line, so the PQ result itself, and the ssl.curves.list=X25519 negative, are not in the record: Unverified until rerun. Ubuntu 24.04 was not run. On 10.0-alpine it failed to load its native library (DllNotFoundException), a packaging issue not investigated. librdkafka's changelog says it bundles OpenSSL 3.5.x from 2.14.2 (Documented, changelog); upgrade below that.
// Confluent.Kafka: pin the group order explicitly so it is visible in config reviews.
var cfg = new ProducerConfig { BootstrapServers = brokers, SecurityProtocol = SecurityProtocol.Ssl };
cfg.Set("ssl.curves.list", "X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256");
  • Grpc.Core (legacy, BoringSSL): out of support; migrate to Grpc.Net.Client.
  • Microsoft.Data.SqlClient, Oracle.ManagedDataAccess: believed to use SslStream on Linux and so follow the OS. Unverified (not tested, source not checked).
  • APM agents (OpenTelemetry exporters, Datadog, New Relic): export over HttpClient or gRPC .NET, so they should follow the OS. Unverified.
  • Shelling out to curl, git, wget: Alpine and resolute curl use OpenSSL 3.5 (PQ). GnuTLS builds (Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04 git's libcurl-gnutls) have no ML-KEM (lead's finding, not rerun here). Check curl -V.
  • macOS developer machines: Apple TLS. AWS says PQ needs .NET 10 with the Network.framework opt-in (AWS, Microsoft). Documented; not tested.

I. Pinning, HSM/PKCS#11, TPM and KMS-backed keys

  • Certificate or public-key pinning (comparing GetPublicKeyString() or a SPKI hash): unaffected; PQ key exchange does not change the server certificate. Pins break only when the certificate changes, for example a later move to ML-DSA. Reasoning from the TLS 1.3 design, consistent with cases C/D; no pinning test run.
  • Keys in an HSM or TPM on Linux: .NET 9+ can open a key through an OpenSSL provider with SafeEvpPKeyHandle.OpenKeyFromProvider (API). Key exchange is still done in software by OpenSSL, so PQ applies the same way. Using such a key as a Kestrel certificate key was not tested.
  • Windows CNG / smart-card / HSM keys: Schannel uses them for signing; group choice is the machine curve order. Not tested.
  • AWS KMS asymmetric keys: .NET has no built-in way to use a KMS key as a TLS private key. Use ACM certificates on the load balancer, or ACM exportable certificates. Calls to KMS itself are PQ when the client is.

J. Other cases

  • HTTPS proxy (HTTPS_PROXY): HttpClient opens a CONNECT tunnel and does TLS end to end with the target, so the target's group applies. A TLS-intercepting proxy terminates TLS and its stack decides (Network devices and egress chapter). Not tested.

  • WebSockets (ClientWebSocket, SignalR): use SocketsHttpHandler; same as case A. Not run.

  • Service mesh sidecar (Istio, Linkerd, Envoy): if the app talks plain HTTP to the sidecar, the sidecar decides that leg (Service mesh chapter).

  • Lambda pq-egress extension: the function sends plain HTTP to http://127.0.0.1:2779/<route>/<path> and the extension (Go) makes the PQ connection. An interim option when the function cannot be rebuilt soon (Tested, e2e *-extension, *-combined; not tested with AWS SDK calls). Compute chapter.

  • Windows hosts (IIS, HTTP.sys, Kestrel, HttpClient, .NET Framework): all use Schannel.

    # Windows Server 2025 / Windows 11 24H2+, July 2026 cumulative update or later
Enable-TlsEccCurve -Name 'X25519_MLKEM768' -Position 0
Get-TlsEccCurve
    • Status: Microsoft announced Schannel hybrid ML-KEM as GA in Windows 11 and Windows Server 2025 on 2026-07-14, disabled by default (post). The Server 2025 update is KB5099536 (2026-07-14) per secondary sources; the Windows 11 KB number is not confirmed here. Microsoft's supported-groups page (updated 2026-10-20) still names Insider builds as the minimum, so check your build before relying on it. Not sure which statement is current.
    • Group Policy route: Computer Configuration > Administrative Templates > Network > SSL Configuration Settings > ECC Curve Order, X25519_MLKEM768 first. A GPO overrides the cmdlet; a new order takes effect after reboot (Manage TLS).
    • Limits: TLS 1.3 only, disabled by default, not available in legacy FIPS mode (supported groups).
    • .NET version: AWS lists PQ on Windows from .NET 10. Whether .NET 8 or .NET Framework on a patched host picks up the group is not confirmed (Schannel is machine-wide, so it is likely; Unverified).

Solution and automation

  1. Inventory. Per repo: base image (grep -rn "^FROM" Dockerfile*), target framework (grep -rn "<TargetFramework" --include=*.csproj), Lambda runtime (Runtime: dotnet8 in SAM/CFN, runtime = "dotnet8" in Terraform), and the case F blockers (check-dotnet-pq.sh). Per running container: cat /etc/os-release; openssl version (chiseled images have no shell: check the tag).

  2. Change, per compute type: the row in Quick start. Details:

    • Lambda zip: add pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf with CopyToPublishDirectory so it lands at /var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq.cnf; set OPENSSL_CONF in SAM, CloudFormation or Terraform. If the function already sets OPENSSL_CONF, merge and review rather than overwrite.
    • Lambda Native AOT: same file and variable. Build on AL2023 so glibc matches.
    • Containers: prefer aspnet:10.0-alpine, 10.0-resolute or 10.0-resolute-chiseled (no variable needed). For .NET 8 on glibc: AL2023 base + config file + ENV OPENSSL_CONF. Pin the image by digest.
    • EC2 AL2023: SSM document running update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ then restarting services, or Environment=OPENSSL_CONF=... in one service's systemd unit.
    • EC2 Windows: July 2026 cumulative update or later, then Enable-PqTls.ps1 through SSM Run Command (or the GPO), reboot.

    Dockerfiles, csproj files, SAM, CloudFormation, Terraform, ECS task definition, EKS manifest, systemd unit, SSM documents and the PowerShell script are in Full configuration files and configs/dotnet/.

  3. CI guardrail. Run check-dotnet-pq.sh in every pipeline. It fails on SslProtocols pinned to TLS 1.2 or lower without Tls13 (code and appsettings), MySqlConnector TlsVersion=TLS 1.2, ServicePointManager.SecurityProtocol without Tls13/SystemDefault, and base images with OpenSSL below 3.5 (aspnet:8.0, 10.0, -noble, -jammy, -bookworm-slim, -azurelinux3.0). It warns on CipherSuitesPolicy, ssl.curves.list, Grpc.Core, OPENSSL_CONF and disabled certificate validation. Tested on fixtures (tests/deep/dotnet/v2/ci/fixtures): bad tree exit 1 with 4 findings, good tree exit 0.

How to test

  1. Posture. Image or host: cat /etc/os-release; openssl version (3.5+ needed). AL2023: echo $OPENSSL_CONF or update-crypto-policies --show (DEFAULT:PQ). Windows: Get-TlsEccCurve lists X25519_MLKEM768 first. Repo: sh check-dotnet-pq.sh . exits 0.
  2. Harness. One container runs four OpenSSL 3.5 servers with your private CA's certificate: PQ-only (8443), classical-only (8444), and the same two requiring a client certificate (8445, 8446). See run-cases.sh (Full configuration files).
  3. Client tests (cases A, D, E, F). Run your app's own call, or the probe kclient.dll, against all four ports. Pass = PQ-only and classical-only both return 200; the cert-required ports return 200 only with your client certificate. Script: client-cases.sh.
  4. Server tests (cases B, C, E, F). Start your Kestrel service with its real certificate config, then from an OpenSSL 3.5 container:
printf 'GET / HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: api.internal\r\nConnection: close\r\n\r\n' | \
  openssl s_client -connect api.internal:8443 -servername api.internal -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 \
  -CAfile ca.crt -verify_return_error -brief -ign_eof   # expect: Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768
# fallback: same with -groups X25519 (and no -tls1_3): must also succeed
# mTLS: add -cert client.crt -key client.key; without them the request must be refused
# every SNI name you serve: repeat with each -servername
  1. Negative control. Same tests on the old image or without OPENSSL_CONF: PQ-only must fail, classical must pass. This proves the test can tell the difference.
  2. In the cloud. .NET doesn't expose the negotiated group (ITlsHandshakeFeature shows protocol and cipher only), so app logs can't prove PQ. Use CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager and ACM calls from the service role (expect X25519MLKEM768), the database or server side, or load balancer and front-door logs (their chapters).
  3. Rollback test. Remove the variable or revert the image in a canary and rerun step 3: PQ-only fails again, classical still passes.

Rollout checklist

# Step Done when
1 Inventory every .NET workload: compute, image tag or Lambda runtime, target framework, OPENSSL_CONF already set, Windows hosts One row per service
2 Add check-dotnet-pq.sh to CI in warn mode, then fail mode Pipelines green with no TLS 1.2 pins
3 Remove SslProtocols/TlsVersion TLS 1.2 caps (case F) Grep and CI clean
4 Pick the fix per row of Quick start; for Lambda dotnet8 also schedule the move to dotnet10 (blocked from 2027-07-29 create, 2027-08-31 update) Plan per service
5 Lambda code that opens an X509Store: add the HOME=/tmp exec wrapper, or load the PFX/PEM directly Client-cert call works with the fix on
6 Musl check before moving to Alpine: native packages (Confluent.Kafka), ICU, DNS. Use 10.0-resolute if any fail Service tests pass on the new image
7 Canary one service per compute type with the image or variable change only, no app code Harness: PQ-only and classical both pass
8 Watch 24 h: error rate, TLS failures, CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for the role X25519MLKEM768 seen, no new errors
9 Roll out in waves (10%, 50%, 100%): ECS/EKS rolling updates, Lambda alias weights, SSM rate control for EC2 All services PQ
10 Windows: patch, enable the group by GPO or SSM, reboot in maintenance windows Get-TlsEccCurve shows it first
11 Keep the rollback ready (next section) and test it once in the canary PQ-only fails again after rollback

Pitfalls and rollback

  • Default image tags are not PQ. 8.0 (Debian 12), 10.0 (Ubuntu 24.04) and -noble ship OpenSSL 3.0; Azure Linux 3.0 ships 3.3. No variable fixes OpenSSL below 3.5. Change the image.
  • Tests against classical servers hide the problem. Every certificate and trust case passed classically on Debian 12 (Tested). Always test against a PQ-only server.
  • The kit config file is AL2023-only. It includes /etc/crypto-policies/..., which doesn't exist on Debian, Ubuntu or Alpine.
  • OPENSSL_CONF replaces the system config. The kit file re-includes the AL2023 policy; a hand-written file that doesn't can drop provider or FIPS settings.
  • The kit config file adds ML-KEM, not ML-DSA. With it, ML-DSA-only servers still fail (Tested, e2e). Hosts that need ML-DSA certificates should use DEFAULT:PQ or a native OpenSSL 3.5 image.
  • TLS 1.2 pins (handler, Kestrel, MySQL TlsVersion, Redis SslProtocols) disable PQ without an error against servers that also accept 1.2. A TLS 1.2-only Kestrel with an EC certificate also refused clients whose group list lacks P-256 (F3b).
  • Alpine is musl. Test native dependencies (Confluent.Kafka failed to load here), ICU and DNS before switching; 10.0-resolute is the glibc alternative.
  • X509Store on Linux lives under $HOME; a read-only root filesystem, a new container or Lambda loses it unless mounted, re-imported or HOME is writable (case C).
  • CreateFromPemFile(caPath) with one argument throws for a CA-only file. Use CreateFromPem(File.ReadAllText(path)) (Tested).
  • Windows: the group order is machine-wide; a GPO overrides local cmdlet changes, and changes take effect after reboot.
  • Classical servers keep working. Every PQ client in the tests still connected to X25519-only and TLS 1.2-only servers, so the change is low risk for outbound calls.

Rollback, per compute type; no certificate or store to roll back, because none changed:

Compute Rollback
Lambda zip / AOT Remove OPENSSL_CONF (layer: PQ_TLS_DISABLE=1), or shift the alias back
Lambda image Redeploy the previous image
ECS, EKS Previous task definition revision or kubectl rollout undo (reverts the FROM line or variable)
EC2 AL2023 update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT or remove the unit's Environment= line; restart services
Windows Disable-TlsEccCurve -Name 'X25519_MLKEM768' or remove it from the GPO; reboot

Each client case connected to the observer's listeners: hybrid, PQ-only, mTLS, EC certificate, TLS 1.2-only, and three ML-DSA certificate targets.
