No libpq connection parameter for groups was found (not confirmed); use OPENSSL_CONF (see openssl-groups.cnf in Full configuration files) if you must change the client's list.

Clients linked to GnuTLS instead of OpenSSL get no ML-KEM, even on Debian 13 or Ubuntu 26.04: wget and libcurl-gnutls fail against a PQ-only server (Tested by another group for this document). Run ldd on any database client or tool before relying on the OS version.

Server side

RDS for PostgreSQL 18 and Aurora PostgreSQL 18

  • Parameter group family postgres18 (RDS instance) or aurora-postgresql18 (Aurora, cluster parameter group).

  • ssl_groups: hybrid first, classical after for fallback: X25519MLKEM768,SecP256r1MLKEM768,X25519,prime256v1. Use commas in the API, Terraform and CloudFormation (Claimed: the RDS API rejects colon lists).

  • rds.force_ssl = 1 (default on 15+).

  • To enforce PQ: ssl_groups = X25519MLKEM768,SecP256r1MLKEM768 and ssl_min_protocol_version = TLSv1.3, because ML-KEM needs TLS 1.3 (Documented).

aws rds modify-db-parameter-group --db-parameter-group-name pg18-pq \
  --parameters "ParameterName=ssl_groups,ParameterValue='X25519MLKEM768,SecP256r1MLKEM768,X25519,prime256v1',ApplyMethod=immediate"

Full Terraform and CloudFormation are in Full configuration files.

RDS and Aurora MySQL, RDS for MariaDB

  • Aurora MySQL: upgrade to 8.4.8+ (8.4-compatible line) or 3.14.0+ (8.0-compatible line); no parameter. Check SHOW STATUS LIKE 'Aurora_ssl_named_group'. Keep require_secure_transport = ON and tls_version = TLSv1.2,TLSv1.3; TLS 1.2 clients stay classical.

  • RDS for MySQL 8.4.11 and RDS for MariaDB (12.3.3, 11.4.13, 11.8.9): upgrade the minor version. The enabling parameter is not confirmed here. After the upgrade, list the parameter group for a groups or PQ parameter and probe with s_client (Verify).

Self-managed PostgreSQL 18 (Tested)

# postgresql.conf (excerpt)
ssl = on
ssl_groups = 'X25519MLKEM768:X25519:prime256v1'   # PG 18 name; ssl_ecdh_curve is an alias
ssl_min_protocol_version = 'TLSv1.2'               # 'TLSv1.3' to enforce PQ

hostssl lines in pg_hba.conf reject non-TLS clients. SELECT pg_reload_conf(); applies TLS settings to new connections.

Self-managed MySQL

  • 8.4 LTS and 9.x: no PQ on the server, even linked to OpenSSL 3.5.8 (Tested). Plan for MySQL 26.x.

  • 26.x: PQ on by default (Tested). Variables (Documented, MySQL 26.10 manual): tls_kex (colon list, case-sensitive names), force_pqc, use_pqc_sign, each with admin_, replication_, group_replication_ and mysqlx_ variants. Status: Tls_key_exchange_algorithm, Tls_sign_algorithm.

  • Version history: server-side PQC (PQ groups, force_pqc) was added in MySQL 26.7.0 (MySQL 26.7 manual, Documented); 26.10 adds the MySQL Shell and client options tls-kex and force-pqc. Any 26.7+ server qualifies; the tests here used 26.10.

# my.cnf (excerpt)
[mysqld]
require_secure_transport = ON
tls_version = TLSv1.2,TLSv1.3
tls_kex = X25519MLKEM768:secp256r1MLKEM768:X25519:secp256r1
# force_pqc = ON          # enforcement: classical clients fail (Tested)

Self-managed MariaDB, Valkey, Redis OSS

No group option of their own was found. They follow OpenSSL's defaults, which on OpenSSL 3.5 put X25519MLKEM768 first. To change the order or enforce PQ-only, point OPENSSL_CONF at a file with Groups = ... (Tested on Valkey 8.1.11: the PQ-only file rejected an X25519-only client).

# valkey.conf (excerpt)
port 0
tls-port 6379
tls-cert-file /etc/valkey/tls/server.crt
tls-key-file  /etc/valkey/tls/server.key
tls-ca-cert-file /etc/valkey/tls/ca.crt
tls-protocols "TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3"

Kafka and Cassandra (JVM)

The broker's JDK decides. Run JDK 25.0.5+ (PQ by default) or attach the Bouncy Castle JSSE agent from the Java tab. Kafka: ssl.enabled.protocols=TLSv1.3,TLSv1.2. To pin groups, set -Djdk.tls.namedGroups=X25519MLKEM768,x25519,secp256r1 in KAFKA_OPTS. Not tested on a broker (Unverified).

Packaging per compute type

Database servers do not run on Lambda; database clients do, so follow the language chapter for each runtime. For engines you run yourself:

Where the engine runsWhat to shipEvidence
ECS/Fargate or EKSAn image with OpenSSL 3.5: postgres:18 (Debian 13), mysql:26, mariadb:13 (Ubuntu 26.04), valkey/valkey:8 or :8-alpine. Pin by digest. Config by container args, file mount or baked-in file.Tested (images)
EC2 on AL2023OpenSSL 3.5 is present but the crypto policy omits ML-KEM (see Hosts and OS). Set ssl_groups explicitly (PostgreSQL) or OPENSSL_CONF in the service's systemd drop-in.Unverified for database daemons on AL2023
Client containersDebian 13 or Alpine 3.24 bases; avoid -bookworm tags (OpenSSL 3.0)Tested

The EKS StatefulSet, ECS task definition and systemd drop-in are in Full configuration files.

Verify

  1. Posture: engine version and parameter values.

aws rds describe-db-parameters --db-parameter-group-name pg18-pq \
  --query "Parameters[?ParameterName=='ssl_groups'].[ParameterValue]" --output text
psql "host=$H sslmode=verify-full sslrootcert=global-bundle.pem" -c "SHOW ssl_groups"

  1. Live test from a host with OpenSSL 3.5, for example a debian:trixie-slim task in the same VPC. PQ must succeed; classical must still work (fallback).

# PostgreSQL
openssl s_client -connect $H:5432 -starttls postgres -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -brief </dev/null
openssl s_client -connect $H:5432 -starttls postgres -groups X25519 -brief </dev/null
# MySQL / MariaDB
openssl s_client -connect $H:3306 -starttls mysql -groups X25519MLKEM768 -brief </dev/null
# Valkey / Redis (direct TLS)
openssl s_client -connect $H:6379 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -brief </dev/null

Expect Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768 for the PQ run and Peer Temp Key: X25519 for the classical one (Tested on every self-managed engine above).

  1. Observed signal on the server:

  • PostgreSQL: log_connections prints protocol, cipher and bits, not the group (Tested); pg_stat_ssl has no group column. Use the probe.

  • MySQL 26: SHOW SESSION STATUS LIKE 'Tls_key_exchange_algorithm' (Tested). Aurora MySQL: Aurora_ssl_named_group (Documented in the 3.14.0 notes).

  • Valkey, MariaDB: no server-side field; use the probe.

Pitfalls and rollback

  • Fallback is silent. A Debian 12 libpq client still connects to a PQ-first server using X25519 (Tested). Only enforcement (PQ-only groups, force_pqc) exposes it, by breaking it.

  • Server order is not always obeyed. With OpenSSL 3.5, a client that sent only an X25519 key share got X25519 although the server listed ML-KEM first (Tested on Valkey); OpenSSL avoids a HelloRetryRequest. Fix the client, or enforce.

  • Self-managed MySQL 8.4 and 9.x cannot be fixed with OpenSSL 3.5 alone; the server sticks to P-256 (Tested).

  • Official MariaDB images before 13 run on Ubuntu 24.04 with OpenSSL 3.0: no PQ (Tested).

  • RDS Proxy terminates TLS; that leg stays classical. Bypass it for sensitive data or record an exception.

  • ML-KEM needs TLS 1.3. Clients or servers pinned to TLS 1.2 stay classical.

  • Rollback: set ssl_groups back to prime256v1:X25519 (dynamic, new connections only), set force_pqc=OFF, or remove OPENSSL_CONF and restart. Existing sessions are not dropped.

Configuration files

The complete files for this chapter are in the repository under configs/databases-and-data-services/. This paper shows only the main change from each; copy from the repository, not from the paper. 12 files:

7.11 Hosts and operating systems

What decides PQ here

The host OS decides PQ only for programs that use the system TLS library: OpenSSL, GnuTLS or NSS on Linux, Schannel on Windows, Apple's stack on macOS. On Red Hat-family systems (AL2023, RHEL) the system-wide crypto policy writes the group list for all three Linux libraries and for OpenSSH. Programs that bring their own TLS (Go, Java, the official Node.js binaries, rustls, AWS CLI v2) follow the language chapters whatever the host is.

Status matrix

Tested = run here on 2026-11-14 in containers against a PQ-only openssl s_server (-groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3) and a classical-only one (-groups X25519), plus sshd restricted to one key exchange. Scripts and output: deep/hosts/run-hosts*.sh and run-hosts*.out in the work dir.

Bottlerocket

Bottlerocket has no package manager and no crypto-policies tool, and I couldn't confirm which OpenSSL version its core kit ships (searched release notes 2026-11-14). That matters little in practice:

  • Workload TLS comes from the container image, not the host.

  • Host agents (containerd, kubelet, ecs-agent, the SSM agent in the control container) are Go programs; current Go builds offer X25519MLKEM768 by default. Not confirmed per Bottlerocket version.

  • The admin and control containers can't be retuned in a supported way. Treat any TLS they make as unknown until Bottlerocket documents it.

Not testable here (needs a Bottlerocket VM).

macOS command-line curlLibreSSL 3.3.6Use a curl linked to OpenSSL 3.5 (Homebrew, or a container)Tested (fails on PQ-only)

Per-service override with systemd

On AL2023, to change one service and not the whole host, point that unit at the PQ config. Tested equivalent: with the policy at DEFAULT, curl run with OPENSSL_CONF=/tmp/openssl-pq.cnf negotiated X25519MLKEM768 and still reached the classical server.

# /etc/systemd/system/myapp.service.d/pq-tls.conf (excerpt)
[Service]
Environment=OPENSSL_CONF=/etc/pki/tls/openssl-pq.cnf

Then systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl restart myapp. Only OpenSSL-linked programs read this variable.

OpenSSH client

OpenSSH ≥ 10.0 puts mlkem768x25519-sha256 first by default (OpenSSH PQ); nothing to set. For 9.9 hosts, or to pin the order:

# ~/.ssh/config or /etc/ssh/ssh_config.d/40-pq-kex.conf (excerpt)
Host *
    KexAlgorithms mlkem768x25519-sha256,sntrup761x25519-sha512@openssh.com,curve25519-sha256,curve25519-sha256@libssh.org

OpenSSH before 9.9 rejects the unknown name and exits; on 9.0–9.8 (Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 12) put sntrup761x25519-sha512@openssh.com first instead. sntrup761 is not a NIST algorithm; use it only as an interim step.

Server side

Amazon Linux 2023: crypto policy

  1. Patch to AL2023.12 or later.

  2. dnf -y install crypto-policies-scripts && dnf -y update crypto-policies crypto-policies-scripts.

  3. update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ (FIPS:PQ on FIPS hosts).

  4. Restart services, or reboot. Already-running processes keep the old list.

Tested on AL2023.12.20261102. At DEFAULT, OpenSSL's Groups line is *X25519:secp256r1:… and both curl and s_client fail on the PQ-only server. At DEFAULT:PQ it becomes *?X25519MLKEM768:?x25519_mlkem768:?SecP256r1MLKEM768:…/*X25519:…; curl reports X25519MLKEM768; the classical server still works; sshd -T and the ssh client list mlkem768x25519-sha256 first; GnuTLS lists GROUP-X25519-MLKEM768 first.

DEFAULT:PQ also enables ML-DSA signatures and the P-256/P-384 ML-KEM hybrids. For the smallest change, use a custom subpolicy that adds only the X25519 hybrid group:

# /etc/crypto-policies/policies/modules/PQ-HYBRID.pmod
group = +X25519-MLKEM768

Apply with update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ-HYBRID. Tested: OpenSSL's Groups line became *?X25519MLKEM768:?x25519_mlkem768/*X25519:… and curl negotiated X25519MLKEM768. (AL2023's own PQ.pmod spells the group MLKEM768-X25519; the tested spelling above also works.)

OpenSSH server

HostWhat to doEvidence
AL2023DEFAULT:PQ (the policy writes opensshserver.config)Tested (sshd -T)
Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04, Alpine 3.24, RHEL 10.1Nothing: OpenSSH 10.x prefers ML-KEM by defaultDocumented; client side Tested
Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 12Upgrade the OS; sntrup761 meanwhileTested

To restrict a server to PQ only (a bastion, or an SFTP host for PQ clients):

# /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/40-pq-kex.conf (excerpt; not on AL2023, where the policy owns this)
KexAlgorithms mlkem768x25519-sha256,sntrup761x25519-sha512@openssh.com

Tested on Debian 13 sshd with -o KexAlgorithms=mlkem768x25519-sha256: OpenSSH 9.9+ clients connected with ML-KEM; Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 12 and AL2023-at-DEFAULT clients failed with no matching key exchange method found.

Session Manager and EC2 Instance Connect

  • Session Manager doesn't use sshd; the SSM agent opens a TLS WebSocket to ssmmessages. The agent is Go 1.26 (offers PQ) but that endpoint isn't on AWS's PQ list. Assume classical (AWS service endpoints).

  • EC2 Instance Connect pushes a key through a PQ-capable API, then you SSH to the instance's sshd: the sshd rules above apply.

Web servers and other daemons on the host

nginx, Apache httpd, HAProxy and Postfix linked to the system OpenSSL follow the crypto policy unless their config sets groups. Server config: Web servers and proxies.

Windows Server 2025 (Schannel)

  1. Install KB5099536 (2026-07-14) or later.

  2. Enable-TlsEccCurve -Name "X25519_MLKEM768" -Position 0 (elevated). Microsoft says position 0 is needed for it to be negotiated.

  3. TLS 1.3 must be on. Group Policy overrides the PowerShell setting, so pick one ("don't mix-n-match", Microsoft).

  4. Restart services that hold TLS contexts. Whether a reboot is needed isn't stated; not confirmed.

Windows Server 2022 and 2019 have no announced PQ Schannel. For PQ on those hosts, terminate TLS in front of them (Load balancers, Edge and API front doors) or move to 2025.

Packaging and rollout per compute type

ComputeMechanismFiles (last section)

Rollback: retag instances PQRollout=rollback. The rollback association sets DEFAULT and restarts the listed units.

Verify

  1. Posture (SSM Run Command or Inventory): update-crypto-policies --show = DEFAULT:PQ; grep Groups /etc/crypto-policies/back-ends/opensslcnf.config starts with *?X25519MLKEM768; sshd -T | grep ^kexalgorithms starts with mlkem768x25519-sha256; rpm -q openssl-libs openssh-server (≥ 3.5, ≥ 9.9). Windows: Get-TlsEccCurve lists X25519_MLKEM768 first.

  2. Live: from the host, openssl s_client -connect pq-test:443 -brief (no -groups, so the policy is what's tested) must print Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768; a classical server must still connect. ssh -v host true 2>&1 | grep 'kex: algorithm' must print mlkem768x25519-sha256.

  3. Observed: CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for the host's AWS calls made by a system-OpenSSL client; Zeek on VPC Traffic Mirroring for other host traffic; ALB connection logs for calls to your ALBs.

Test results (2026-11-14)

CheckResult
AL2023 DEFAULT → PQ-only server (curl, s_client)Fail (alert 40), classical OK
AL2023 DEFAULT:PQ → PQ-only (curl, s_client, wget, gnutls-cli)Pass X25519MLKEM768; classical OK
AL2023 DEFAULT:PQ-HYBRID custom subpolicyPass; classical OK
AL2023 rollback to DEFAULTPQ-only fails again
AL2023 DEFAULT + OPENSSL_CONF=openssl-pq.cnf (curl)Pass; classical OK
ssh to ML-KEM-only sshd from Debian 13, Ubuntu 26.04, Alpine 3.24, Alpine 3.21, AL2023 DEFAULT:PQPass mlkem768x25519-sha256
ssh to ML-KEM-only sshd from Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 12, AL2023 DEFAULTFail (no matching kex)
wget/git on Debian 13, wget on Ubuntu 26.04 (GnuTLS) → PQ-onlyFail
macOS 27.0.1 /usr/bin/curl → PQ-onlyFail (LibreSSL 3.3.6)

Pitfalls and rollback

  • DEFAULT:PQ changes every OpenSSL, GnuTLS, NSS and OpenSSH program at once, including dnf, agent helpers and monitoring agents. Roll out in waves by tag.

  • Rollback: update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT, then restart. Tested: the PQ-only server failed again right after rollback.

  • Debian/Ubuntu GnuTLS programs stay classical even on Debian 13 and Ubuntu 26.04 (Tested). Don't assume wget or git follow curl.

  • OpenSSH config: mlkem768x25519-sha256 in ssh_config on OpenSSH < 9.9 breaks every ssh call. Gate by version.

  • Middleboxes: the larger ClientHello can be dropped by old inspection devices. Test each fleet's egress path before its wave (Network devices and egress).

  • AL2 hosts can't be fixed. They're past end of support (2026-06-30); migrate to AL2023.

7.12 Certificates and signatures (ML-DSA)

What decides PQ here

ML-DSA (FIPS 204) replaces the signatures in certificates; it is the second step after ML-KEM key exchange. It is less urgent: recorded traffic can be decrypted later, but a signature only has to hold at the moment of the handshake. Today ML-DSA works in private PKI only.

Three parties decide whether a handshake uses an ML-DSA certificate. The CA must sign with ML-DSA. The server must hold an ML-DSA certificate and a TLS library that can sign with it (OpenSSL 3.5+). The client must offer ML-DSA in its signature_algorithms extension and be able to verify the chain. A server that also holds an ECDSA certificate falls back to it for clients that don't offer ML-DSA; that dual-certificate setup is the safe way to start (Tested 2026-11-14).

ML-KEM (key exchange)ML-DSA (signatures, certificates)
ThreatTraffic recorded today, decrypted laterForged certificates once a quantum computer exists
UrgencyNowLater
What changesTLS libraries and config at both endsThe CA, every certificate, every verifier
Where it worksMost of this documentPrivate PKI only

Status matrix

AWS services

ServiceML-DSAEvidence
AWS Private CACA and end-entity certificates. KeyAlgorithm and SigningAlgorithm ML_DSA_44, ML_DSA_65, ML_DSA_87 (the same value in both). All commercial, GovCloud and China regions since November 2025.Documented (algorithms, API)
AWS KMSSigning keys ML_DSA_44/65/87, algorithm ML_DSA_SHAKE_256; RAW messages up to 4 KB, EXTERNAL_MU for largerDocumented (KMS ML-DSA)
IAM Roles AnywhereML-DSA trust anchors and end-entity certificates (March 2026)Documented
ACM public certificates; ALB, NLB, CloudFront, API Gateway listenersRSA and ECDSA only; cannot serve ML-DSAClaimed (third-party report)
ACM private certificates (from Private CA)ACM supports "some but not all" Private CA algorithms; ML-DSA through ACM not confirmedDocumented (the caveat); ML-DSA Unverified
AWS CloudHSMAWS's PQC migration page lists ML-DSA as preview; GA not confirmedUnverified
Public web PKI and browsersNo public CA issues ML-DSA; browsers don't accept it; Chrome is pursuing Merkle Tree CertificatesClaimed

Clients and runtimes

Tested 2026-11-14 against nginx 1.29 (OpenSSL 3.5.5) holding an ML-DSA-44 chain and an ECDSA P-256 chain.

ClientTLS: offers ML-DSA? Leaf receivedOffline ML-DSA chain verificationEvidence

OpenSSL 3.5 with -sigalgs ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256No; ECDSA leaf–Tested

End to end (run r1117034807, 2026-11-16): 409 client cases on Lambda, ECS Fargate, ECS on EC2 and EC2 against a dual-certificate server, an ML-DSA-only server and a server requiring an ML-DSA client certificate. Results per client stack are in the overview’s Use cases section; every client that connects at all connected to the dual server.

Size cost (Tested, DER): ML-DSA-87 root 7,510 bytes, ML-DSA-65 intermediate 6,876, ML-DSA-44 leaf 4,953; ECDSA P-256 root 424 and leaf 464. Leaf plus intermediate grows from about 0.9 KB to 11.8 KB. An ML-DSA-44 signature is 2,420 bytes and its public key 1,312 bytes, against 64 bytes for an ECDSA P-256 signature.

Client side (app level)

A client needs the private root in its trust store and, to receive the ML-DSA certificate, a TLS stack that offers ML-DSA. Clients that have only the root still work against a dual-certificate server; they receive ECDSA.

RuntimeTrust the private rootNotes
OpenSSL tools, curl, libpq, Valkey--cacert / sslrootcert=; or the system store: Debian/Alpine /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/*.crt + update-ca-certificates; AL2023 /etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/ + update-ca-trustOpenSSL 3.5 offers mldsa44/65/87 by default (seen in a ClientHello trace, Tested)
Pythonssl.create_default_context(cafile=...); SSL_CERT_FILE; requests: REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE (certifi has no private roots)Python must link OpenSSL 3.5 (Python chapter)
Node.jstls.connect({ ca }); NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS=/path/root.pemNode 24 bundles OpenSSL 3.5 (Tested)
Gox509.NewCertPool() + AppendCertsFromPEM; SSL_CERT_FILEGo 1.27 verifies ML-DSA chains; crypto/tls never selects ML-DSA, so the server must also hold ECDSA
JavaPKCS12 truststore (javax.net.ssl.trustStore)Stock JDK 25 validates ML-DSA chains, but JSSE negotiates ECDSA
.NETX509ChainPolicy.CustomTrustStore with TrustMode = CustomRootTrust, or the OS storeLinux needs OpenSSL 3.5; macOS has no ML-DSA in .NET 10 (Claimed)

.NET 10 validation callback (Tested). Copy the server-sent intermediates into ExtraStore when you rebuild the chain, or the build fails:

c.ChainPolicy.TrustMode = X509ChainTrustMode.CustomRootTrust;
c.ChainPolicy.CustomTrustStore.AddRange(roots);
foreach (var el in ch!.ChainPolicy.ExtraStore) c.ChainPolicy.ExtraStore.Add(el);
bool ok = c.Build(cert!);

Server side

nginx: ML-DSA and ECDSA side by side (Tested)

ssl_certificate     /etc/nginx/pki/mldsa-fullchain.crt;
ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/pki/mldsa-leaf.key;
ssl_certificate     /etc/nginx/pki/ec-fullchain.crt;
ssl_certificate_key /etc/nginx/pki/ec-leaf.key;
ssl_ecdh_curve      X25519MLKEM768:X25519:prime256v1;

OpenSSL picks the certificate from the client's signature_algorithms. OpenSSL 3.5, Python, Node and .NET clients got ML-DSA; Go, Java and OpenSSL 3.0 clients got ECDSA. Key exchange stayed X25519MLKEM768 (or X25519 for OpenSSL 3.0). Full configs for other web servers: Web servers and proxies.

Apache httpd, HAProxy, Envoy

Apache accepts several SSLCertificateFile / SSLCertificateKeyFile pairs and HAProxy loads multi-type bundles, but neither was tested with ML-DSA here (Unverified). Envoy uses BoringSSL; ML-DSA serving not confirmed (Unverified). AWS load balancers cannot serve ML-DSA.

AWS Private CA

CLI flow (Documented values; not run here, no AWS credentials):

aws acm-pca create-certificate-authority --certificate-authority-type ROOT \
  --certificate-authority-configuration '{"KeyAlgorithm":"ML_DSA_65","SigningAlgorithm":"ML_DSA_65","Subject":{"CommonName":"Example ML-DSA Root"}}'
aws acm-pca get-certificate-authority-csr --certificate-authority-arn $CA --output text > ca.csr
aws acm-pca issue-certificate --certificate-authority-arn $CA --csr fileb://ca.csr \
  --signing-algorithm ML_DSA_65 --template-arn arn:aws:acm-pca:::template/RootCACertificate/V1 \
  --validity Value=10,Type=YEARS
# get-certificate, then import-certificate-authority-certificate to activate
openssl genpkey -algorithm ML-DSA-44 -out leaf.key
openssl req -new -key leaf.key -subj "/CN=svc.internal" -addext "subjectAltName=DNS:svc.internal" -out leaf.csr
aws acm-pca issue-certificate --certificate-authority-arn $CA --csr fileb://leaf.csr \
  --signing-algorithm ML_DSA_65 --template-arn arn:aws:acm-pca:::template/EndEntityCertificate/V1 \
  --validity Value=90,Type=DAYS

Private CA copies the SAN and public key from the CSR without validating them, so build the CSR carefully. A subordinate CA must use a key algorithm its parent supports (both Documented). Terraform and CloudFormation are in Full configuration files; whether your provider or resource schema version accepts ML_DSA_* is not confirmed (Unverified).

AWS KMS (ML-DSA signing, not TLS)

aws kms create-key --key-spec ML_DSA_65 --key-usage SIGN_VERIFY
aws kms sign --key-id $K --message fileb://msg.bin --message-type RAW \
  --signing-algorithm ML_DSA_SHAKE_256 --query Signature --output text | base64 -d > sig.bin
aws kms verify --key-id $K --message fileb://msg.bin --message-type RAW \
  --signing-algorithm ML_DSA_SHAKE_256 --signature fileb://sig.bin

Documented. Use it for code and artifact signing or as a KMS-backed CA key; it does not put ML-DSA into TLS by itself.

Composite and hybrid certificates

  • Composite ML-DSA for X.509 (ML-DSA plus RSA or ECDSA in one key and signature) was published as RFC 10073, Proposed Standard, November 2026 (Documented, datatracker).

  • .NET 10 has a CompositeMLDsa API that moved to draft-08 at GA (Documented, Microsoft Learn); check it matches the RFC before use.

  • OpenSSL 3.5 composite support: not confirmed (Unverified). AWS Private CA lists no composite algorithms.

  • The ML-DSA TLS code points (mldsa44 0x0904, mldsa65 0x0905, mldsa87 0x0906) come from draft-ietf-tls-mldsa, still a draft; OpenSSL 3.5 already sends them (Tested).

  • Until composites are widely supported, dual certificates (ML-DSA plus ECDSA) give the same fallback without a new format.

The CLM product's role

The CLM codebase already has ML-DSA plumbing: certificate policy (clm/internal/certpolicy/quantumsafe.go), HSM signing (clm/internal/hsm/craton_mldsa.go), the Nitro enclave signer (nitro-enclave/internal/pqc/signer.go), and composite and hybrid certificate tests. These were found by file name; the code paths weren't reviewed.

Split of responsibilities:

  • ML-KEM: discovery plus runtime and config fixes (the rest of this document).

  • ML-DSA: a CLM re-issuance campaign. Start with internal mutual TLS, service-to-service and code signing; public endpoints wait for the web PKI. CLM should issue the ML-DSA and ECDSA pair together so servers can run dual certificates.

Steps

  1. Finish ML-KEM rollout first; it protects against the urgent threat.

  2. Inventory certificate signature algorithms in CLM, starting with long-lived roots and intermediates.

  3. Build an ML-DSA hierarchy beside the existing one (Private CA or CLM) and add its root to every client trust store.

  4. Pilot dual certificates (ML-DSA plus ECDSA) on one internal mutual TLS path. Expect OpenSSL 3.5, Python, Node and .NET clients to take ML-DSA, and Go and Java to stay on ECDSA.

  5. Expand per path, keeping the ECDSA certificate until every verifier on the path negotiates ML-DSA.

  6. Public certificates when CA/Browser Forum rules and browsers allow.

Packaging per compute type

Clients need the private root in their trust store; servers need both key pairs.

ComputeClient trustServer certificatesEvidence
Lambda zipShip pki/roots.pem in the package; SSL_CERT_FILE=/var/task/pki/roots.pem (Python, Go), NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS=/var/task/pki/roots.pem (Node)n/aUnverified on Lambda
Lambda imageCOPY roots.pem plus the same variablesn/aUnverified
ECS/Fargate, EKSDebian 13 or Alpine 3.24 base; root into /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/ + update-ca-certificatesnginx (Debian 13) with both pairs from Secrets Manager or a mounted secretTested (nginx, clients); update-ca-certificates with an ML-DSA root Unverified
EC2 (AL2023)/etc/pki/ca-trust/source/anchors/ + update-ca-trustnginx on OpenSSL 3.5 with both pairsUnverified

Verify

# Which certificate does the server pick? (OpenSSL 3.5 client)
openssl s_client -connect web:443 -CAfile roots.pem -sigalgs mldsa44:mldsa65:mldsa87 </dev/null | grep -E 'Peer signature type|a:PKEY'
openssl s_client -connect web:443 -CAfile roots.pem -sigalgs ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256 </dev/null | grep -E 'Peer signature type|a:PKEY'
# Inventory: signature algorithm of a certificate on disk
openssl x509 -in cert.pem -noout -text | grep -E 'Signature Algorithm|Public Key Algorithm'

Expect Peer signature type: mldsa44 for the first and ecdsa_secp256r1_sha256 for the second (Tested). Old clients (OpenSSL 3.0, Go, Java) must still complete the handshake (Tested).

Pitfalls and rollback

  • Never serve only an ML-DSA certificate to a mixed fleet: Go, Java and OpenSSL 3.0 clients cannot negotiate it (Tested).

  • Amazon Linux 2023: the crypto policy lists only classical SignatureAlgorithms, so OpenSSL 3.5.8 never offers ML-DSA, even with the ML-KEM config file. A dual-certificate server sends ECDSA, and an ML-DSA-only server or an ML-DSA client certificate fails the handshake. Adding mldsa44:mldsa65:mldsa87 ahead of the policy list (openssl-pq-mldsa.cnf) fixes all three (Tested 2026-11-16, AL2023 Python 3.12). update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ also enables ML-DSA, and so does Node’s openssl-pq-node-system.cnf, which sets its own list (Tested, e2e).

  • .NET 8 negotiates ML-DSA server certificates through OpenSSL 3.5 but cannot load an ML-DSA private key, so it cannot present an ML-DSA client certificate (“not a known key algorithm”). .NET 10 can (Tested, e2e).

  • Chains grow about 13 times (0.9 KB to 11.8 KB). Watch MTU-sensitive paths, middleboxes, IoT devices and client-certificate size limits.

  • A certificate carries one algorithm. Re-issue; don't re-sign.

  • Rollback: remove the ML-DSA ssl_certificate pair and reload; ECDSA keeps serving. The ML-DSA root can stay in trust stores; it is harmless when unused.

Configuration files

The complete files for this chapter are in the repository under configs/certificates-ml-dsa/. This paper shows only the main change from each; copy from the repository, not from the paper. 9 files:

FileWhat it is

configs/certificates-ml-dsa/ca.extca.ext
configs/certificates-ml-dsa/openssl-pq-mldsa.cnfopenssl-pq-mldsa.cnf (AL2023: ML-KEM groups and ML-DSA signature algorithms, Tested)

7.13 Web application firewalls (WAF)

What decides PQ here

AWS WAF never terminates TLS. The resource it is attached to does: CloudFront, ALB, API Gateway REST, AppSync, Cognito, App Runner, Verified Access, Amplify or Bedrock AgentCore Gateway. So PQ for an AWS WAF-protected app is set on that resource (Load balancers, Edge and API front doors). Shield Advanced and Firewall Manager have no TLS role. Third-party WAFs that run as a reverse proxy (appliance, virtual appliance, SaaS/CDN) terminate TLS themselves, so their own TLS stack and profile decide both the client leg and the origin leg. Software WAF modules (ModSecurity, Coraza) inherit TLS from the host proxy.

Status matrix

WAFDeployment typeTerminates TLSClient leg PQOrigin leg PQHow to enableEvidence
AWS WAFManaged, attached to a resourceNoThe resource's policyThe resource'sSet the resource policyDocumented (protected resources)
AWS Shield AdvancedManaged DDoS serviceNon/an/an/aDocumented
AWS Firewall ManagerOrg-wide policy serviceNo; policy types are WAF, Shield Advanced, security groups, network ACLs, Network Firewall, third-party firewalls; none for TLS policiesn/an/aUse it to force WAF everywhere; enforce TLS policies elsewhereDocumented (FMS policies)
F5 BIG-IP Advanced WAF / LTMHardware appliance, VE on EC2YesX25519MLKEM768 in TLS 1.3 from 17.5.1; SecP256r/384r hybrids from 21.1Yes, server-ssl profileCipher rule with dh-groups, cipher group, client-ssl / server-ssl profileDocumented (F5 PQC)
NetScaler ADC (WAF)MPX, SDX, VPX on EC2, CPXYesX25519_MLKEM768, enhanced SSL profile onlyNot documented (page is "on the front end")bind ssl profile -eccCurveName X25519_MLKEM768Documented (NetScaler hybrid PQC)

Network firewalls that do TLS inspection (AWS Network Firewall, Palo Alto, FortiGate) are in Network devices and egress.

Client side: the WAF's connection to the origin

WAFOrigin-leg settingNotes
AWS WAFNone of its ownCloudFront origin leg is classical; ALB backend uses ELBSecurityPolicy-TLS13-1-0-PQ-2025-09 when the listener is PQ

Server side: configuration to enable

F5 BIG-IP (17.5.1+; tmsh from F5's PQC chapter; F5's sample output has naming inconsistencies, so check tmsh list after creating):

tmsh create ltm cipher rule PQC_RULE cipher DEFAULT dh-groups X25519MLKEM768:DEFAULT signature-algorithms DEFAULT
tmsh create ltm cipher group PQC_GROUP allow add { PQC_RULE }
tmsh create ltm profile client-ssl clientssl-pq cipher-group PQC_GROUP ciphers none options { dont-insert-empty-fragments }
tmsh create ltm profile server-ssl serverssl-pq cipher-group PQC_GROUP ciphers none

NetScaler ADC (enhanced SSL profile):

bind ssl profile pq_fe_profile -eccCurveName X25519_MLKEM768
# or rebind all curves with the hybrid first
bind ssl profile pq_fe_profile -eccCurveName ALL

FortiWeb 8.0.3+:

config server-policy policy
  edit "app-policy"
    set tls-v13 enable
    set tls-pqc-support enable
    set tls-pqc-groups x25519mlkem768 secp256r1mlkem768 secp384r1mlkem1024
  next
end

Cloudflare: nothing to set for visitors; for origins, keep Automatic key exchange on and make the origin offer X25519MLKEM768. Akamai: enable the PQC behaviors in Property Manager or Delivery Manager; TLS 1.3 must be on. AWS WAF: set the PQ policy on the protected resource.

Verify

AWS WAF logs carry no TLS version, cipher or key-exchange field. Fields include ja3Fingerprint (32 hex) and ja4Fingerprint (36 characters), computed only for CloudFront and ALB (log fields, Documented).

Can fingerprints find non-PQ clients? Not directly:

  • JA4 hashes the sorted extension type codes and signature algorithms. It includes the fact that supported_groups and key_share extensions exist, not their values, so a PQ and a classical build of the same client can share a JA4 (JA4 spec, Documented).

  • JA3's input string does include the supported groups list, but AWS WAF logs only the MD5 hash, so you cannot read the groups back.

  • Use them only as a lookup: find a classical client in the ALB connection log (tls_keyexchange), note its JA4 from the WAF log, then count or label that JA4 in WAF (the label-known-classical-client rule below, Count action). Correlation is by client IP and time, so treat it as a hint.

Use the resource's own logs for the real signal: ALB connection logs and NLB access logs (Load balancers), the CloudFront CloudFront-Viewer-TLS-Details header (Edge and API front doors). F5 has an open community request for logging the negotiated PQ group; no BIG-IP log field was found.

Live test for any WAF that terminates TLS (OpenSSL 3.5 client):

echo | openssl s_client -connect waf.example.com:443 -servername waf.example.com -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 2>&1 | grep 'Negotiated TLS1.3 group'
echo | openssl s_client -connect waf.example.com:443 -servername waf.example.com -groups X25519 2>&1 | grep Protocol

Tested here (2026-11-14): ModSecurity CRS nginx image, digest sha256:9a386aa4a9ee...: PQ client negotiated X25519MLKEM768; classical TLS 1.3 and TLS 1.2 clients connected; the WAF proxied to an openssl s_server -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 upstream with HTTP 200. 4 of 4 checks passed. Script and output: deep/edge/tests/run-modsec-pq.sh.

Pitfalls, enforcement and rollback

  • An AWS WAF rule cannot require PQ: no request component exposes the key exchange.

  • Firewall Manager can force a web ACL onto every ALB or CloudFront distribution, but not a TLS policy. Use the SCP and Config rules in Load balancers and Enforcement and guardrails.

  • Appliances: PQ needs TLS 1.3 on the profile; older code (BIG-IP before 17.5.1, FortiWeb before 8.0.3) has no ML-KEM, and BIG-IP 17.5.0 only had the draft X25519Kyber768.

  • Passive (out-of-band) WAFs that decrypt from a copied private key cannot read any (EC)DHE session, PQ or not. Move them inline.

  • SaaS WAFs: the client leg can be PQ while the origin leg is not; check both.

  • Rollback: remove the PQ group from the cipher rule or curve binding (appliances), turn off the PQC behavior (Akamai), or revert the resource policy (AWS).

7.14 Web servers, reverse proxies and app servers (self-managed)

Every OpenSSL 3.5, Go 1.24+ and JDK 25 server tested here negotiates X25519MLKEM768 on both legs with one or two lines of config; Envoy and anything under the Amazon Linux 2023 crypto policy stay classical until you set the group list. 110 checks across 20 server configurations, run 2026-11-14.

What decides PQ here

The TLS library linked into the server binary decides, separately for each leg. Inbound (client to server) uses the listener's group list. Upstream (server to backend: proxy_pass, ProxyPass, HAProxy server lines, Envoy clusters) uses the proxy's client-side group list.

OpenSSL 3.5 puts X25519MLKEM768 first in its built-in default, so nginx, Apache httpd, HAProxy and Tomcat's OpenSSL connector are PQ on both legs with no directive at all, unless a system policy overrides it (Amazon Linux 2023 and other RHEL-family crypto-policies). Envoy (BoringSSL) before 1.40 defaults to X25519 and P-256 and must be told; from 1.40.0 X25519MLKEM768 is in the default list on both legs (Tested on 1.40.2, 2026-11-16: with no ecdh_curves, a PQ-only client negotiated X25519MLKEM768 and a PQ-only backend returned 200). Caddy and Traefik use Go's crypto/tls, PQ by default since Go 1.24. Tomcat's JSSE connector follows the JDK (Java); IIS follows Windows SChannel (.NET, Hosts and OS).

Status matrix

Tested = run here on 2026-11-14 with the image and version shown (arm64 images, pulled by digest). Pass counts are in Verify. Lambda does not apply: there is no long-running web server.

Device types: everything above is software on Linux-class compute (EC2, ECS/Fargate, EKS) or Windows for IIS. Managed front doors (ALB, NLB, CloudFront, API Gateway) are in Load balancers and Edge and API front doors; hardware and virtual appliances (F5, Citrix, firewalls) are in Network devices and egress.

Client side (upstream leg)

The upstream leg is the one most often missed: a PQ listener in front of a classical proxy_pass leaves the backend hop exposed. Test it against a PQ-only backend (Verify).

  • nginx (1.19.4+ for proxy_ssl_conf_command). Without it nginx uses OpenSSL's default list: PQ on OpenSSL 3.5, but not under the AL2023 policy (Tested: HTTP 502 to a PQ-only backend).

    location /api/ {
    proxy_pass https://backend:8443/;
    proxy_ssl_protocols TLSv1.3;
    proxy_ssl_conf_command Groups X25519MLKEM768:X25519;
    proxy_ssl_verify on;
    proxy_ssl_trusted_certificate /etc/ssl/certs/ca.pem;
}

  • Apache httpd: there is no SSLProxy* directive for groups; SSLOpenSSLConfCmd applies to the server side only. The proxy leg takes OpenSSL's default: PQ on the Debian and Alpine images (Tested). On AL2023 it failed with AH02003: SSL Proxy connect failed ... handshake failure until the host policy was set to DEFAULT:PQ (Tested). The alternative is OPENSSL_CONF pointing at a PQ config in the systemd unit (Hosts and OS).

    SSLProxyEngine on
SSLProxyProtocol -all +TLSv1.3
SSLProxyVerify require
SSLProxyCACertificateFile /etc/ssl/certs/ca.pem
ProxyPass /api/ https://backend:8443/

  • HAProxy: curves on each server line, or once in global with ssl-default-server-curves (both accepted by 3.4.7, Tested).

    backend be_api
    server api1 backend:8443 ssl verify required ca-file /etc/ssl/certs/ca.pem sni str(backend) curves X25519MLKEM768:X25519 ssl-min-ver TLSv1.3

  • Envoy: set ecdh_curves in the cluster's UpstreamTlsContext, and set both minimum and maximum version. The upstream maximum defaults to TLS 1.2, so tls_minimum_protocol_version: TLSv1_3 alone fails with NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS_ENABLED (Tested). Without ecdh_curves, a PQ-only backend returns 503 (Tested).

    transport_socket:
  name: envoy.transport_sockets.tls
  typed_config:
    "@type": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.transport_sockets.tls.v3.UpstreamTlsContext
    sni: backend
    common_tls_context:
      tls_params:
        tls_minimum_protocol_version: TLSv1_3
        tls_maximum_protocol_version: TLSv1_3
        ecdh_curves: ["X25519MLKEM768", "X25519"]

  • Caddy: tls_curves in the reverse_proxy HTTP transport (Tested; PQ without it too, from Go's default).

    reverse_proxy https://backend:8443 {
    transport http {
        tls_server_name backend
        tls_curves x25519mlkem768 x25519
    }
}

  • Traefik: serversTransport has no curve option (Traefik 3.7 TLS options). The backend leg uses Go's default, which includes X25519MLKEM768 in builds on Go 1.24+ (Tested with 3.7.19).

  • Tomcat as a client (HttpClient, RestTemplate, WebClient inside the webapp) follows the JDK: Java.

Server side (inbound)

Keep a classical fallback (X25519, then P-256) after the hybrid group unless you are deliberately enforcing PQ-only. Every row was Tested with four clients: PQ-only (got X25519MLKEM768), default OpenSSL 3.5 (got X25519MLKEM768), classical X25519 (got X25519), and TLS 1.2 (refused, because each config is TLS 1.3-only).

ServerDirective (excerpt)Docs
nginxssl_protocols TLSv1.3; ssl_ecdh_curve X25519MLKEM768:X25519:prime256v1;ssl_ecdh_curve
Apache httpdSSLProtocol -all +TLSv1.3 and SSLOpenSSLConfCmd Groups X25519MLKEM768:X25519:secp256r1SSLOpenSSLConfCmd
HAProxybind :443 ssl crt /etc/haproxy/site.pem curves X25519MLKEM768:X25519:P-256 ssl-min-ver TLSv1.3curves
EnvoyDownstreamTlsContext tls_params.ecdh_curves: ["X25519MLKEM768","X25519","P-256"]TlsParameters
Caddytls cert key { protocols tls1.3; curves x25519mlkem768 x25519 secp256r1 }tls directive
TraefikTLS option minVersion: VersionTLS13, curvePreferences: [X25519MLKEM768, X25519, CurveP256]TLS options
Tomcat JSSE<SSLHostConfig protocols="TLSv1.3" groups="X25519MLKEM768,x25519,secp256r1">SSLHostConfig groups
Tomcat OpenSSL<OpenSSLConf><OpenSSLConfCmd name="Groups" value="X25519MLKEM768:X25519:secp256r1"/></OpenSSLConf>same page

PQ-only enforcement (internal endpoints whose every client you control): list only X25519MLKEM768. Classical clients then fail with no suitable key share, the error we saw in the reverse case (AL2023 nginx refusing a PQ-only client). In Traefik, setting curvePreferences without X25519MLKEM768 turns PQ off, the same as an explicit CurvePreferences in Go (Go).

Kubernetes ingress and gateways

  • ingress-nginx: ConfigMap ssl-ecdh-curve: "X25519MLKEM768:X25519:prime256v1" and ssl-protocols: "TLSv1.3" (or "TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3"). The v1.14.0 image links OpenSSL 3.5.4, so the upstream leg is PQ by default. The project was retired on 2026-03-24 with no further fixes (Kubernetes blog); plan the move to Gateway API.

  • AWS Load Balancer Controller: the ALB or NLB terminates TLS, so set the PQ security policy with alb.ingress.kubernetes.io/ssl-policy (Ingress) or service.beta.kubernetes.io/aws-load-balancer-ssl-negotiation-policy (Service). Policy names and the target-group leg: Load balancers.

  • Envoy Gateway: ClientTrafficPolicy.spec.tls.ecdhCurves for listeners and EnvoyProxy.spec.backendTLS.ecdhCurves for backends. Both default to X25519 and P-256 (API reference v1.10). Documented; not run here.

  • Istio ingress and egress gateways: meshConfig.tlsDefaults.ecdhCurves or COMPLIANCE_POLICY=pqc (Service mesh).

Packaging per compute type

The rule for containers: pick an image on Debian 13 or Alpine 3.22+ and pin it by digest; the config file does the rest. No environment variable is needed.

The SSM document's nginx snippet was Tested on AL2023 (nginx 1.30.6, OpenSSL 3.5.8): nginx -t passed, a PQ-only client got X25519MLKEM768 and a classical client got X25519. The CloudFormation templates passed cfn-lint and the Terraform passed terraform fmt; neither was deployed (no AWS credentials here).

Verify

  1. Posture. OpenSSL must be 3.5 or later: nginx -V 2>&1 | grep OpenSSL, haproxy -vv | grep 'SSL library', httpd -v plus openssl version. Envoy: envoy --version (BoringSSL; check the config, not the version). Go servers: caddy version, traefik version (Go 1.24+). On AL2023 also update-crypto-policies --show.

  2. Inbound, live. The PQ client must get the hybrid group and the classical client must still connect:

    openssl s_client -connect host:443 -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 </dev/null 2>&1 | grep 'Negotiated TLS1.3 group'
# Negotiated TLS1.3 group: X25519MLKEM768
openssl s_client -connect host:443 -tls1_3 -groups X25519 </dev/null 2>&1 | grep 'Peer Temp Key'
# Peer Temp Key: X25519, 253 bits
openssl s_client -connect host:443 -tls1_2 </dev/null   # must fail when the listener is TLS 1.3-only

  3. Upstream, live. Point a test route at a PQ-only backend (openssl s_server -accept 8443 -www -tls1_3 -groups X25519MLKEM768 -cert c.pem -key k.pem) and at a classical-only one (-groups X25519). Both must return HTTP 200 through the proxy.

  4. Logs and metrics. nginx: add $ssl_curve to log_format (Tested: proto=TLSv1.3 cipher=TLS_AES_256_GCM_SHA384 group=X25519MLKEM768). Envoy: admin /stats counters listener.<addr>.ssl.curves.X25519MLKEM768 and cluster.<name>.ssl.curves.X25519MLKEM768 (Tested). Apache httpd and HAProxy log formats have no negotiated-group variable that we found, so use the s_client checks or a packet capture (Network devices and egress).

Test results, 2026-11-14 (harness run-tests.sh in the work dir; backends were OpenSSL 3.5.7 s_server on Debian 13):

Pitfalls and rollback

  • Envoy's upstream TLS maximum defaults to 1.2. Setting only the minimum to 1.3 gives NO_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS_ENABLED and 503s. Set both (Tested).

  • Envoy before 1.40 has no ML-KEM by default. Without ecdh_curves, a PQ-only client is refused and a PQ-only backend gives 503 (Tested on 1.36.13). 1.40.0+ includes X25519MLKEM768 by default (Tested on 1.40.2); keep the explicit list so older and pinned builds behave the same.

  • Amazon Linux 2023's crypto policy drops ML-KEM from OpenSSL's defaults, even with OpenSSL 3.5.8. Explicit server directives work; the Apache proxy leg needs the host policy (Tested).

  • Old OpenSSL rejects the name. On OpenSSL 3.0 hosts, a config naming X25519MLKEM768 is expected to fail at load (nginx -t, haproxy -c). Not tested here, so run the config check in CI before rollout.

  • Explicit lists replace the default. Traefik curvePreferences, an nginx ssl_ecdh_curve or a HAProxy curves list without X25519MLKEM768 turns PQ off.

  • PQ-only listeners lock out classical clients. Keep X25519 after the hybrid group on anything internet-facing. One community report describes intermittent failures after forcing PQ-only on nginx (forum thread, Claimed).

  • Larger ClientHello (about 1.2 KB more) can upset old middleboxes on the upstream path: Network devices and egress.

  • ingress-nginx is retired, so fixes have stopped; plan the Gateway API move rather than relying on it long term.

  • Rollback: remove the group directive, or put X25519 first, then reload (nginx -s reload, systemctl reload haproxy, apachectl graceful, Envoy hot restart or xDS push). Clients need no change. On AL2023, update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT and restart the service.

7.15 Mobile, desktop and CLI clients

What decides PQ here

Each client's own TLS stack decides what it offers: the OS stack on iOS, macOS and Windows; BoringSSL in Chrome; NSS in Firefox; OpenSSL, GnuTLS or LibreSSL in command-line tools; Go's crypto/tls in kubectl, helm and terraform. A client only gets PQ if the server also offers it, so most of these clients start using PQ against your estate only after the front doors run a PQ policy (Load balancers, Edge and API front doors). Every client below still connects to classical servers.

Status matrix

Tested = run here on 2026-11-14 against a PQ-only server (openssl s_server -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3, OpenSSL 3.5.7) and a classical-only server (-groups X25519). Every Tested client also reached the classical server. Scripts and output: deep/hosts/run-hosts*.sh and run-hosts*.out in the work dir.

For Go tools, run go version -m <binary>: Go ≥ 1.24 and no tlsmlkem=0 in DefaultGODEBUG means PQ is offered.

Client side (app level)

iOS, iPadOS, macOS apps

  • Nothing to change for URLSession and Network.framework on 26+. The ClientHello carries X25519MLKEM768 plus a key share (Apple 122756).

  • Apple says some legacy servers fail to read the larger ClientHello. It offers a temporary compatibility mode that "will not be available in a future version". Fix the server instead.

  • Apps on their own TLS (BoringSSL, OpenSSL, Flutter's dart:io) follow that library. Not checked here.

  • I found no public Apple API that returns the negotiated group. Verify on the server side (ALB connection log keyExchange).

Android apps

  • Chrome and WebView traffic: PQ.

  • For app traffic on the platform TLS provider, I found no documented Android 16 or 17 default. Treat it as classical until Google documents it.

  • To control it in the app, bundle Conscrypt and install it as the first provider; OkHttp uses Conscrypt when it is the default provider. A Conscrypt maintainer says 2.6.1 offers X25519MLKEM768 by default (Claimed; not tested here).

// Application.onCreate() (excerpt)
Security.insertProviderAt(Conscrypt.newProvider(), 1)

Windows clients

  • Schannel apps: Enable-TlsEccCurve -Name "X25519_MLKEM768" -Position 0 on Windows 11 24H2/25H2 with KB5089573. GPO overrides PowerShell. SSM document in Hosts and OS.

  • Browsers on Windows use their own stacks and are PQ already.

  • PowerShell 7 runs on .NET, which uses Schannel on Windows, so the same setting applies (.NET).

curl

  • PQ by default when linked to OpenSSL ≥ 3.5 and the OS policy allows it. Check curl -V (look for OpenSSL/3.5) and curl -v, which prints SSL connection using TLSv1.3 / … / X25519MLKEM768.

  • To pin the order or test a PQ-only server: --curves X25519MLKEM768:X25519 (OpenSSL backend).

  • macOS: the system curl can't do PQ; use a curl built on OpenSSL 3.5 (Homebrew, or a container).

wget and git

  • Debian and Ubuntu build wget, and git through libcurl-gnutls, on GnuTLS. Their GnuTLS (3.8.9 on Debian 13, 3.8.12 on Ubuntu 26.04) has no ML-KEM group, so these fail on PQ-only servers even where curl works.

  • Use curl instead of wget in scripts, or use Alpine 3.24 or AL2023 (DEFAULT:PQ) images.

  • For git in CI on Debian/Ubuntu images I found no switch to an OpenSSL backend. Use an Alpine-based git image, or keep the git server's front door accepting classical.

AWS CLI

  • v2 on Linux: ≥ 2.34.54 (Documented); 2.40.3 Tested.

  • v1: follows Python's OpenSSL (Tested both ways above).

  • macOS: Homebrew install with Homebrew OpenSSL ≥ 3.5 (AWS doc). Windows: depends on the OS; not confirmed.

  • Details: AWS service endpoints.

kubectl, helm, terraform and other Go tools

  • Released binaries built with Go ≥ 1.24 offer PQ (kubectl 1.34.1 and helm 4.3.2 tested live).

  • Plugins and providers are separate binaries: terraform providers, kubectl plugins and credential helpers each have their own TLS (aws eks get-token is the AWS CLI). Check each with go version -m.

OpenSSH client

OpenSSH ≥ 10.0 is PQ by default; 9.9 supports ML-KEM; 9.0–9.8 only sntrup761. Config in Hosts and OS.

Server side

These clients benefit only if the server offers PQ:

Front doorWhat to setChapter
ALB / NLBELBSecurityPolicy-TLS13-1-2-Res-PQ-2025-09Load balancers
CloudFront (viewer)Nothing: PQ by defaultEdge and API front doors
API Gateway RESTSecurityPolicy_TLS13_1_2_PQ_2025_09Edge and API front doors
nginx / Envoy / HAProxy on your hostsOpenSSL 3.5 + groupsWeb servers and proxies
sshd / SFTPOpenSSH ≥ 9.9, or Transfer Family PQ policyHosts and OS; Edge

Keep classical groups on public front doors: Debian/Ubuntu wget and git, macOS system curl, Android platform TLS, Windows Server 2019/2022 and older JDKs can't do PQ yet.

Packaging

WhereWhat to ship
CI runners and build imagesA toolbox image on Alpine 3.24 (curl, wget, git, OpenSSH 10.3) plus kubectl and helm copied from their images; run the AWS CLI from amazon/aws-cli ≥ 2.34.54. Dockerfile below

Mobile appsiOS 26+ minimum for guaranteed PQ; Android: bundle Conscrypt ≥ 2.6.1 if PQ is required

Verify

  1. Posture: curl -V, ssh -V, aws --version, go version -m $(which kubectl), ldd $(git --exec-path)/git-remote-https | grep -E 'gnutls|ssl', wget --version | grep ssl/.

  2. Live: against a PQ-only test endpoint, curl -sv https://pq-test/ 2>&1 | grep 'SSL connection using' must show X25519MLKEM768; ssh -v host true 2>&1 | grep 'kex: algorithm' must show mlkem768x25519-sha256; a classical endpoint must still work.

  3. Observed: ALB connection logs and NLB access logs carry keyExchange per connection. Group them by user agent to see which client families are still classical.

Pitfalls

  • curl and wget on the same Debian host differ (OpenSSL vs GnuTLS). So do curl and git.

  • Upgrading macOS doesn't upgrade /usr/bin/curl's LibreSSL (still 3.3.6 on macOS 27.0.1).

  • A PQ-only listener breaks every classical client in the matrix. Use PQ-only only on internal endpoints whose client list you control.

  • Older middleboxes may drop the bigger ClientHello; Apple and AWS both warn about it.

  • Rollback for a client is its previous version or config; servers keep accepting classical clients unless you made them PQ-only.

8 Enforcement and guardrails

8.1 What decides enforcement here

No single AWS control can force ML-KEM. Policy can only force the server-side setting (ELB / API Gateway security policy, runtime, env var, image) and then measure what clients actually negotiate. So enforcement is layered: the organisation blocks non-PQ settings at the API, the pipeline blocks them before deploy, the cluster blocks them at admission, and runtime logs catch the clients that still connect classically.

Layer Runs where Blocks or detects Tools on this chapter
Organisation AWS Organizations (all accounts) Blocks API calls SCP, RCP
Account (detective) AWS Config, Security Hub, every account/Region Detects drift Config managed + Guard custom rules, AWS PQC Readiness Scanner pack, ELB.17, APIGateway.11, CloudFront.15
Pipeline CI (PR and deploy job), CloudFormation service Blocks merge / deploy cfn-guard, Guard Hook, conftest (Terraform plan, Dockerfile, SBOM), Checkov, image scripts
Cluster EKS admission Blocks or audits objects Kyverno (1.20+ CEL and legacy), Gatekeeper
Runtime CloudTrail, ELB logs, network Detects classical handshakes CloudTrail Lake/Athena, metric filter alarm, EventBridge, ELB log Athena, Zeek

Rule IDs are shared across tools (PQ-L Lambda, PQ-E ECS, PQ-B ELB, PQ-A API Gateway, PQ-I image, PQ-D Dockerfile, PQ-K Kubernetes), so one exception ID covers every layer.

8.2 Enforcement matrix

Evidence: Tested = the rule ran here in a container against sample good and bad inputs (syntax + sample evaluation, not tested in AWS). Documented = AWS or vendor docs. Tool versions: cfn-guard 3.2.1, cfn-lint 1.60.1, conftest 0.71.1 (OPA 1.21.1), Checkov 3.4.5, Terraform 1.13 + AWS provider 6.x, Kyverno CLI 1.19.0 and 1.20.0, gator 3.20.0, Syft (pinned by digest).

8.3 Organisation level

SCP. Only two services expose a TLS-policy condition key: Elastic Load Balancing (elasticloadbalancing:SecurityPolicy on CreateListener, ModifyListener, CreateLoadBalancerPolicy and SetLoadBalancerPoliciesOfListener, not CreateLoadBalancer) and API Gateway (apigateway:Request/SecurityPolicy and apigateway:Resource/SecurityPolicy on DomainNames and RestApis). CloudFront has only tag keys and IoT Core has no TLS-policy key. Checked in the AWS programmatic service reference on 2026-11-14 (service authorization reference). So CloudFront and IoT domain configurations can only be checked by Config and the pipeline. Documented.

The SCP has four statements:

  1. Deny CreateListener/ModifyListener when the named policy is not a -PQ-2025-09 policy (TLS 1.0 PQ variants excluded).
  2. Deny an HTTPS/TLS CreateListener with no policy. CloudFormation, CLI and CDK otherwise default to ELBSecurityPolicy-2016-08.
  3. Deny new Classic Load Balancer listener policies (Classic cannot do TLS 1.3).
  4. Deny REST API / custom domain writes whose SecurityPolicy is not a PQ policy.

The bypass is one role, pq-exception-break-glass, the same as the SCP on the Load balancers tab. This org baseline allows all eleven non-TLS-1.0 PQ ELB policies during migration; that chapter's four-policy SCP is the next stage (TLS 1.2+1.3 restricted PQ and TLS 1.3-only PQ, plus FIPS). The end state, once TLS 1.2 traffic is zero, allows only the TLS 1.3-only PQ policies (ELBSecurityPolicy-TLS13-1-3-PQ-2025-09 and its FIPS variant). Read the two allowlists as stages, not a conflict. Full text in Full configuration files.

API Gateway limits: HTTP and WebSocket APIs support only TLS_1_2, so no SCP can make them PQ (AWS). There is no TLS 1.3-only PQ policy; all PQ policies are SecurityPolicy_TLS13_1_2_*PQ_2025_09 (list). Edge-optimized endpoints have no PQ-named policy; the SCP allows SecurityPolicy_TLS13_2025_EDGE and SecurityPolicy_TLS12_PFS_2025_EDGE because the viewer leg is CloudFront. Whether that leg negotiates ML-KEM for edge-optimized APIs is not confirmed; see Edge and API front doors.

RCP. No IAM condition key exposes the key-exchange group; aws:SecureTransport is the only global TLS key. The useful RCP is s3:TlsVersion of 1.3 or higher on named buckets, because ML-KEM exists only in TLS 1.3. It does not force ML-KEM. Use it only for buckets whose callers all run PQ-capable SDKs (RCPs). Documented.

AWS Config managed rules (org-config-rules.yaml, deployed per Region from the Config delegated administrator):

Rule Parameter Note
elbv2-predefined-security-policy-ssl-check sslPolicies = the 11 PQ policies Not available in me-central-1, ap-south-2, ap-southeast-4, ca-west-1, eu-south-2, eu-central-2
apigateway-domain-name-tls-check allowedSecurityPolicies = PQ + edge policies REST custom domains only; execute-api endpoints are covered by the scanner pack
cloudfront-ssl-policy-check securityPolicies = TLSv1.2_2021, TLSv1.2_2025, TLSv1.3_2025 us-east-1 only; viewer PQ is on by default, origin leg is classical
lambda-function-settings-check Not used It also checks memorySize (default 128) and timeout (default 3), so most functions fail; use the Guard rule below

Custom Guard rule (lambda-pq-tls.guard, Config custom policy rule, runtime guard-2.x.x): blocks nodejs18.x/20.x, python3.8, java8, dotnet6; python3.12+, nodejs22+ and dotnet8+ need OPENSSL_CONF set to /var/task/pq-tls/openssl-pq*.cnf (bundled) or /opt/pq-tls/openssl-pq*.cnf (layer), or AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper; java8.al2/al2023 to java21, including the .al2023 variants of 11 and 17, need the agent in JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS or the wrapper; GODEBUG must not contain tlsmlkem=0. Image functions are skipped (the pipeline checks the image). Before enforcing, pull one real configuration item (aws configservice get-resource-config-history --resource-type AWS::Lambda::Function --resource-id <name> --limit 1) and confirm the variables sit under configuration.environment.variables; I could not confirm that path from docs.

AWS PQC Readiness Scanner (May 2026). AWS Security Blog, 2026-05-14, Automating post-quantum cryptography readiness using AWS Config; code at aws-samples/sample-PQC-Readiness-using-AWS-Config. It is a sample, not a managed rule. Four custom rules on two Lambda functions (pqc-elb-pqc-compliance-*, pqc-elb-legacy-tls-*, pqc-apigateway-pqc-compliance-*, pqc-apigateway-legacy-tls-*) cover ALB/NLB HTTPS/TLS listeners and REST (Regional, private), HTTP and WebSocket APIs. Tier 1 = TLS 1.3 only with PQ; Tier 2 = TLS 1.2 + 1.3 with PQ; Tier 3 = no PQ (high priority). CloudFront and Classic LB are out of scope. Org deploy: StackSet pqc-readiness-lambda-functions, then aws configservice put-organization-conformance-pack --organization-conformance-pack-name pqc-legacy-tls-compliance with conformance-packs/pqc-legacy-tls-conformance-pack.yaml. Run it next to the managed rules: it adds execute-api endpoints and the tier view. Documented (blog); not deployed here.

Security Hub. ELB.17 uses the same Config rule with a fixed list that includes non-PQ policies (ELBSecurityPolicy-TLS13-1-3-2021-06, FIPS 2023, RFC9151), so PASS does not mean PQ. APIGateway.11 allows SecurityPolicy_TLS13_1_2_PFS_PQ_2025_09 but also non-PQ TLS 1.3 policies. CloudFront.15 wants TLSv1.2_2025 or TLSv1.3_2025. Keep them on; use the customised Config rules as the PQ signal. Documented.

Firewall Manager. Its policy types are WAF, Shield Advanced, security groups, network ACLs, Network Firewall, DNS Firewall and third-party firewalls (AWS). None sets an ELB, API Gateway or CloudFront TLS policy, so FMS has no PQ role beyond rolling out Network Firewall (see Network devices and egress). Documented.

8.4 Pipeline

Run every check on the PR (fast feedback) and again in the deploy job (the gate). Order: Dockerfile lint, build, SBOM and binary checks, IaC checks (cfn-guard / conftest / Checkov), then the CloudFormation Guard Hook in the account as the last line.

cfn-guard (pq-tls.guard, 14 rules): cfn-guard validate --rules pq-tls.guard --data template.yaml --show-summary fail.

Rule Resource Check
PQ-L1 Lambda, SAM function, SAM Globals.Function.Runtime Runtime not nodejs18.x/20.x, python3.8, java8, dotnet6
PQ-L2 Lambda python3.12-3.14, nodejs22/24, dotnet8/10 OPENSSL_CONF = /var/task/pq-tls/ or /opt/pq-tls/ openssl-pq*.cnf, or AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER=/opt/pq-tls/wrapper
PQ-L3 Lambda java8.al2, java8.al2023, java11, java11.al2023, java17, java17.al2023, java21 JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS loads pq-tls-agent.jar, or the wrapper
PQ-L4 (warn) Lambda python3.9-3.11 Wrapper set (shim covers urllib3/botocore only)
PQ-L5 Lambda GODEBUG has no tlsmlkem=0
PQ-E1-E3 ECS task definition No GODEBUG tlsmlkem=0; jdk.tls.namedGroups includes X25519MLKEM768; no OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null
PQ-B1/B2 ELBv2 listener HTTPS/TLS SslPolicy present and in the PQ list
PQ-A1-A3 API GW REST domain and API (Regional/Private) *_PQ_2025_09 policy and EndpointAccessMode set
PQ-A4 API GW v2 domain Tag PqTlsException (HTTP/WebSocket cannot be PQ)

For warn-then-fail, run PQ-L4 and PQ-A4 (or the whole file during the warn window) in a non-blocking step.

CloudFormation Guard Hook (guard-hook.yaml): AWS::CloudFormation::GuardHook reads the same rule file from S3, TargetOperations: [RESOURCE, STACK], FailureMode: WARN during the warn window, then FAIL. Deploy with a service-managed StackSet. It catches console and ad-hoc stacks that skip CI (GuardHook). Documented; the template passes cfn-lint.

OPA / conftest for Terraform (terraform/pq_tls.rego):

terraform plan -out plan.out
terraform show -json plan.out > plan.json
conftest test --policy rules/opa/terraform --namespace terraform.pq_tls plan.json

Same rule IDs. Exceptions: tag pq-tls-exception = "EXC-nnnn". The AWS provider 6.x accepted security_policy = "SecurityPolicy_TLS13_1_2_PQ_2025_09" and endpoint_access_mode = "BASIC" on aws_api_gateway_domain_name in a real offline plan (Tested).

Checkov (rules/checkov/pq_tls_1.yaml to _4.yaml, one policy per file): checkov -d . --framework terraform --external-checks-dir rules/checkov --check CKV2_PQTLS_1,CKV2_PQTLS_2,CKV2_PQTLS_3,CKV2_PQTLS_4. Use it if Checkov is already your scanner; conftest covers more (ECS JSON, exceptions, warn level).

Container images.

  • OpenSSL in the image: syft <image> -o syft-json > sbom.json, then conftest test --policy rules/opa/image --namespace image.openssl sbom.json. Fails libssl/openssl below 3.5 (PQ-I1), warns when no system OpenSSL is present (PQ-I2: check the language runtime instead) and on Amazon Linux 2023 (PQ-I3: needs DEFAULT:PQ or the conf).
  • Go binaries: check-go-pq.sh <binary>... runs go version -m and fails a toolchain below 1.24 or tlsmlkem=0 in DefaultGODEBUG. 1.24 is the PQ floor (X25519MLKEM768 by default), so the check fails only binaries with no PQ; the Go chapter's target, 1.26 with go 1.26, adds the two NIST hybrids and is still in support. Raise the floor to 1.26 when the fleet allows. It cannot see an explicit CurvePreferences in code; add grep -rn 'CurvePreferences' --include=*.go . and require X25519MLKEM768 in every hit (see Go).
  • JDK: check-jdk-pq.sh <image> reads the JDK release file without running the image; JDK 25+ passes, 8-21 warns unless JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS loads the agent (see Java).

Dockerfile lint (dockerfile/pq_dockerfile.rego, conftest's built-in Dockerfile parser): warns on classical base images (debian:bookworm, ubuntu 22.04/24.04, node:18/20 and Node tags pinned below 22.20 / 24.5, python -bookworm/-bullseye tags (default python tags are trixie and PQ), golang 1.22/1.23, JDK 17/21 images, amazonlinux:2); denies ENV GODEBUG=...tlsmlkem=0, OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null, jdk.tls.namedGroups without X25519MLKEM768, and update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT without :PQ. hadolint has no custom-rule mechanism, so this rule lives in conftest:

conftest test --parser dockerfile --policy rules/opa/dockerfile --namespace dockerfile.pq_tls Dockerfile

8.5 Kubernetes

Kyverno 1.20 no longer runs kyverno.io/v1 ClusterPolicy. The 1.20.0 CLI rejected it with "Kyverno v1.20 removed execution of legacy kyverno.io policy types … Migrate to ValidatingPolicy" (Tested; see the migration guide). So there are two files: kyverno-pq-tls-cel.yaml (ValidatingPolicy, CEL, policies.kyverno.io/v1) for 1.20+, and kyverno-pq-tls.yaml (ClusterPolicy) for 1.19 and older.

Policy Object Check
PQ-K1 Ingress, class alb, with a certificate alb.ingress.kubernetes.io/ssl-policy in the PQ list (controller default is ELBSecurityPolicy-2016-08)
PQ-K2 Service LoadBalancer with aws-load-balancer-ssl-cert service.beta.kubernetes.io/aws-load-balancer-ssl-negotiation-policy in the PQ list
PQ-K3 ConfigMap ingress-nginx-controller ssl-ecdh-curve starts with X25519MLKEM768; works only when the controller image links OpenSSL 3.5 (see Web servers and proxies)
PQ-K4/K5 istiod Deployment, ztunnel DaemonSet env COMPLIANCE_POLICY=pqc (Istio 1.27+, see Service mesh)
PQ-K6/K7 Pod No GODEBUG with tlsmlkem=0, no OPENSSL_CONF=/dev/null

Exception: annotation pq-tls.example.com/exception: EXC-nnnn on K1/K2 objects. Rollout: validationActions: [Audit] (ClusterPolicy failureAction: Audit), read PolicyReports for 30 days, then [Deny] / Enforce. In the CEL version a Pod that breaks both K6 and K7 reports only the first failed validation of that policy.

Gatekeeper alternative (gatekeeper-pq-tls.yaml): ConstraintTemplate K8sPqTlsElbPolicy with the PQ list as a parameter covers K1/K2; start with enforcementAction: warn, then deny. Extend the same Rego for K3-K7 if Gatekeeper is your admission controller.

8.6 Runtime detection and alerting

CloudTrail records tlsDetails.keyExchange only for KMS, STS, IAM, Secrets Manager, ACM and Payment Cryptography. Calls a service makes on your behalf have no tlsDetails; exclude them with userIdentity.invokedBy. The alarm (cloudtrail-alarm.yaml) extends the metric filter in the KMS guide to all six services: keyExchange not matching *MLKEM* or missing, Sum of 1 or more per hour. cloudtrail-non-pq.sql lists the callers (principal, user agent, source IP) for CloudTrail Lake and for Athena; the stock Athena CloudTrail DDL needs keyexchange:STRING added to the tlsdetails struct. See AWS service endpoints for which endpoints negotiate PQ.

EventBridge (eventbridge-rules.json): listener created or modified with a non-PQ sslPolicy; any API Gateway domain or API create/update (re-check with Config); Config NON_COMPLIANT for pq-tls-* rules; Guard Hook invocations. The patterns are valid JSON; the anything-but + wildcard match and the Hook event detail-type are not confirmed against live events.

ALB and NLB logs (elb-logs-athena.sql): ALB connection logs field 13 and NLB TLS access logs field 17 hold tls_keyexchange (ALB, NLB). The file has table DDL, the ML-KEM share per load balancer, top classical clients, and an hourly count to publish as a CloudWatch metric (EventBridge Scheduler → Step Functions → Athena → PutMetricData) with an alarm on the classical share. ALB access logs (not connection logs) do not carry the key exchange. CloudFront: the CloudFront-Viewer-TLS-Details origin request header includes the key exchange (Documented; see Edge and API front doors).

Zeek on a traffic mirror or behind Network Firewall sees the TLS named group for east-west and egress flows that no AWS log covers. Alert on TLS 1.3 sessions whose group is not X25519MLKEM768 for destinations on the PQ-required list. Configuration is on Network devices and egress.

8.7 Warn-then-fail rollout

  1. Week 0, inventory. Deploy the Config rules, the scanner pack, the CloudTrail alarm (notify only) and the ELB log tables. Pipeline checks run in warn mode: cfn-guard in a non-blocking step, conftest warn, Checkov --soft-fail, Guard Hook WARN, Kyverno Audit, Gatekeeper warn.
  2. Week 2, block new risk. SCP on sandbox and new-workload OUs. Pipeline fails on resources in the change set only.
  3. Week 6, block everything in CI. Fail the pipeline on all resources; open exceptions for the rest. Guard Hook FAIL, Kyverno Deny.
  4. Week 8, org-wide SCP. Attach to all workload OUs once Config shows no non-compliant listeners outside the exception register.
  5. Ongoing. The classical-share metric per load balancer trends down to the exception list. Apply the RCP per bucket once its logs show TLS 1.3 only.

Each step is reversible by flipping the mode (WARN, Audit, soft-fail) or detaching the SCP; no resource changes.

8.8 Exception register

One register (GRC tool or a repo file) keyed by EXC-nnnn. The same ID appears as tag pq-tls-exception (Terraform; PqTlsException in CloudFormation rules where a hyphen breaks the rule syntax), annotation pq-tls.example.com/exception (Kubernetes), and as the session tag when someone assumes pq-exception-break-glass to pass the SCP.

Field Content
ID EXC-nnnn
Rule IDs e.g. PQ-B2, PQ-A4
Resources Exact ARNs or repo paths; no wildcards
Reason e.g. HTTP API (TLS_1_2 only), partner client on TLS 1.2, Lambda nodejs20.x pending migration
Data exposure Confidentiality lifetime of the data on this path (harvest-now-decrypt-later risk)
Compensating control e.g. CloudFront in front, VPN, short-lived data
Owner and approver Team and security approver
Created / expires 90 days maximum; 180 for vendor-blocked items
Exit plan Fix and target date
Evidence Ticket, client inventory, log query result

Review monthly (platform security lead) and report count and age quarterly. No renewal without a new exit date. Recommended: a nightly job that compares tags and annotations with the register and removes or flags expired IDs, so the pipeline fails again on expiry.

In an account, verify in a sandbox OU: create one non-PQ listener and confirm (1) the SCP returns AccessDenied, (2) the Guard Hook warns, (3) Config marks it NON_COMPLIANT, (4) the EventBridge rule fires.

8.10 Pitfalls and rollback

  • CloudFormation, CLI and CDK default ELB listeners to ELBSecurityPolicy-2016-08; only the console defaults to ELBSecurityPolicy-TLS13-1-2-Res-PQ-2025-09. Require an explicit policy.
  • Security Hub ELB.17 and APIGateway.11 pass non-PQ TLS 1.3 policies. Do not report them as PQ coverage.
  • lambda-function-settings-check also checks memory and timeout, so most functions show non-compliant.
  • A PQ server policy is not PQ traffic: clients must offer ML-KEM. Measure with logs.
  • Kyverno 1.20 needs ValidatingPolicy; pin the policy file to the Kyverno version you run.
  • The SCP's API Gateway statement depends on apigateway:Request/SecurityPolicy being populated on POST/PATCH; test in a sandbox OU before attaching widely.
  • Config custom Guard rules run Guard 2.x; keep them free of 3.x-only syntax.
  • A plain prefix match on amazonlinux:2 also catches amazonlinux:2023; the Dockerfile rule matches on tag boundaries for that reason.
  • Rollback: detach the SCP (takes effect in minutes), set Guard Hook FailureMode: WARN, Kyverno Audit, conftest rules to warn. Config rules only detect and need no rollback.

Sources: AWS PQC readiness blog (2026-05-14) · ALB security policies · NLB security policies · API Gateway security policies · Security Hub ELB controls · GuardHook · KMS hybrid PQ TLS · Firewall Manager policy types · RCPs · Kyverno CEL migration

9 Rollout, gaps and open items

The dated plan (waves, owners, the certificate track and decisions) is in the companion rollout plan, artifacts/PQC-TLS-rollout-plan.md. This chapter gives the mechanism every wave uses and the open items.

9.1 Rollout through build, package and release

Every change, including configuration, ships as a release here. So the fix lives in the shared build template, and each workload picks it up on its next release with no per-service code edits.

The loop has four steps, repeated every release cycle: Classify → Change request → Release → Verify.

The release step is the only one that changes a workload; the others are automation around it.

  1. Classify. The posture inventory sorts each workload into a matrix row and records the gap.
  2. Change request. A bot opens a pull request in each repo that bumps the shared module or template. The template ships the right files under /var/task/pq-tls/ (config file, agent jar or shim) and sets the matching variable. It leaves a workload's own OPENSSL_CONF alone and flags it for review. Bundling into the package avoids Lambda's 5-layer limit.
  3. Release. The normal pipeline builds, packages and releases. A guardrail (cfn-guard, OPA/conftest or a CloudFormation Hook) warns, then later fails, on Lambda and task definitions missing the PQ settings, and on non-PQ ELB policies.
  4. Verify. Observed signals confirm the workload's connections moved to PQ; shipping isn't proof. CLM tracks each workload as a campaign item.

Dormant workloads with no release coming: rank them by observed classical traffic. One making no classical outbound TLS doesn't need forcing; the rest get a no-op rebuild and release. Runtimes past AWS's block-update date can't be changed at all, so schedule those first.

9.2 Gaps, risks and open items

Item Impact Next step
Node.js 18 and 20 on Lambda No in-process fix short of a runtime upgrade. Interim: the pq-egress extension (Tested in e2e; the app calls it over loopback) Schedule upgrades to nodejs22 or 24; use the extension only for the calls that must be PQ before then
Runtime deprecation dates After block-update, no config change or layer can be applied. AWS's runtime page (read 2026-11-16): nodejs18.x, nodejs20.x, python3.9, python3.10, provided.al2 and dotnet8 are already deprecated, with function create blocked from 2027-07-29 and update from 2027-08-31; python3.11, java8.al2, java11 and java17 (AL2) deprecate 2027-06-30 with the same block dates. Dates are AWS forecasts and can move (Documented) Move these workloads first, and finish before 2027-08-31; re-read the AWS runtimes page each quarter
Python 3.9 to 3.11 shim Covers requests and boto3 only; overrides a botocore design choice; needs idna in the package. Tested with verification on against a private CA and mTLS (e2e), not against real AWS endpoints Test against real AWS endpoints; prefer the runtime upgrade
Bouncy Castle agent on Java 8 to 21 Replaces the TLS provider process-wide Test FIPS, custom keystore and mutual TLS functions before rollout
Bouncy Castle JSSE as a server with an EC (P-256) certificate Resolved. The JDK default SunX509 key manager returns no alias for Bouncy Castle's EC/secp256r1 key type. ssl.KeyManagerFactory.algorithm=PKIX fixes it (Tested: Tomcat, Jetty, HttpsServer on JDK 17 and 21). Code that hard-codes SunX509 still fails Ship kit v2; CI check java-pq-check.sh flags hard-coded SunX509; test servers with their real keystore. RSA+EC stores start serving EC, so check clients that pin the RSA certificate
IaC and guardrail files not deployed Terraform, CloudFormation, SAM, SCP, Config and policy files follow AWS docs but were not applied to an AWS account (no access here) Plan or validate each in a sandbox account first; start SCPs and policies in audit mode
GnuTLS programs on Debian 13 and 14, and Ubuntu 26.04 wget and git over HTTPS stay classical although the OS is "PQ by default"; they fail outright against a PQ-only server. No Debian or Ubuntu GnuTLS release has ML-KEM yet (Fedora's does) Count them in posture; use OpenSSL-linked curl or Alpine/Fedora-based tool images where the traffic matters; SSH is already PQ (OpenSSH 10)
Security Hub control ELB.17 Passes six non-PQ policies (for example ELBSecurityPolicy-TLS13-1-3-2021-06, the FIPS-2023-04 and RFC9151 policies), so a green ELB.17 does not mean PQ (Documented) Deploy the org Config rule pq-tls-elbv2-listener-policy with a PQ-only sslPolicies list, plus the SCP (Enforcement and guardrails chapter)
Lambda and Fargate outbound to non-AWS services No AWS log records the negotiated group Rely on posture; add an egress sensor where the traffic matters
AWS IoT Core No PQ policy; device legs stay classical Track AWS roadmap; new devices choose wolfSSL 5.8+ or OpenSSL 3.5
API Gateway egress, CloudFront origin leg Classical by AWS design today Accept and record as exceptions
Node config drift Our Node file replaces AWS's Pipeline diff check against each new runtime image
ML-DSA certificates Public HTTPS cannot use ML-DSA (ACM, ELB, CloudFront, browsers); Go and Java do not negotiate it; AL2023 needs SignatureAlgorithms as well as Groups; chains grow 0.9 KB to 11.8 KB Dual certificates only; certificate track in the rollout plan; recheck AWS and browser support each quarter
Untested rows Ruby, PHP, Rust and non-Corretto/Temurin JDK 25 builds are Documented or Unverified. Also not confirmed: which AWS SDK for Go v2 release added the FIPS curve list, AWS Network Firewall TLS-inspection PQ legs, the RDS enable setting for MySQL PQ groups, and Oracle's 17/21 backport dates Add them to the emulator test matrix

9.3 Checking a workload

  • Observed: CloudTrail tlsDetails.keyExchange for its AWS API calls shows X25519MLKEM768, ELB logs show the same for inbound traffic, and Zeek shows no classical sessions from its hosts (Detection).
  • Test endpoint: a test server that accepts only X25519MLKEM768 (openssl s_server -groups X25519MLKEM768 -tls1_3 with OpenSSL 3.5) fails any client that isn't PQ. Each language chapter's verify step uses it.
  • Inventory: the component's status is pq or pq-fixed, and the Fix verification dashboard shows no classical calls for seven days (Inventory and dashboards).

Appendix A. What is tested, per language

Every result below comes from the LocalStack Pro end-to-end suite, run r1117041043 (2026-11-16): 471 cases, 471 passed (437 client cases, 32 load balancer target cases and 2 detection checks). Earlier container and Lambda-emulator tests (2026-11-12 to 2026-11-14) are listed per language under "Earlier tests". Nothing here ran on real AWS, on x86_64 or through a real ALB or NLB listener.

How a case works. Each case deploys a real Lambda function, ECS task or EC2 instance in LocalStack, with the language runtime and the fix under test. Its probe connects to a TLS observer that records what the client offered and negotiated. The probe sets no TLS options itself, so the result reflects the runtime and the fix. Each case is graded against the expectation written in its case file, which follows this paper. PASS means the observed behaviour matched the expectation, so a baseline case that stays classical as documented passes.

Observer target Port Accepts Question it answers
hybrid 8443 X25519MLKEM768 first, classical groups too Does the client negotiate PQ when it can?
pqonly 8444 X25519MLKEM768 only Does the client offer ML-KEM at all?
mtls 8445 Hybrid, client certificate required Does mutual TLS still work with the fix?
eccert 8446 Hybrid with an ECDSA server certificate Do EC certificates work (Bouncy Castle PKIX)?
tls12 8447 TLS 1.2 only Does the client still connect to a legacy server?
mldsa-dual, mldsa-only, mldsa-mtls 8448 to 8450 Dual certificate, ML-DSA only, ML-DSA client certificate Which certificate does the client get, and can it use ML-DSA?

Shared test code: observer e2e/observer/, ML-DSA fixture e2e/mldsa/server.py, certificates e2e/certs/gen.sh and e2e/certs/gen-mldsa.sh, case framework e2e/cases/lib.py, ML-DSA expectations e2e/cases/mldsa_rules.py, runner e2e/run.sh, Lambda layers e2e/build/layers.sh, the pq-egress extension e2e/lambda/extension/pq-egress/, ECS and EC2 deployment e2e/ecs/ and e2e/ec2/. Every case and result: e2e/results/latest/summary.md. How to run: e2e/RUNNING.md.

What the cases vary: no change: the runtime's default (expected classical where the paper says so) (35); kit files bundled in the Lambda package (18); the pq-tls layer and AWS_LAMBDA_EXEC_WRAPPER (9); the pq-egress Lambda extension (8); update-crypto-policies --set DEFAULT:PQ (image or host) (8); OpenSSL config file and OPENSSL_CONF (7); a code-level group setting (4); Lambda container image, unchanged (2); Lambda container image with the fix in the Dockerfile (2).

ML-DSA: 62 used ML-DSA on all three targets; 23 got ECDSA from the dual server and failed the ML-DSA-only targets.

Load balancer target leg: 7 servers (ecs-ec2, ecs-fargate); 5 negotiated PQ with the ALB's PQ backend policy (the rest are baselines); 7 of 7 negotiated classical TLS 1.2 with the classic policy.

Not tested

  • Anything on real AWS: CloudTrail keyExchange, ALB and NLB listener legs and logs, Athena queries and dashboards, SCPs, Config rules, Guard hooks.
  • x86_64: all tests ran on arm64.
  • Ruby, PHP and Rust; JDK 25 builds other than Corretto and Temurin; the pq-egress extension for AWS SDK calls.

Appendix B. Configuration file index

Every configuration file the paper refers to, with what it does, which side of the connection it changes, and where it applies. Each file name links to the complete file in the repository under configs/ (the per-folder list is configs/MANIFEST.md); the chapter named in the last column explains it. Side: Client = outbound TLS from the workload; Server = inbound TLS it terminates; Both = the file sets the runtime or image, so it covers both directions; Infra = deployment, policy, detection or CI file that changes no handshake by itself. IaC and SSM files were linted or parse-checked, not deployed.

Appendix C. Evidence and sources

Tests

Tests (2026-11-12). AWS Lambda base images under the Runtime Interface Emulator, against a Node 24 test server with one port accepting only X25519MLKEM768 and one accepting only X25519. Images: python 3.9 to 3.14, nodejs 18 to 24, java 8.al2023, 11, 17, 21, 25, dotnet 8 and 10, provided.al2023 with Go 1.23 to 1.25. Scripts and results: tests/lambda/.

Tests (2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16), end to end. 471 cases in LocalStack Pro (run r1117041043, 471 of 471 passed) (e2e/): real Lambda zip, layer, extension and image functions, ECS Fargate and ECS on EC2 tasks, and EC2 instances from AL2023 and AL2 AMIs, for all five languages, each graded against this document by a TLS observer, plus 32 load balancer target-leg cases. Results: e2e/results/latest/summary.md. Real AWS, x86_64 and the ALB/NLB listener leg were not covered.

Tests (2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14). Existing-certificate cases (own keystores and key files, private CA trust, mutual TLS, explicit TLS settings) for all five languages, in official language and distro images (Debian 12, 13 and 14, Ubuntu 26.04, Alpine, AL2023, Fedora) against OpenSSL 3.5 s_server instances that accept only X25519MLKEM768 or only X25519. The Bouncy Castle EC server fix was tested on Tomcat 11.0.29, Jetty 12.1.15 and HttpsServer, JDK 17, 21 and 25. Scripts and results: tests/deep/. Appendix A lists them per language.
